## business-model — module synthesis

_Source: `99_business-model-synthesis.md`_

# Business Model Reality Check — TTWO (Synthesis)

**Regime:** US / SEC domestic filer (NasdaqGS:TTWO, Delaware), US GAAP, reporting currency USD (millions as the filings state them), fiscal year ends **March 31** — "FY2026" = year ended March 31, 2026; FY2027 is in progress [`FY26 10-K, cover page`; `00_data-triage.md §2A`]. All 13 upstream specialist outputs were present and read; none is missing. **Grand Theft Auto VI released today, November 19, 2026** [`Q2 FY27 10-Q, MD&A — Hardware Platforms`; `Q2 FY27 investor deck, Nov 9 2026, p.13`] — nothing below is written as a pending release, and no post-launch outcome is assumed.

---

## Abstract

Take-Two makes and publishes video games through its own studios — Grand Theft Auto and Red Dead under Rockstar, NBA 2K and Borderlands under 2K, and free mobile titles under Zynga. It runs as one reportable segment, inside which continuing in-game spending was 78.1% of FY26 revenue and mobile was the biggest platform at 50.1%. The strongest positive is a brand that just carried an $80 price for Grand Theft Auto VI against the industry's $70, on a series that has sold over 465 million units. The strongest negative is capital: return on capital averaged −0.7% across the five years to FY2026 against a roughly 8.2% estimated cost of capital. No automatic disqualifier triggered. This is a cyclical, hit-driven business that needs a timing edge, and today is the hinge.

---

## 1. First-Pass Verdict

### Automatic Disqualifier Check

Restated from `01_disqualifier-scan.md`. **All eight rows clear. No row is Y, so no verdict-lock applies.**

No going-concern language in either annual filing |
| 2 | >50% promoter / insider shares pledged | **N** | No promoter group exists (US issuer; largest tracked holder BlackRock ~8.7% of ~187.2m shares). The word "pledge" appears zero times in the FY26 10-K [`FY26 10-K, cover page`; `CIQ Public Ownership → History — vendor export`]. Ratio not computable; the insider ownership table sits in the absent DEF 14A — routed to management-governance as a gap, not a trigger |
| 3 | Related-party transactions >25% of revenue or expenses | **N** | No related-party note (ASC 850) in Notes 1–21 and no related-party line in audited revenue or expenses. Thresholds: $1,664.1m of revenue / $1,690.2m of expenses [`FY26 10-K, Consolidated Statements of Operations, p.63`]. The live arrangement (ZelnickMedia / ZMC Advisors) cost $79.5m combined in FY26 = **1.2% of revenue** [`FY26 10-K, Note 3; Note 16`] |
| 4 | Auditor changed twice in last 3 years without disclosed reason | **N** | Zero changes. Ernst & Young signed FY2024–FY2026; "We have served as the Company's auditor since 2006" [`FY26 10-K, Report of Independent Registered Public Accounting Firm, p.61`] |
| 5 | Material restatement (>5% of revenue or net income) in last 2 years | **N** | Both cover-page error-correction boxes unchecked; no restatement note; 0.0% of revenue and 0.0% of \|net loss\| [`FY26 10-K, cover page and Consolidated Statements of Operations, p.63`]. The vendor's "RS" tags on FY2017/18/21/22 columns are its own re-presentation, not a company restatement [`CIQ Financials_Annual → Cash Flow — vendor export`] |
| 6 | Active regulatory enforcement action on financial reporting | **N** | No action, investigation, subpoena or consent order anywhere in the pool. Contingencies are "routine litigation… not… material" [`FY26 10-K, Note 14, p.92`; repeated unchanged at `Q2 FY27 10-Q, Note 12, p.18`]. The only enforcement reference is a January 2025 FTC action against a **different** developer |
| 7 | >40% of revenue from single customer with no long-term contract | **N** | Largest single customer **24.9%** of FY2026 net revenue (22.8% FY2025, 23.2% FY2024) — below the 40% bar [`FY26 10-K, Note 1, p.68`]. Separate fact, not this trigger: the **five largest together were 80.6%** |
| 8 | Negative operating cash flow in 3 of last 4 years (excl. growth-stage) | **N — near-miss** | **2 of 4 negative**, not 3: FY2023 +$1.1m, FY2024 −$16.1m, FY2025 −$45.2m, FY2026 +$624.3m [`FY25 10-K, Consolidated Statements of Cash Flows, p.69`; `FY26 10-K, Consolidated Statements of Cash Flows, p.67`]. Growth-stage exclusion not invoked and not needed |

**Verdict-lock applied correctly: no lock.** Disqualifier #8 sits one year below its own threshold and is recorded as a near-miss, exactly as `01_disqualifier-scan.md §4` logged it (1 near-miss of 5 tested; no compounding signal, which requires ≥2). A near-miss does not lock the verdict — and it is not quietly upgraded to clean either: three consecutive years (FY2023–FY2025) in which operations threw off essentially no cash is carried into the negatives below and handed to balance-sheet-survival, where the same pool's vendor credit panel scores relative credit health "Bottom" [`CIQ Credit Health Panel → Summary — vendor export`, tier-5, not a filing].

### Verdict

- **Verdict: Cyclical business — worth deeper work only with a strong timing edge**
- **Disqualifier triggered: N**
- **Business clarity /100: 62** — what the company does and how it charges are unambiguous (four independent revenue cuts each summing to $6,656.4m), but where the money is actually *made* is undisclosed: one reportable segment with profit only at the consolidated level, two goodwill reporting units never named, no label-level split, and zero engagement metrics on the 50.1% of revenue that is mobile [`FY26 10-K, Note 21, p.102`; `FY26 10-K, MD&A, p.44`; `03_segment-map.md §3`; `02_business-identity.md §3a`]
- **Business quality /100: 38** *(from `07_business-quality.md`)* — Weak. Binding constraint is the band anchor off competitive intensity (25), not the §24 cap
- **Moat /100: 60** *(strongest-moat strength from `09_moat.md` — brand)*; module verdict is **"No moat proven"**, trajectory **eroding (not confirmed)**
- **External dependency risk /100 — INVERTED, HIGHER = WORSE: 52** *(from `10_external-dependency.md`)* — "Partly externally driven", material exposure with mixed mitigation
- **Capital allocation & governance /100: 50** *(from `11_capital-allocation-governance.md`, already capped — see caps below)*
- **Data quality /100: 78** — triage verdict **Sufficient**: audited FY2026 10-K (year to March 31, 2026, signed May 11, 2026) plus the Q2 FY27 10-Q, earnings release, deck and transcript all dated November 2026, across 30 sources and 62 workbook tabs with **zero extraction failures** [`00_data-triage.md §3`]. Marked down from the 80s, not into the 60s, for four real gaps named in Section 4: the empty DEF 14A export, the misfiled Alphabet key-developments export, zero engagement metrics, and no peer return-on-capital or market-share data anywhere
- **Overall usefulness /100: 64** *(capped at 70 — see caps below)*
- **Business type (one line):** Hit-driven owner-operator of entertainment intellectual property — a premium console/PC franchise publisher bolted onto a free-to-play mobile microtransaction business, selling almost entirely through four platform gatekeepers [`02_business-identity.md §3`]
- **Biggest business-model risk (one line):** Through a full release cycle the business has not earned its cost of capital — ROIC averaged **−0.7%** over FY2022–FY2026 against a ~8.2% estimated cost of capital (−890bps), and **+4.2%** even on the most generous ten-year reading (−400bps) — so the entire case rests on whether the Grand Theft Auto VI cycle that began today builds a second durable live-service annuity rather than one peak year [`09_moat.md §3`]

**REJECTOR-FILTER CAPS (CLAUDE.md §24).** This module owns three of the six filters. Each was checked:

- **Filter 1 — Crooks / integrity: NOT tripped, hard or soft.** `01_disqualifier-scan.md §2` found no allegation, enforcement action, investigation, restatement, material weakness or media claim touching the honesty of the controller or senior management — so there is no lock and no unverified "buzz" to route as a conviction cap. Two governance **data gaps** are routed to management-governance rather than scored here: whether any officer or director has pledged shares, and the full dollar picture of the ZelnickMedia / ZMC Advisors arrangement — both unreadable because the DEF 14A export is an empty shell. Neither is evidence of a problem; both are facts this pool cannot see.
- **Filter 4 — Serial acquirers: TRIPPED. Caps applied.** `11_capital-allocation-governance.md` scored the acquisition-pattern row **75** (≥70). Two material deals in four fiscal years — Zynga (May 2022, consideration build ≈$9,168m = 46.3m shares at $5,377.7m + 3.7m shares at $479.7m for assumed convertible notes + $3,310.9m of FY2023 cash acquisitions) and Gearbox (June 2024, $448.6m in stock) — against a pre-deal total capitalisation of **$4,059.9m** (roughly 2.3x), part-funded by **$3,248.9m** of FY2023 debt issuance, and followed by **$6,665.1m of acquisition-related write-downs** (built from $5,887.3m of accumulated goodwill impairment plus $777.8m of acquisition-related intangible impairments: $577.4m FY24, $137.0m + $39.3m FY25, $24.1m FY26). That $5,887.3m of accumulated impairment sits against **$6,949.2m of gross goodwill — 84.7% of all goodwill ever recorded** — leaving $1,061.9m net [`FY26 10-K, Note 9, pp.83–84`; `FY25 10-K, Consolidated Statements of Stockholders' Equity`; `CIQ Financials_Annual → Cash Flow and Historical Capitalization — vendor export`]. **Therefore: Capital allocation & governance capped at 50/100, and Overall usefulness capped at 70/100.** Qualifier carried intact: the FY26 10-K attributes the goodwill impairments to "one of our reporting units" **without naming it**; the FY25 10-K attributes the intangible impairments to "our Zynga acquisition" — so the Zynga attribution is explicit for the intangibles and strongly implied but not named for the goodwill.
- **Filter 5 — Fast-changing industry: TRIPPED. Cap applied, and it does not bind.** `07_business-quality.md` scored the industry rate-of-change / disruption row **35 (≤40)**, so Business quality is capped at 65 — the reported aggregate of **38** already sits well inside that cap, and the binding constraint is instead the band anchor off competitive intensity (25), which holds the aggregate at or below 45. **The thesis is flagged as a sector / technology-cycle bet rather than a durable compounder**, and `09_moat.md` applied the required durability discount throughout its report on that basis.

**RF-BQ-005 (fast-changing industry: rate-of-change ≤40)**

If multiple caps affect the same score, the most restrictive is used. On Capital allocation & governance, Filter 4's cap and the capital-structure-transaction cap both resolve to the same 50 (below).

**Module Disconfirmation (CLAUDE.md §8).**

- **Strongest bear point:** the company has not earned its cost of capital through a full cycle, and the proof is permanent rather than cyclical — **$5,887.3m of goodwill written off against $6,949.2m ever recorded (84.7%)** explicitly because of "industry conditions and changes in our strategies for games within the reporting unit", alongside five consecutive years of negative return on capital and three straight operating losses: $(104.2)m FY26, $(4,391.1)m FY25, $(3,590.6)m FY24 [`FY26 10-K, Note 9, pp.83–84`; `FY26 10-K, MD&A, p.43`].
- **Strongest bull point (the steelman):** the brand asset is real and was just exercised. Grand Theft Auto has sold-in **over 465 million units**, GTA V alone **over 225 million** (nearly 235 million by November 9, 2026), GTA Online still produces "record-setting engagement at times" thirteen years after launch, and the company took an **$80** base price against the industry's $70 — a 14% rise — while holding NBA 2K27 at $70 by choice, with pre-orders "tracking far in excess of our initial expectations" [`FY26 10-K, Item 1, pp.1–6`; `Q1 FY27 earnings call, Aug 7 2026, Q&A (Zelnick)`; `Q2 FY27 earnings call, Nov 9 2026, prepared remarks`]. On the cost side the business also recovered **56% as reported (~106% ex-stock-compensation)** of a 250bps input-cost rise in the latest reported half, without any escalator clause [`Q2 FY27 10-Q, Item 2 (MD&A)`].
- **Single killer risk:** the new Grand Theft Auto base fails to build a second durable live-service annuity, and the group reverts to the FY2024–FY2026 pattern of negative return on capital between launches. FY27 guidance already sets a thin bar for a peak year — GAAP net revenue $7,980–8,180m with GAAP **net income of $109–142m**, a net margin of roughly **1.3%–1.8%** [`Q2 FY27 investor deck, Nov 9 2026, p.7`].
- **Disconfirming evidence already visible:** three items, each cited and each pointing against the bear read. (1) FY26 revenue grew **+18.2%** ($1,022.8m, to $6,656.4m) — faster than the ten-peer median LTM growth of +14.6% — and FY27 net-bookings guidance of $8,000–8,200m against FY26's $6,721m implies **+19% to +22%** [`FY26 10-K, MD&A, p.44`; `Q2 FY27 investor deck, pp.5 and 8`]. (2) Cash conversion improved sharply, CFO ÷ EBITDA from 38.5% (FY2022) to **79.2%** (FY26) [`FY26 10-K, Consolidated Statements of Cash Flows`]. (3) Realised price rose on the flagship — the $80 GTA VI price against the $70 standard. `09_moat.md` adjudicated all three by name rather than averaging them away, and they are why its trajectory read is **"eroding — not confirmed"** rather than confirmed erosion.

**CAPITAL STRUCTURE TRANSACTION CAP — applied, and it resolves to the same number.** `11_capital-allocation-governance.md` reports capital-structure changes that clear the thresholds inside the window this module reviews, on both limbs. **Debt:** total debt went from $250.2m to $3,682.1m across FY2023 on $3,248.9m of issuance (far above the 50% YoY bar), then back down — $4,105.9m (FY2025) → $2,958.3m (FY2026), a 28% fall, with **$1,150m of notes repaid out of equity proceeds rather than operating cash** ($600.0m 2025 Notes on April 14, 2025; $550.0m 2026 Notes on March 28, 2026, "with proceeds from our May 2025 equity issuance") [`Q2 FY27 10-Q, Note on Debt`; `CIQ Financials_Annual → Historical Capitalization — vendor export`]. **Shares:** 115.8m at March 31, 2022 → **187.2m at November 19, 2026, +61.7%**, with the Zynga-era issuance alone 50.0m shares = 43% of the pre-deal count (above the 25% bar), zero buybacks in FY2024, FY2025 and FY2026, and treasury stock frozen at −$1,020.6m since FY2022 [`FY26 10-K, Item 5`; `CIQ Financials_Annual → Historical Capitalization and Balance Sheet — vendor export`]. **Capital allocation score capped at 50/100 due to the Zynga acquisition and its financing, which took total debt from $250.2m to $3,682.1m in one fiscal year and the share count up 43% in one transaction (61.7% to date), with $1,150m of subsequent debt repayment funded by a new equity issuance rather than operating cash.** Clean hygiene cannot override that: Ernst & Young's 20 unbroken years and unqualified opinions on both the accounts and internal control, no restatement, no off-balance-sheet vehicles, 0.0% intra-group share of disclosed trading relationships, and zero turnover in the CEO, CFO and Chair seats are all real and all recorded — and none of them lifts the score above 50. Note the cap is not additive with Filter 4: both bind at 50, and the most restrictive is used.

Only one quantified sensitivity exists: a 10% USD rise cuts revenue 4.0% (±$266m), stated gross of a cost offset the company declines to size |

## 3. Reconciliation

Nine disagreements or supersessions were found. In each case the more conservative or later-filed reading is adopted, per CLAUDE.md §4.

**1. Console licence expiry — the live contradiction, reconciled on the filing date, not averaged.**
`05_customer-geography.md §3` (added flag), `06_value-chain.md §5` and `07_business-quality.md §4` all state that **both** console licences — Sony's and both Microsoft agreements — expire **March 31, 2027**, covering the 39.0% of FY26 revenue earned on console. That was correct as of the FY26 10-K, signed May 11, 2026 [`FY26 10-K, Item 1 — Arrangements with Platform Manufacturers, pp.3–4`]. `12_red-flags-sweep.md` read the newer document and found it superseded on the Microsoft limb. **The later filing wins (§4). The corrected position, stated precisely:**

- **Microsoft limb — superseded.** A **new XBOX Publisher License Agreement, effective September 17, 2026**, between Take-Two and Microsoft Corporation was filed as Exhibit 10.1 to the Q2 FY27 10-Q (filed November 9, 2026). Its merger clause states the agreement "merges, replaces, and supersedes all related oral understandings, representations, prior discussions, letters of intent, or any preliminary or earlier agreements, **including the XBOX Console Publisher License Agreement between the parties**" [`Q2 FY27 10-Q, Exhibit 10.1, §18.10`; `Q2 FY27 10-Q, Exhibit Index, item 10.1`]. So **the March 31, 2027 expiry no longer describes the Microsoft limb.** What replaced it is **not readable**: the Initial Term `[***]`, each Renewal Term `[***]`, the non-renewal notice window `[***]` and the **Royalty Percentage** `[***]` are all redacted [`Q2 FY27 10-Q, Exhibit 10.1, §17.1 and Digital Content Royalty Terms §1.3`]. Redacting confidential commercial terms is normal and permitted; the consequence is still that the fee and the term on two-fifths of revenue are now invisible to an outsider where the expiry date previously was not. And one term IS readable and is sharper than an expiry date: Microsoft may terminate **"without cost or penalty" on a "Game-by-Game or country-by-country basis" on notice** if it determines at any time before commercial release that a game does not materially comply with the Publisher Guide or any Law [`Q2 FY27 10-Q, Exhibit 10.1, §17.2`].
- **Sony limb — intact and unchanged.** The PlayStation Global Developer and Publisher Agreement (effective March 23, 2017; PS5 Amendment September 30, 2020) still **expires March 31, 2027** with automatic one-year renewals, and **"Sony may terminate the agreement for any or no reason upon 30 days' notice"** [`FY26 10-K, Item 1 — Arrangements with Platform Manufacturers, p.3`].
- **The qualifier that travels with it:** console revenue was **39.0% of FY26 net revenue and 41.4% of the six months to September 30, 2026** [`FY26 10-K, MD&A, p.44`; `Q2 FY27 10-Q, MD&A, p.23`] — but that share is split across the two limbs and the filings do not disclose a Sony-only or Microsoft-only share, so neither limb's exposure can be sized on its own. **Downstream agents must not restate "both console licences expire March 31, 2027" as a current fact.** The accurate short form is: *the Sony limb carries a March 31, 2027 expiry plus a 30-day at-will termination right; the Microsoft limb was replaced on September 17, 2026 with its term and royalty redacted and a per-title, per-country cancellation right added.* Net direction is genuinely mixed — one expiry cliff removed, one piece of disclosure lost, one new counterparty right gained.

**2. Gross margin — vendor vs filing.** `ciq_facts.json → margin_trend` reads gross margin as 58% for both FY2025 and FY2026 on a reclassified basis; the FY26 10-K's own cost-of-revenue table reads **57.2% (FY26) and 54.3% (FY25)** [`FY26 10-K, MD&A, p.43`]. The ~290bp FY25 gap is a vendor reclassification, not a misread of either source. `06_value-chain.md` and `07_business-quality.md` both flagged it rather than overriding it silently. **Reconciled view: the filing's figures are used throughout this synthesis.**

**3. LTM EBIT — vendor vs filings-derived.** The vendor records LTM EBIT to September 30, 2026 at **$(112.5)m**; `09_moat.md` computed **$(180.8)m** from the filings (FY26 $(104.2)m − H1 FY26 $(76.4)m + H1 FY27 $(153.0)m) [`Q2 FY27 10-Q, p.2`]. About 100bps of margin apart. **Reconciled view: the filings-derived, more conservative $(180.8)m is used.**

**4. ROIC — vendor vs computed.** Vendor: −0.8% FY26 / −1.0% LTM. `09_moat.md` computed −1.3% FY26 / −2.2% LTM on a normalised 21.0% tax rate and gross invested capital. 40–120bps apart, same sign, same band, same verdict. **Reconciled view: the computed figures lead; the vendor series is retained for the ten-year sweep, labelled tier-5.** Note the normalised 21.0% rate is the conservative end — with EBIT negative, a higher assumed rate makes NOPAT less negative, so no plausible rate turns the return positive.

**5. Net debt basis.** Three figures circulate for the same date (September 30, 2026): **strict** net debt (total debt − cash) **$1,370.4m**; **broad** basis also netting short-term investments **$846.2m**; and the vendor's **$1,237.8m** on a CIQ basis that includes ~$0.4bn of lease liabilities [`Q2 FY27 10-Q, Condensed Consolidated Balance Sheets`; `ciq_facts.json → net_debt_m, total_debt_m`]. **Reconciled view: the strict basis ($1,370.4m) leads, every non-strict figure is labelled inline with its basis, and no investment-inclusive number is presented as bare "net cash" or "net debt" (§15).**

**6. Competitive position vs the growth numbers.** `08_competitive-map.md` reads "losing share" off LTM revenue +3.1% against a ten-peer median +14.6%; the same engine's data shows FY26 revenue +18.2% and FY27 guidance implying +19–22%. **Reconciled view: competitive-map's own adjudication is adopted verbatim — "Losing share on the latest twelve-month window; the read is a one-metric proxy inside a stated launch trough, is not confirmed, and is not durable evidence of competitive decline."** There is **no market-share figure of any kind** in this pool, and revenue growth is a share proxy only if the market itself grew, which this pool does not measure. Peer LTM windows also end on different dates (March 31, 2026 for Ubisoft through November 2026).

**7. Capital intensity — framing, not fact.** `03_segment-map.md` labels capital intensity "**High** — but in content, not in plant"; `07_business-quality.md` scores it **42 (mixed)** on a low-intensity-earns-a-high-score basis. These are the same read in two directions, not a disagreement: fixed-asset capex was only $162.8m (2.45% of FY26 revenue), while cash into content plus fixed assets was $851.7m = **12.8% of revenue**, and $2,149.7m is sunk into titles nobody has bought yet [`FY26 10-K, Consolidated Statements of Cash Flows`; `FY26 10-K, Note 7, p.83`]. **Reconciled view: asset-light in plant, capital-heavy in content, with the content capital committed years before any revenue.**

**8. Customer concentration vs receivable concentration.** Top five customers were **80.6% of FY26 net revenue** [`FY26 10-K, Note 1, p.68`]; five customers were **69.6% of gross accounts receivable** at the same date (72.1% a year earlier) [same note]. Not a contradiction — two different measures (a full-year flow versus a point-in-time balance). **Reconciled view: both are carried, each labelled with its own basis, and neither is used as a proxy for the other.**

**9. One upstream bookkeeping error, corrected.** `12_red-flags-sweep.md` states that "`09_moat.md` does **not** exist in the output folder". It does exist and was read in full for this synthesis. Red-flags-sweep's own caveat — "Nothing in Section 2 depends on moat reasoning" — holds, so no flag is affected; the note is recorded here so no downstream reader treats the moat module as missing. **This module declares no missing orbs.**

*On language: all 73 extracts in this pool are English and the issuer is a US domestic filer, so CLAUDE.md §27's translation rule has nothing to correct here. No upstream module logged a language barrier, and none is inherited.*

---

## 4. Note To The Final Synthesizer

- **Strongest business-model positive — the brand is real and was just exercised, on a minority of revenue.** Grand Theft Auto has sold-in **over 465 million units**; the series carried an **$80** base price for GTA VI against the industry's $70 (+14%) while NBA 2K27 was held at $70 by choice; GTA Online still produces "record-setting engagement at times" thirteen years after launch [`FY26 10-K, Item 1, pp.1–6`; `Q1 FY27 earnings call, Aug 7 2026, Q&A (Zelnick)`]. The limit travels with it: **GTA products were only 12.4% of FY26 net revenue** and the top five franchises 54.3%, the CEO states "Our goal is not to maximize price", and the company cuts wholesale price by design "typically… three to nine months after a product's initial launch" [`FY26 10-K, Item 1A, p.10`; `FY26 10-K, Item 1 — Marketing, p.5`].
- **Strongest business-model negative — through a full cycle the business has not earned its cost of capital, and the destruction is audited rather than modelled.** Through-cycle ROIC **−0.7%** (FY2022–FY2026) against a ~8.2% estimated cost of capital; **+4.2%** on the most generous ten-year reading, still −400bps; robust even at a 4.74% floor [`09_moat.md §3`]. The audited corroboration: **$5,887.3m of accumulated goodwill impairment against $6,949.2m of gross goodwill — 84.7% of all goodwill ever recorded** — plus $777.8m of acquisition-related intangible impairments, for **$6,665.1m of total acquisition-related write-downs**, taken because of "industry conditions and changes in our strategies for games within the reporting unit" [`FY26 10-K, Note 9, pp.83–84`].
- **Most important segment — there is only one, and that is itself the finding.** "Sale of software titles", 100% of FY26 revenue and 100% of the result; the segment measure of profit **is** consolidated net income (loss) and segment assets **are** consolidated total assets, so the segment note adds nothing beyond the face of the accounts [`FY26 10-K, Note 21, p.102`]. What matters inside it: continuing in-game spending (78.1% of FY26 revenue, 82.5% of H1 FY27) is the larger half, and mobile (50.1%) is the biggest platform **and** the explicitly lower-margin one. The centre of gravity is moving from mobile in-game spending toward console full-game sales — FY27 guidance implies in-game spending falling to roughly 65% of net bookings from 78% — and no segment disclosure lets an outsider measure that margin-mix shift.
- **Cleanest unit-economics read — there isn't one, and the reason is disclosure, not performance.** The natural unit is one released game title; the company capitalises and amortises exactly that way ("recorded on a **title-by-title basis** in cost of revenue"), so the title-level P&L exists inside the company and is simply not published. **Per-title revenue, per-title cost and payback period cannot be derived from this pool — that is "not proven from available data", NOT a measured negative unit.** What can be tested is the sum: content spend of $5,011.9m across FY24–FY26 against gross profit less selling-and-marketing of $4,109.0m, a **$902.9m shortfall** — and that comparison is conservative-biased against the company by an unmeasurable amount, because **$2,149.7m of the $2,346.3m capitalised content balance (91.6%) sits in titles not yet released.** The single most valuable missing disclosure in the whole module is a title-level vintage table: cumulative net revenue to date against capitalised development cost, per released title.
- **Where the company sits vs named peers — bottom of the set on margin; on capital efficiency the comparison cannot be made at all.** Gross margin **57.7%** against a ten-peer median **66.9%** (−920bps); EBIT margin **−1.8% (vendor) / −2.8% (filings-derived)** against the median **+15.9%** — fourth-lowest of eleven, behind both direct structural rivals, Electronic Arts (+17.9%) and Playtika (+18.9%) [`CIQ Comps → Operating Statistics`, As-Of 2026-11-19 — vendor export]. **No peer ROIC, ROCE or ROE column exists anywhere in this pool**, so "Insufficient data to compare against named peers" applies to the return-on-capital comparison specifically, in either direction — and no such claim may be made downstream. Two qualifiers travel with the margin read: peer LTM windows end on different dates, and every peer figure is the vendor's normalised read, not that peer's audited accounts. Electronic Arts was taken private on August 4, 2026 (~$55bn, ~$20bn of debt), so the closest rival's future results will not be publicly observable — and its 13.8% net margin was measured **before** that debt landed.
- **Main external dependency — four gatekeepers who are also four competitors, and nothing about it can be hedged.** Apple, Sony, Google and Microsoft each took more than 10.0% of FY26 net revenue, inside a top five at 80.6%; **91.0% of mobile revenue** ran on Apple and Google, where mobile is 50.1% of the group — roughly **46% of group revenue** through two app stores. Sales are made "primarily without long-term agreements", platform providers have "broad discretion to change and interpret… terms of service" and to change the fee structure, and the company's own risk heading reads "We cannot publish our titles without the approval of hardware licensors that are also our competitors" [`FY26 10-K, Item 1 — Sales, p.4`; `Item 1A, pp.11, 23`]. The one partial offset — a direct-to-consumer channel the CEO says had "a material effect positive on our margins in the Mobile business" — is **not sized anywhere in the pool**, so it cannot be credited with a number.
- **Most important capital allocation or governance signal — the acquisition pattern, and the fact that the deal-making has stopped.** $6,665.1m of write-downs on roughly $9.6bn of deals (Zynga ≈$9,168m + Gearbox $448.6m), funded part in debt ($3,248.9m issued in FY2023, taking total debt from $250.2m to $3,682.1m) and part in stock (50.0m shares = 43% of the pre-deal count), against a pre-deal total capitalisation of $4,059.9m. Opportunity cost is charged, not ignored: the Private Division label was sold in October 2024 for $32.7m with an $11.4m loss on divestiture, studios were shut, and several rounds of layoffs ran [`FY25 10-K, Item 1 and Item 1A`; `FY26 10-K, Note 9`]. **What keeps it off a worse score: business acquisitions were $(2.6)m in FY2026 and nil in H1 FY27**, with no new material transaction disclosed. The quieter route to the same problem is the **$2,494.9m capitalised software-development asset at September 30, 2026**, impaired in each of the last three fiscal years and re-tested quarterly.
- **Whether any automatic disqualifier triggered: NO.** All eight rows clear. One near-miss (operating cash flow negative in 2 of 4 years against a 3-of-4 bar) is carried forward, not upgraded to clean and not treated as a lock. §24 Filter 1 (crooks / integrity) did not trip in either form — no hard evidence and no unverified adverse signal.
- **Which rejector filters tripped, and the cap each applied:**
  - **Filter 1 (crooks / integrity): not tripped.** No lock, no conviction cap. Two proxy-dependent governance facts routed to management-governance as gaps: insider share pledging and the full ZMC Advisors compensation picture.
  - **Filter 4 (serial acquirers): TRIPPED** on an acquisition-pattern severity of 75. **Capital allocation & governance capped at 50/100; Overall usefulness capped at 70/100.** The capital-structure-transaction cap resolves to the same 50 and is applied alongside it.
  - **Filter 5 (fast-changing industry): TRIPPED** on an industry rate-of-change score of 35. **Business quality capped at 65** — non-binding, since the reported 38 is already well inside it. **The thesis must be flagged as a sector / technology-cycle bet rather than a durable compounder**, and moat durability was discounted on that basis.
- **Forensic tag propagation (CLAUDE.md §13; eval checks AD/AE/AQ):**
  - **RF-BQ-005 (fast-changing industry: rate-of-change ≤40)** — **FIRED** by `07_business-quality.md` and propagated here as a standalone line in Section 1.
  - **RF-DISQ-001 (multiple sub-threshold disqualifier near-misses)** — **NOT fired.** `01_disqualifier-scan.md §4` records 1 near-miss of 5 tested and states "Compounding signal: None — fewer than 2 near-misses." Recorded explicitly so the cross-module roll-up knows it was checked rather than dropped.
  - **RF-RFS-001 (aggressive accounting practice pattern)** — **NOT emitted as a tag** by `12_red-flags-sweep.md`, but its substance IS present and must reach the cross-module forensic roll-up (synthesizer.md Pre-Write Gate step 4B) regardless. Its "Pattern one" finds that every discretionary reporting choice visible in the last two years lands in the same direction, into the peak year, on a metric that pays management: a **negative $(27.9)m** stock-comp charge in FY26 cost of revenue off $49.1m of forfeitures, worth about **19% of the +290bp FY26 gross-margin improvement**; **$98.0m** of stock comp capitalised into a development asset impaired three years running; **90.3% of a $91.8m London property purchase allocated to non-depreciating land** ($82.9m land, useful life "N/A", versus $8.9m of building over 20 years); title-amortisation and player-life windows both shortened; and a **$215.0m receivables-purchase facility** standing unused whose proceeds, by the company's own stated accounting, "will be reflected as cash provided by operating activities" if drawn — while management's performance RSUs vest on **recurrent consumer spending net bookings**, a company-defined metric, and FY27 guidance is given in that same non-GAAP currency. Every item is disclosed, none is a restatement, Ernst & Young gave unqualified opinions on both the FY2026 accounts and internal control, and no single item moves the verdict. It is a verification task for the accounting-forensics layer, not a finding of wrongdoing — and it is exactly the kind of mosaic no single module's own cap would catch.
- **Every red-flags-sweep flag at severity ≥40 is surfaced (mandatory propagation), with its severity:**
  - **55 — the tax line.** A **$1,262.3m valuation allowance against $1,454.8m of gross deferred tax assets (86.8%)**, whose stated basis is the company's own record: "Due to our **cumulative loss position, which provides significant negative evidence**, we recognized a tax expense of **$135.3m** from an increase in our valuation allowance" [`FY26 10-K, Note 17`]. Management has formally stated it does not expect to earn enough taxable profit to use 86.8% of its stored deductions — independent corroboration of the negative-return read. FY26 printed an effective rate of **(50.8)%** — a **$100.4m tax charge on a pre-tax loss**. And the newest fact in the pool makes the rate unforecastable rather than merely volatile: "In **November 2026**, we completed a reorganization involving certain intangible assets… our jurisdictional mix of earnings will be affected beginning in November 2026. We are **still evaluating the impact** of this on our estimated annual effective tax rate, deferred tax assets, and valuation allowance for future periods" [`Q2 FY27 10-Q, Note 16 — Subsequent Events, p.20`]. **Two consequences the final synthesizer must not miss:** a release of that allowance after a successful cycle would drop a very large **non-cash** credit into FY27–FY28 net income and EPS, and reading it as operating performance would be a category error; and **$307.7m of loss carryforwards begin expiring inside the 2027–2033 window** regardless ($34.9m 2027–2029, $272.8m 2027–2033).
  - **48 — the replacement Microsoft console licence.** Reconciled in full in Section 3, item 1. One expiry cliff removed; the term and the **Royalty Percentage** now redacted on a channel that is 39.0%–41.4% of revenue; a per-game, per-country termination right "without cost or penalty" added.
  - **45 — stock compensation at $403.3m, 6.1% of revenue, against a reported operating loss of $(104.2)m.** $305.3m through the P&L plus **$98.0m capitalised** into the game-development asset — and the capitalised slice sits inside the $2,346.3m balance of which $2,149.7m is unreleased titles, an asset impaired in each of the last three years ($77.5m FY25, $109.9m FY24) [`FY26 10-K, Note 16; Note 7, p.83`].
  - **40 — the pre-release assets of the flagship franchise have already been stolen once.** In September 2022 "an unauthorized third party illegally accessed and downloaded confidential information from Rockstar Games' systems, **including early development footage for the next Grand Theft Auto**", and separately took credentials for a 2K Games help-desk vendor platform. Direct costs were "immaterial" and that is fair on cash; the flag is that the loss event is **proven, not hypothetical**, in a business whose entire unbuilt inventory is source code and game assets, with $2,149.7m of capitalised cost sitting in unreleased titles and the company writing that such disruptions "**have** negatively affected" its operations [`FY26 10-K, Item 1A, pp.26–27`]. Governance side for balance: a dedicated cybersecurity team under the CTO, NIST and ISO frameworks, independent penetration testing, and at least annual Audit Committee reporting [`FY26 10-K, Item 1C, pp.35–36`].
  - Below the mandatory threshold and summarised rather than omitted: **38 — receivable concentration** (five customers = 69.6% of gross receivables, three individually 22.7% / 21.0% / 14.0%; "We do not require collateral from our customers"; trade credit insurance covers only customers who "sell our physical products", and physical channels are 3.0% of revenue — so effectively the digital 97.0% of the receivable book is unsecured and uninsured, and the launch quarter that began today will push that balance up sharply against the same three or four counterparties); **35 — the $91.8m London freehold** (90.3% allocated to non-depreciating land, capital committed to property while return on capital is negative); **30 — unionisation risk** flagged by the company itself on a workforce 77% of which is developers (9,998 of 12,909), with no current organising activity disclosed at TTWO. Two candidate flags were tested and **not** raised: pension/post-employment obligations (defined-contribution only, $55.1m FY26 cost, no defined-benefit cost in any of ten annual columns) and litigation overhang (routine only; recorded as "not proven from available data" rather than a clean bill, because the DEF 14A export is empty and the pool holds no TTWO material-events timeline).
- **Biggest missing data point — and it is a tie between two, both of which cap conviction rather than merely annoy.** First, **zero engagement metrics**: no MAU, DAU, paying-user count, ARPDAU or retention figure appears anywhere in the pool, in a business where mobile is 50.1% of revenue and continuing in-game spending is 78.1% of it. The volume half of `players × spend per player` cannot be read at all, so **the durability of the 78.1% recurrent-spending base is asserted by the company and unverifiable by an outsider.** Second, the **platform fee rate** Apple, Google, Sony and Microsoft charge — the single most important competitive number in this business — is nowhere in the pool as a rate, and the newest Microsoft agreement redacts it. Behind those: no peer return-on-capital, no market-share or industry-size data, no direct-to-consumer share, no title-level vintage economics, and no identity or size for either of the two goodwill reporting units. **Declared pool defects, surfaced honestly:** the DEF 14A export (July 27, 2026) extracted cleanly but holds one cell reading "This filing has no tabular data to export", so proxy compensation and governance content is **absent** and every proxy-dependent checklist item is "Not proven from available data" — not clean; and `Alphabet-Inc-NasdaqGS-GOOGL-Key-Developments__Key-Developments.txt` is a **misfiled export for a different issuer (Alphabet Inc.)**, never cited as TTWO evidence anywhere in this module, with the side effect that **TTWO's own key-developments / material-events timeline is missing** from the pool. Both are pool defects, not disclosure failures by the company, and neither is a language issue.
- **Does the business deserve deeper work, and what would change the answer? Yes — but only as a cycle-timing question, not as a compounder question.** It deserves deeper work because the brand asset is genuine and cited, the balance sheet is no longer at its 2022 stress point (total debt down from a $4,122.3m peak to $2,519.8m, leverage from 8.77x to 3.64x), and the decisive uncertainty has a **dated resolution point**: the Q3 FY27 result — the first full quarter with Grand Theft Auto VI on sale — covering the quarter ending December 31, 2026 and flagged for on or about **February 9, 2027** [`CIQ Estimates → Consensus`; `CIQ Events Calendar — vendor export`]. **What would move the answer toward "low-quality business — avoid deeper work":** the launch quarter landing materially short of the guided FY27 net bookings of $8,000–8,200m while the implied second-half in-game-spending share keeps sliding toward ~65%, which would move the erosion read from unconfirmed to confirmed; a material impairment of the $2,494.9m capitalised software-development asset; one further large, debt-funded acquisition, which `11_capital-allocation-governance.md` says would move its read from "capital allocation concerns" to "governance red flags"; loss or material repricing of the Sony licence (expiry March 31, 2027, terminable on 30 days' notice) or an adverse use of Microsoft's new per-title cancellation right; or loot-box rulemaking landing enforceably against the 78.1% of revenue that is in-game monetisation. **What would move it toward "high-quality business":** a full release cycle — not one peak year — on which group return on capital clears the ~8.2% cost of capital, which on FY27 guidance-level delivery would be roughly +19% ROIC. **The symmetric trap to flag loudly:** today's reported returns are a cyclical **trough**, not a peak (LTM EBITDA $724.9m is *below* FY26's $788m, and the vendor's TEV/LTM EBITDA of 52.2x was struck on that trough denominator the day before the launch) — and FY27–FY28 will print this cycle's **peak**. Neither end is the through-cycle level. FY27 guidance itself is the first honest marker: **GAAP net income of $109–142m, a net margin of roughly 1.3%–1.8%**, in the year carrying the biggest release the company has ever had — thin for a cycle peak, and still guidance rather than a result.

---

## 5. Simple Summary

- **What it does:** writes and publishes video games in its own studios — Grand Theft Auto and Red Dead (Rockstar), NBA 2K and Borderlands (2K), free mobile games like Toon Blast and Color Block Jam (Zynga). 9,998 of its 12,909 staff are developers.
- **How it makes money:** mostly not from the first sale. **78.1% of FY26 revenue ($5,196.6m) was continuing in-game spending** — virtual currency, add-on content, in-game purchases and advertising bought after the game is already owned. 97.0% of revenue arrives digitally, and **80.6% of it is paid by just five customers**: Apple, Sony, Google, Microsoft and one other, each of the first four above 10% on their own.
- **Does each new unit create value? Cannot be answered from what the company publishes** — no per-title revenue, no per-title cost, no payback period, even though the company computes amortisation title by title internally. What can be checked is the total, and over FY24–FY26 content spending exceeded gross profit after marketing by **$902.9m** — a comparison biased against the company, because **$2,149.7m of capitalised content sits in titles nobody has bought yet**.
- **Which segment matters most:** there is only one, by the company's own audited note. Inside it, continuing in-game spending is the bigger half (78.1%) and mobile is the biggest platform (50.1%) — and mobile is the one management says carries the **lower** margin, by an amount it never discloses.
- **Does it have a moat, and against whom? No moat proven.** The brand is real — Grand Theft Auto has sold over 465 million units and just carried an $80 price against the industry's $70 — but it covers only 12.4% of revenue and does not lift returns: **through-cycle return on capital of −0.7% against a cost of capital of roughly 8.2%.** It is up against Electronic Arts (now private, levered), Roblox (growing ten times faster), Playtika, Nintendo, Konami, NEXON, Ubisoft and others — **and against three of its own biggest customers**, since Sony, Microsoft and Apple/Google all publish or distribute competing games.
- **What external variables it depends on:** four gatekeepers above all — Apple, Google, Sony and Microsoft — who set the fee, write the terms, and in Sony's case can walk away on 30 days' notice. Then regulation of in-game spending in six jurisdictions aimed at 78.1% of revenue, the third-party console cycle under 39–41% of revenue, discretionary consumer spending, and currency (a 10% stronger dollar cuts revenue 4.0%). No commodity exposure at all.
- **Is capital allocated well? No — and the evidence is audited, not inferred.** Roughly $9.6bn of acquisitions have produced **$6,665.1m of write-downs**, including $5,887.3m of goodwill impairment against the $6,949.2m ever recorded (84.7%). Share count is up 61.7% since March 2022 with zero buybacks in three years, $1,150m of debt was repaid out of a new equity issue rather than operating cash, and insiders made 0 open-market buys against 16 sells in the last twelve months. The one mitigating fact: the deal-making has stopped.
- **Does it deserve deeper work? Yes — as a cycle-timing call, not as a compounder.** No disqualifier triggered, the brand is genuine, and the decisive test is dated: Grand Theft Auto VI went on sale **today, November 19, 2026**, and the first full quarter with it on sale reports on or about **February 9, 2027** against FY27 guidance of $8,000–8,200m of net bookings and **$109–142m of net income**. Treat today's weak numbers as a trough and next year's as a peak — neither is the real level.

## business-model / 00_data-triage.md

_Source: `00_data-triage.md`_

# Data Triage — TTWO

**Evidence binding (frozen):** `NOSTRA_FROZEN_EVIDENCE_ROOT` was present with the complete quartet, so `DATA_PATH` is the frozen evidence root and `data/TTWO/` is a citation label only. No extractor was run, no live `data/TTWO/` read, no rebuild. Every read below came from the bound generation `.extract-generations/0af6384…3203d` (`manifest.json`, `ciq_facts.json`, `relationships.json`, and the per-tab extracts inside it).

**Manifest totals:** 30 source files · 19 workbooks · 62 tabs · 73 extracts written · **0 failures**. No source carries `fail`, `fallback-text`, `missing-dependency`, or `gdrive-pointer` status, so nothing in the pool is in a failure state and no extraction-failure cap applies.

**On the "Last Modified" column (fix F23):** every file in the frozen copy carries the same filesystem timestamp (2026-11-19 18:43), which is the freeze time, not a document date. That column is therefore recorded as `freeze` and is analytically useless. The **Period Covered** column is parsed from INSIDE each document (period-end line, "as-of" line, fiscal-year header, first/last data row) and is the only date basis used for the verdict.

**No external research:** there is no `external/` subfolder in the pool and no manifest row carries `external: true` or a `provenance` object. There is nothing to list in a `1A. External Data` section, and no external document influenced the verdict.

## 2A. Filing Regime

| Item | Detected Value | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| Primary listing country | United States — NASDAQ Global Select Market, ticker TTWO; incorporated in Delaware | `FY26 10-K, cover page (Delaware, I.R.S. No. 51-0350842, Commission file number 001-34003)`; `Capital IQ Public Company Profile — "Ticker: TTWO (NasdaqGS)"` |
| Filing regime | **US SEC** — domestic filer: Form 10-K annual, Form 10-Q quarterly, 8-K material events, DEF 14A proxy | `FY26 10-K, cover ("Annual Report Pursuant to Section 13 or 15(d) of the Securities Exchange Act of 1934")`; `Q2 FY27 10-Q, cover ("For the quarterly period ended September 30, 2026")` |
| Reporting standard | **US GAAP** | `FY26 10-K, Report of Independent Registered Public Accounting Firm — "for each of the three years in the period ended March 31, 2026, in conformity with U.S. generally accepted accounting principles"`; `FY26 10-K, Note 1 (Basis of Presentation) — "in conformity with U.S. GAAP"` |
| Reporting currency + fiscal-year end | **USD**; fiscal year ends **March 31** (FY2026 = year ended March 31, 2026; FY2027 in progress, ends March 31, 2027) | `FY26 10-K, cover ("fiscal year ended March 31, 2026")`; `CIQ Financials_Annual → Income Statement header ("In Millions of the reported currency", USD)`; `CIQ Estimates → Consensus ("Current Fiscal Year End: Mar-31-2027")` |
| Document language(s) | **English only** — all 73 extracts are English | Manifest: all 30 sources `status: ok`, no non-English or OCR-fallback rows |

Downstream agents should apply CLAUDE.md §27 as the US/SEC branch of the map: read and cite 10-K / 10-Q / 8-K earnings releases / DEF 14A. The fiscal year ending March 31 is the trap to watch — "FY2026" here means the year to **March 31, 2026**, and FY2027 interim periods are standalone quarters (US 10-Q basis), not cumulative, so a vendor's standalone-quarter estimate is on the same basis the company actually reports.

---

## 3. Sufficiency Verdict

- **Verdict:** Sufficient
- **Reason:** The pool holds an audited annual filing from within the last 18 months (FY2026 10-K, year ended March 31, 2026, signed May 11, 2026 — 7.6 months old by period end) plus three independent sources from within the last 6 months covering the same latest quarter (Q2 FY2027 10-Q for the quarter ended September 30, 2026; the November 9, 2026 earnings release and investor deck; and the November 9, 2026 earnings-call transcript), with zero extraction failures across 30 sources and 62 workbook tabs.
- **Non-blocking gaps (recorded, do not change the verdict):**
  - The DEF 14A export (`Take-Two_Interactive_Software_Inc_-_Form_DEF_14A(Jul-27-2026).xls`) extracted cleanly but contains one cell saying the filing has no tabular data. The proxy's actual content is absent from the pool. This bites management-governance (pay, board votes), not the business-model module, which has the Board Members, Professionals, and Auditors exports plus the 10-K's own governance sections.
  - `Alphabet Inc NasdaqGS GOOGL Key Developments.xls` is an export for a different issuer (Alphabet Inc., confirmed in the manifest `entities` block). It is off-subject and must not be cited as TTWO evidence; as a side effect, TTWO's own Key Developments / material-events timeline is not in the pool. Downstream agents needing material events should use the 10-K/10-Q and the two earnings releases instead.
  - Five vendor tabs are chart shells or disclaimers with almost no data (`Credit Health Panel` → Operational / Solvency / Liquidity Metrics Charts; two `Disclaimer` tabs), and four more are near-empty (`Industry Specific` and `Pension OPEB` in both the annual and quarterly financials workbooks, 20–65 populated cells each). They are listed for completeness and carry no analytical weight.
  - The Suppliers and Customers exports cover only the vendor's "recently disclosed … within the last two years" window for current subsidiaries. They prove a relationship exists, never its size, and 3 of the 28 named entities are flagged `likely_group` — treat those as related parties until a filing says otherwise.

## business-model / 01_disqualifier-scan.md

_Source: `01_disqualifier-scan.md`_

# Disqualifier Scan — TTWO

**Filing regime (from triage `00`):** US / SEC domestic filer — Form 10-K annual, Form 10-Q quarterly, 8-K earnings releases, DEF 14A proxy. Reporting standard **US GAAP**, currency **USD**, fiscal year ends **March 31** (so "FY2026" = year ended March 31, 2026). All documents in English. Figures in USD millions unless stated.

**Pool limits that bear on this agent (recorded, not speculated past):** the `Take-Two_Interactive_Software_Inc_-_Form_DEF_14A(Jul-27-2026).xls` export extracted cleanly but holds one cell reading "This filing has no tabular data to export," so the proxy's own content — the beneficial-ownership table (where pledged insider shares would appear) and Item 13's dollar amounts for related-party deals — is absent from this pool. `Alphabet Inc NasdaqGS GOOGL Key Developments.xls` is a misfiled export for a different issuer and was not read as TTWO evidence; as a side effect TTWO's own material-events timeline is absent, so material events were taken from the 10-K, the two 10-Qs and the two earnings releases instead.

---

## 1. Disqualifier Check

| # | Disqualifier | Triggered (Y/N) | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Auditor qualification or going-concern note (last 3 years) | **N** | Unqualified opinion covering FY2024, FY2025 and FY2026 in one report: "the consolidated financial statements present fairly, in all material respects… for each of the three years in the period ended March 31, 2026, in conformity with U.S. generally accepted accounting principles" — and a separate unqualified opinion on internal control over financial reporting (ICFR, the company's own checks on how it produces its numbers) as of March 31, 2026 [FY26 10-K, Report of Independent Registered Public Accounting Firm, p.60–61]. One Critical Audit Matter (goodwill impairment assessment on a $1,061.9m goodwill balance) is disclosed, and the report states it "does not alter in any way our opinion" [same, p.60]. No going-concern or substantial-doubt language appears in either annual filing; the only "going concern" string in the pool is a credit-agreement covenant *requirement* that future audits carry no such qualification [FY25 10-K, 2022 Credit Agreement exhibit text]. Vendor cross-check agrees: FY2026, FY2025, FY2024 all "Unqualified" [CIQ Auditors export, time frame 1996–2026 — vendor export] |
| 2 | >50% promoter / insider shares pledged | **N** | **Not disclosed in available data — and no promoter group exists.** TTWO is a Delaware-incorporated US issuer with no promoter/controlling block: 185,666,663 shares outstanding net of treasury at May 11, 2026 [FY26 10-K, cover page], largest tracked holder BlackRock, Inc. 16,249,491 shares as of 2026-09-30 [CIQ Public Ownership → History — vendor export], i.e. ~8.7% of the ~187.2m current share count [CIQ Comps → Financial Data, as-of 2026-11-19 — vendor export]. The word "pledge" appears **zero** times in the FY26 10-K. The insider beneficial-ownership table that would disclose pledging sits in the DEF 14A, which is an empty export in this pool. Ratio not computable; no evidence of any pledge, so no trigger. Routed to management-governance as a data gap |
| 3 | Related-party transactions >25% of revenue or expenses | **N** | The FY26 10-K's audited notes run 1–21 and contain **no related-party transactions note** (ASC 850); a related-party transaction material to the financial statements would require one. Item 13 "Certain Relationships and Related Transactions" is incorporated by reference to the 2026 proxy [FY26 10-K, Item 13, p.53], which is the empty export. One standing related-party arrangement is visible: the Management Agreement dated May 3, 2022 between the company and ZelnickMedia Corporation (the firm of Executive Chairman & CEO Strauss Zelnick), plus Restricted Unit Agreements with ZMC Advisors, L.P. [FY26 10-K, Exhibit Index, Ex. 10.26–10.29]. Its dollar amount is not quantified anywhere in this pool. Thresholds for reference: RP **sales** would have to exceed **$1,664.1m** (25% of total net revenue $6,656.4m) and RP **purchases/expenses** would have to exceed **$1,690.2m** (25% of total expenses $6,760.6m = cost of revenue $2,846.7m + total operating expenses $3,913.9m) [FY26 10-K, Consolidated Statements of Operations, p.63]. No related-party line of any size appears in the audited revenue or expense captions. Side evidence: the vendor supply-chain graph shows 0 intra-group relationship rows (0.0% intragroup row share) across 28 disclosed rows [CIQ Suppliers/Customers export (TTWO), retrieved 2026-11-19 — vendor export; scope_notes: "Recently disclosed customers only (within the last two years)" / "Include Customers for: Current subsidiaries"] |
| 4 | Auditor changed twice in last 3 years without disclosed reason | **N** | Zero changes. Ernst & Young LLP signed FY2024, FY2025 and FY2026, and the FY26 report states "We have served as the Company's auditor since 2006" [FY26 10-K, Report of Independent Registered Public Accounting Firm, p.61]. Vendor history shows EY continuously from FY2006 to FY2026 [CIQ Auditors export — vendor export] |
| 5 | Material restatement (>5% of revenue or net income) in last 2 years | **N** | The FY26 10-K cover-page check boxes are both **unchecked** for (a) "financial statements… reflect the correction of an error to previously issued financial statements" and (b) whether any such error correction is a restatement requiring clawback analysis [FY26 10-K, cover page]. No restatement note appears in Notes 1–21, and the Q1 and Q2 FY27 10-Qs disclose no restatement and no material weakness in internal control. Computed: restatement amount $0 → **0.0% of revenue** ($0 ÷ $6,656.4m) and **0.0% of |net loss|** ($0 ÷ $298.2m), both below the 5% threshold; the revenue-based test leads here because net income is a small loss and would make the ratio unstable [FY26 10-K, Consolidated Statements of Operations, p.63]. Note against a misread: the CIQ cash-flow tab marks FY2017, FY2018, FY2021 and FY2022 columns "Restatement Type: RS" — that is the **vendor's own re-presentation** of its standard template, not a company restatement, and the FY2023–FY2026 columns carry "NC"/"O" [CIQ Financials_Annual → Cash Flow — vendor export] |
| 6 | Active regulatory enforcement action on financial reporting | **N** | No enforcement action, investigation, subpoena, Wells notice or consent order against TTWO appears anywhere in the pool. The audited contingencies note says the company is "involved in routine litigation in the ordinary course of business which we do not believe to be material to our business or financial condition or results of operations" [FY26 10-K, Note 14 — Commitments and Contingencies, p.92], and Item 3 Legal Proceedings simply points to that note [FY26 10-K, Item 3, p.37]. The latest interim filing repeats the same language with no change [Q2 FY27 10-Q, Note 12 — Commitments and Contingencies, p.18]. The only enforcement reference in the risk factors is the January 2025 **FTC action against a different game developer**, cited as an industry example of loot-box/children's-privacy regulation, not an action against TTWO [FY26 10-K, Risk Factors — regulation of game mechanics]. Nothing touching financial reporting |
| 7 | >40% of revenue from single customer with no long-term contract | **N** | Largest single customer = **24.9% of net revenue** in FY2026 (22.8% FY2025, 23.2% FY2024); the other named >10% customers were 22.5%, 15.0% and 12.1% in FY2026 [FY26 10-K, Note 1 — Concentration of Credit Risk and Accounts Receivable, p.68]. Computed: 24.9% ÷ 100% of revenue = **24.9%**, below the 40% threshold. The customers are platform partners named in the filing — "Apple, Sony, Google, and Microsoft each accounting for more than 10.0% of our net revenue" [FY26 10-K, Item 1 — Business, p.6]. Separate observation, not this trigger: the **five largest together** are 80.6% of net revenue (81.0% FY2025, 79.8% FY2024) [FY26 10-K, Note 1, p.68] — a real concentration fact that belongs to `customer-geography` and `external-dependency`, since #7 tests a single customer |
| 8 | Negative operating cash flow in 3 of last 4 years (excl. growth-stage) | **N** | Cash from operations, last four fiscal years: **FY2023 +$1.1m**, **FY2024 −$16.1m**, **FY2025 −$45.2m**, **FY2026 +$624.3m** → **2 of 4 negative**, not 3 [FY25 10-K, Consolidated Statements of Cash Flows, p.69 (FY2025 (45.2) / FY2024 (16.1) / FY2023 1.1); FY26 10-K, Consolidated Statements of Cash Flows, p.67 (FY2026 624.3 / FY2025 (45.2) / FY2024 (16.1))]. Vendor read matches exactly: 1.1 / −16.1 / −45.2 / 624.3 [CIQ Financials_Annual → Cash Flow — vendor export]. The growth-stage exclusion is **not** invoked and is not needed: TTWO is a 1993-founded, 12,909-employee issuer [CIQ Public Company Profile — vendor export], not early-stage, and the trigger fails on the count alone. The company's own disclosed reason for the two negative years is cash spent on content: net cash used in operating activities "was primarily due to investments in software development and licenses" [FY25 10-K, MD&A — Liquidity and Capital Resources]. This row is a **near-miss** — see Section 4 |

---

## 2. Triggered Disqualifiers — Detail

No disqualifier triggered.

Two items are recorded here because they are near the line or are genuine gaps, not because they trigger:

**Operating cash flow was negative in two of the last four years.** FY2024 (−$16.1m) and FY2025 (−$45.2m) were cash-negative at the operating line, and FY2023 cleared zero by $1.1m — three consecutive years in which the operations threw off essentially no cash, before FY2026 recovered to +$624.3m [FY25 10-K and FY26 10-K, Consolidated Statements of Cash Flows]. The threshold for disqualifier #8 is three negative years of four; two is below it, so there is no lock. It is logged as a near-miss in Section 4 and handed to the balance-sheet-survival module, where the same pool's vendor credit panel scores overall relative credit health "Bottom" with operational and solvency categories also "Bottom" [CIQ Credit Health Panel → Summary — vendor export]. That panel is a tier-5 vendor score, not a filing, and it is not a disqualifier under any of the 8 tests.

**Integrity signal (CLAUDE.md §24, filter 1): none found, hard or soft.** The pool contains no allegation, enforcement action, investigation, restatement, material weakness, or media claim touching the honesty of the controller or senior management. There is therefore nothing to lock on and nothing soft to route. One governance **data gap** is routed to the management-governance module rather than resolved here: because the DEF 14A export is empty, this pool cannot show (a) whether any officer or director has pledged shares, or (b) the dollar size of the ZelnickMedia Corporation / ZMC Advisors related-party arrangements, which the exhibit index proves are live [FY26 10-K, Exhibit Index, Ex. 10.26–10.29]. Neither is evidence of a problem; both are facts this pool cannot see, and the correct answer is "not disclosed in available data," not an inferred figure.

---

## 3. Verdict-Lock Signal

- **Any disqualifier triggered:** **N**
- **If Y, names:** none
- **Action:** If Y, the synthesizer will lock the final verdict at "Low-quality business — avoid deeper work" regardless of other scores. **No lock applies.** The synthesizer should carry forward only the Section 4 near-miss and the two routed data gaps named in Section 2.

---

## 4. Near-Miss Signals

| # | Disqualifier | Computed ratio | Near-miss band | In band? (Y/N) |
|---|---|---:|---|---|
| 2 | Pledged shares | Not computable — zero pledge disclosure in the pool; no promoter group (US issuer, largest holder ~8.7%) | ≥40% – ≤50% | **N** |
| 3 | Related-party transactions | No RP line in audited revenue or expenses; 0% quantified against the $1,664.1m (revenue) / $1,690.2m (expenses) 25% bars | ≥20% – ≤25% | **N** |
| 5 | Restatement | 0.0% of revenue ($0 ÷ $6,656.4m); 0.0% of \|net loss\| ($0 ÷ $298.2m) | ≥4% – ≤5% | **N** |
| 7 | Customer concentration | 24.9% (largest single customer ÷ FY2026 net revenue) | ≥32% – ≤40% | **N** |
| 8 | Negative OCF years (of last 4) | 2 of 4 (FY2024 −$16.1m, FY2025 −$45.2m; FY2023 +$1.1m, FY2026 +$624.3m) | exactly 2 | **Y** |

- **Near-misses in band:** 1 of 5
- **Compounding signal:** None — fewer than 2 near-misses
