## business-model / 04_unit-economics.md

_Source: `04_unit-economics.md`_

# Unit Economics — TTWO

**Regime:** US / SEC domestic filer, US GAAP, USD, fiscal year ends March 31. FY2026 = year ended March 31, 2026; FY2027 is in progress. All dollar figures in millions unless stated. Sources used: `FY26 10-K` (year ended March 31, 2026), `FY25 10-K` (year ended March 31, 2025), `Q2 FY27 10-Q` (quarter and six months ended September 30, 2026), `Q2 FY27 investor deck` and `Q2 FY27 earnings call` (both November 9, 2026), `Q1 FY27 earnings call` (August 7, 2026), and the tier-5 `CIQ Financials_Annual` export.

**Upstream used:** `03_segment-map.md` was present and read. It finds ONE reportable segment — "sale of software titles", 100% of $6,656.4 FY26 revenue, with segment profit equal to the consolidated operating loss of $(104.2) [`FY26 10-K, Note 21, p.102`]. There is therefore no segment-level margin split to work with, and the unit below is chosen for the whole company.

**Hard disclosure limit, carried from `02_business-identity.md` and confirmed independently here:** the pool contains almost no engagement data. The FY26 10-K discloses no monthly or daily active users, no paying-player count, no revenue per player, and no quantified player-acquisition cost. So the volume half of `players × spend per player` **cannot be read** — it is "Not proven from available data", not an estimation problem. Two transcript fragments are the only exceptions and are used below: a 15% rise in average daily active users for NBA 2K26 [`Q1 FY27 earnings call, Aug 7 2026, prepared remarks`] and 11 million registered users for one China mobile title [`Q2 FY27 earnings call, Nov 9 2026, prepared remarks`].

**Pool notes honoured:** the DEF 14A export (July 27, 2026) is an empty "no tabular data" shell and was not used; `Alphabet-Inc-NasdaqGS-GOOGL-Key-Developments` is a misfiled export for a different issuer and was not read as TTWO evidence.

---

## 1. Natural Unit

**The natural economic unit for the "sale of software titles" segment is one released game title — the franchise release that Take-Two funds with development capital up front, then earns back over that title's life from full-game sales plus continuing in-game spending.**

This is the unit the company itself capitalises and amortises: development costs are capitalised "on a product-by-product basis" and amortisation "is recorded on a **title-by-title basis** in cost of revenue" [`FY26 10-K, Note 1 — Software Development Costs and Licenses`]. So the title-level profit-and-loss exists inside the company; it is simply not published.

Three secondary unit types sit inside the same segment, and each is named here rather than modelled: **the individual paying player** (mobile in-app purchases, where 50.1% of FY26 revenue sits — unreadable, no player counts or spend-per-player disclosed) [`FY26 10-K, MD&A, p.44`]; **the sold-in copy** (lifetime franchise unit counts are disclosed, per-period units and price per unit are not); and **the advertising impression** (FY26 advertising revenue $494.3 of $6,656.4, with no impression count and no price per thousand impressions disclosed) [`FY26 10-K, Consolidated Statements of Operations`].

---

## 2. Unit Economics Table

Unit = one released game title / franchise release. No column is inverted. "Direction" compares FY26 with FY25 on the same basis, and adds the six months to September 30, 2026 against the same six months a year earlier where the interim filing gives a like-for-like comparable.

| Unit Economic | Value | Period | Direction vs Prior Year | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Revenue per unit** — per individual title, per period | **Not disclosed.** The filings give only (a) lifetime units for a few titles and (b) the year-on-year *change* in franchise revenue, never a franchise's absolute revenue. Lifetime units disclosed: Grand Theft Auto series >465m sold-in; Grand Theft Auto V >225m (released 2013); Red Dead Redemption 2 >80m; Borderlands franchise >100m; Zynga portfolio >10bn downloads. NBA 2K26 sold-in over 12m units; NBA 2K27 over 4m units so far. Management states 15 franchises have sold at least 5m units in a single release on PC and console | Lifetime to Mar 31, 2026 (10-K) and to Nov 9, 2026 (call) | **Improving on the one title with a stated growth rate:** NBA 2K26's 12m units were "9% growth compared to NBA 2K25". Against that, NBA 2K27 launched *below* the company's own forecast | `FY26 10-K, Item 1 Business, pp.4–6`; `Q1 FY27 earnings call, Aug 7 2026, prepared remarks` (NBA 2K26 12m units, +9%); `Q2 FY27 earnings call, Nov 9 2026, prepared remarks` (NBA 2K27 >4m units; Borderlands >100m; GTA V "nearly 235 million"; 15 franchises ≥5m units); `Q2 FY27 investor deck, p.4` (NBA 2K27 "below our forecast for the period") |
| **Revenue per unit** — average per top-five franchise (the closest sourced proxy) | **~$722.9 per franchise** (FY26): the five best-selling franchises were 54.3% of net revenue = $3,614.4, ÷ 5 = $722.9. FY25: 53.1% × $5,633.6 = $2,991.4, ÷ 5 = $598.3. Grand Theft Auto products alone: 12.4% × $6,656.4 = **$825.4** (FY26) vs 12.6% × $5,633.6 = $709.8 (FY25) | FY26 vs FY25 | **Improving.** Average top-five franchise revenue +20.8% ($598.3 → $722.9); Grand Theft Auto products +16.3% ($709.8 → $825.4). Caveat on basis: the filing says the five franchises "may change year over year", so this is a rotating basket, not the same five titles twice | Percentages: `FY26 10-K, Item 1A Risk Factors, p.10` (12.4% and 54.3%); `FY25 10-K, Item 1A Risk Factors` (12.6% and 53.1%). Revenue bases: `FY26 10-K, MD&A, p.43`. Dollar figures computed from those two disclosures |
| **Gross margin per unit** | **Not disclosed at title level.** The company confirms it computes content amortisation title-by-title but publishes margin only consolidated: gross margin (revenue minus cost of revenue, as a share of revenue) **57.2%** FY26, 54.3% FY25, 41.9% FY24. Latest interim: 57.6% for the six months to Sep 30, 2026. Management's only directional statement by unit type: mobile "generally generates a lower gross margin percentage than our Console or PC revenue" because Apple and Google platform fees sit in cost of revenue — **the size of that gap, and the platform fee rate itself, are not disclosed anywhere in the pool** | FY26 / FY25 / FY24; six months to Sep 30, 2026 | **Improving on the annual basis** (+290 basis points, 54.3% → 57.2%; a basis point is one hundredth of a percentage point). **Deteriorating on the latest like-for-like interim**: 57.6% for the six months to Sep 30, 2026 vs 58.7% for the same six months a year earlier, −110bp | `FY26 10-K, Note 1 — Software Development Costs and Licenses` (title-by-title); `FY26 10-K, MD&A, p.43` (57.2% / 54.3% / 41.9%); `Q2 FY27 10-Q, MD&A, p.29` (57.6% vs 58.7%); `Q2 FY27 10-Q, MD&A, p.23` (mobile lower margin) |
| **Contribution margin per unit (after variable costs)** | **Not disclosed per title.** Built here from the filing's own five cost-of-revenue lines by separating the costs that move with each extra sale from the costs that recover already-spent content capital. Sales-linked lines FY26: product costs $863.8 + licences $463.5 + internal royalties $417.4 = **$1,744.7, or 26.2% of revenue** → contribution before any content build **$4,911.7, or 73.8%** of revenue. The other $1,102.0 of cost of revenue (game intangibles $662.2 + software development costs and royalties $439.8, 16.6% of revenue) is amortisation of content already paid for, not a per-sale cost. On the filing's own accrual basis, gross profit less selling and marketing = $3,809.7 − $1,770.8 = **$2,038.9, 30.6% of revenue**. *Inference, not from filings — the filing labels no line "variable"; the split of the five lines is mine* | FY26 | **Improving on both measures.** Sales-linked cost ratio 28.3% FY25 → 26.2% FY26 (−205bp), and 25.4% → 24.7% for the six months to Sep 30, 2026 (−66bp). Gross profit less selling and marketing 24.5% FY25 → 30.6% FY26, and 29.9% → 30.6% in the latest six months | Cost lines: `FY26 10-K, MD&A, p.44` (FY26 and FY25); `Q2 FY27 10-Q, MD&A, p.29` (six-month lines). Selling and marketing $1,770.8 / $1,683.7: `FY26 10-K, MD&A, p.43`; $814.6 / $945.9: `Q2 FY27 10-Q, Condensed Consolidated Statements of Operations` |
| **Cost to acquire / build the unit** | **Not disclosed per title** — the company never gives a title count or a per-title capitalised balance. Disclosed in aggregate: cash spend on software development costs and licences **$688.9** FY26 ($691.6 FY25, $603.4 FY24), plus research and development expensed before a title is judged technically feasible **$1,074.6** FY26 ($1,005.2 FY25) = total content spend **$1,763.5, 26.5% of FY26 revenue**. Balance-sheet stock of unamortised content: $68.8 current + $2,277.5 non-current = **$2,346.3** at Mar 31, 2026, of which **$2,149.7 (91.6%) relates to titles not yet released**. **Player-acquisition cost per install: Not disclosed** — described only qualitatively as sitting inside selling and marketing of $1,770.8 (26.6% of FY26 revenue), with the company stating its "effectiveness or cost… may change" | FY26, FY25, FY24; balance at Mar 31, 2026 | **Mixed.** Total content spend up 3.9% in dollars ($1,696.8 → $1,763.5) but down as a share of revenue (30.1% → 26.5%) — improving on the ratio. The unreleased-title book grew 18.4% ($1,815.0 → $2,149.7), so more capital is parked in titles that have earned nothing yet — deteriorating on cash tied up | `FY26 10-K, Consolidated Statements of Cash Flows` ($688.9 / $691.6 / $603.4); `FY26 10-K, MD&A, p.43` (R&D $1,074.6 / $1,005.2 / $948.2; selling and marketing $1,770.8); `FY26 10-K, Note 7 (Software Development Costs and Licenses), p.83` ($68.8 / $2,277.5 / $2,149.7 / $1,815.0); `FY26 10-K, MD&A, p.44 — Player acquisition costs` |
| **Payback period or unit lifetime** | **Payback period: Not disclosed** — it cannot be computed, because neither per-title revenue nor per-title cost is published. Two lifetimes **are** disclosed. (1) **Title accounting life:** "Amortization periods for our software products generally range from **9 to 36 months**." (2) **Player life**, which the company defines explicitly as "the time period in which an average user plays our software products ('user life')", measured as "the weighted average number of days between players' first day played online or first in-game purchase and last day played online": "generally between **five and fifteen months** depending on the software product". Observed longevity on the best title far exceeds both: Grand Theft Auto V, released 2013, had sold-in >225m units by Mar 31, 2026 and "nearly 235 million" by Nov 9, 2026 | FY26 10-K vs FY25 10-K; GTA V to Nov 9, 2026 | **Deteriorating on both disclosed lifetimes.** Title amortisation period: FY25 "**12 to 36 months**" → FY26 "**9 to 36 months**" — the bottom of the range shortened by three months. Player life: FY25 "**six and fifteen months**" → FY26 "**five and fifteen months**" — bottom shortened by one month, and the Q2 FY27 10-Q repeats five to fifteen. These are management's own estimates of how long a title and a player last, and both got shorter | `FY26 10-K, Note 1 — Software Development Costs and Licenses` (9 to 36 months); `FY25 10-K, Note 1 — Software Development Costs and Licenses` (12 to 36 months); `FY26 10-K, Note 2 — Estimated Service Period` (five to fifteen months, user-life definition); `FY25 10-K, Note 2 — Estimated Service Period` (six to fifteen months); `Q2 FY27 10-Q, MD&A, p.24` (five to fifteen); `FY26 10-K, Item 1, p.4`; `Q2 FY27 earnings call, Nov 9 2026` |

**One per-player datapoint, and it points down.** For NBA 2K26 in the June 2026 quarter, recurrent consumer spending grew 7% while average daily active users grew 15% (with MyCAREER daily active users +25% and average games played per user +35%) [`Q1 FY27 earnings call, Aug 7 2026, prepared remarks`]. Revenue growing slower than the player count means **spend per active player fell about 7%** for that title that quarter (1.07 ÷ 1.15 − 1 = −7.0%). *Inference, not from filings — the company published the two growth rates, not the ratio.* It is one title, one quarter, and no absolute player number is given, so it cannot be scaled to the group.

---

## 3. Value Creation Read

**Unclear at the unit level — the per-unit math cannot be done from what Take-Two discloses — and the only hard aggregate evidence available points the other way.** The value test (contribution margin × unit lifetime > cost to build the unit) needs per-title revenue and per-title cost; the company publishes neither, so no title can be tested. What can be tested is the sum of all titles, and over the three audited years FY24–FY26 the sum does not clear: total content spend was $5,011.9 ($1,983.9 capitalised cash plus $3,028.0 of expensed research and development) against gross profit less selling and marketing of $4,109.0 — a shortfall of **$902.9** over three years [`FY26 10-K, MD&A, p.43`; `FY26 10-K, Consolidated Statements of Cash Flows`]. The audited bottom line says the same: operating losses in each of the last three years — $(104.2) FY26, $(4,391.1) FY25, $(3,590.6) FY24 — and $5,887.3 of accumulated goodwill impairment against gross goodwill of $6,949.2, i.e. roughly 85% of all goodwill ever recorded written off, caused by "a reduction in the forecasted performance of the reporting unit" [`FY26 10-K, MD&A, p.43`; `FY26 10-K, Note 9, p.83`; `FY26 10-K, Critical Accounting Policies — Goodwill`]. A write-off of that size is the audited statement that one large block of acquired game units did not earn back what was paid for it.

**Ring-fence on the cycle, in both directions.** FY26 is **not** a peak-cycle year and must not be read as one: it is the last full year before the largest launch in the company's history, with Grand Theft Auto VI released November 19, 2026 [`Q2 FY27 10-Q`; `Q2 FY27 investor deck, p.13`]. Equally, FY27 will not be a structural year. The through-cycle return series shows both ends: return on invested capital (the profit earned on each dollar of capital put into the business) ran 6.0% → 6.9% → 7.4% → 11.2% → 13.4% → 9.0% in FY2017–FY2022, then turned negative in every year since — $(4.3)\%$ FY2023, $(3.1)\%$ FY2024, $(4.2)\%$ FY2025, $(0.8)\%$ FY2026, $(1.0)\%$ for the twelve months to Sep 30, 2026 [`CIQ Financials_Annual → Ratios`, retrieved 2026-11-19 — **tier-5 vendor export, not a filing**]. So the honest through-cycle read is: single-digit-to-low-teens positive returns before FY2023, negative for four straight years since, with the best year in a decade still only 13.4%.

**Why the three-year shortfall is not yet proof of destruction.** $2,149.7 of the $2,346.3 capitalised content balance — 91.6% — relates to titles **not yet released** [`FY26 10-K, Note 7, p.83`], so FY24–FY26 spending was substantially building revenue for FY27 and later while FY24–FY26 gross profit carried only older vintages. That makes the three-year comparison conservative-biased against the company by a size the filings do not let an outsider measure. The test resolves in FY27, and management's own guidance is the first resolution point: GAAP net revenue $7,980–$8,180 with GAAP net income of **$109–$142**, i.e. a net margin of roughly **1.3%–1.8%** in the year carrying the biggest release the company has ever had [`Q2 FY27 investor deck, p.7`]. That is thin for a cycle peak, and it is guidance, not a result.

**Single most valuable missing disclosure:** a title-level vintage statement — for each released title, cumulative net revenue to date against the capitalised development cost of that title. The company already computes both halves (amortisation is "recorded on a title-by-title basis"), and revenue is partly triangulable from the concentration percentages; the cost side is not triangulable at all, which is why one title-level vintage table would do more than any other single disclosure.

---

## 4. Sensitivity

**Revenue per unit — the hit outcome — dominates, by roughly two to one over the next-largest lever.** A 20% move in top-five-franchise revenue is **$722.9** (20% of $3,614.4), against an FY26 operating loss of $(104.2) — a swing about seven times the size of the loss, and on its own enough to decide whether the year is profitable. The next-largest levers are smaller and close to each other: a 20% relative move in the sales-linked cost ratio (26.2% of revenue → 21.0% or 31.4%) is **$348.9**, and a 20% move in total content spend ($1,763.5) is **$352.7**. Price per unit is the smallest lever of the three that can be sized — a 20% move in all full-game-and-other revenue is $292.0 (20% of $1,459.8) — and the company routinely gives price away anyway, cutting wholesale prices "typically three to nine months after a product's initial launch" [`FY26 10-K, Item 1 — Marketing`]. Retention cannot be sized: a 20% shortening of the disclosed five-to-fifteen-month player life would move both the revenue-recognition period and the amortisation schedule, but with no player count or spend-per-player disclosed, the dollar effect is **not assessable** from this pool — the same gap applies to the platform fee, whose rate is nowhere in the pool even though Apple and Google "generally have the discretion to set the amounts of their platform fees… at their sole discretion" [`FY26 10-K, MD&A, p.44`].

**Which has historically been most volatile: per-unit revenue, clearly.** Group revenue rose 18.2% in FY26 on five franchise moves, then fell 7.5% in the six months to September 30, 2026 [`FY26 10-K, MD&A, p.44`; `Q2 FY27 10-Q, MD&A, p.29`]. Q2 FY27 net bookings came in at $1,601 against guidance of $1,620–$1,670, with recurrent consumer spending down 9% against guidance of down 5% [`Q2 FY27 investor deck, p.4`]. Vendor EBITDA swung $380 (FY25) → $788 (FY26) → $725 (twelve months to Sep 30, 2026) [`CIQ Financials_Annual → Income Statement` — tier-5 vendor export]. And the timing of a single unit has slipped repeatedly: the FY25 10-K stated Grand Theft Auto VI was planned for May 26, 2026; the FY26 10-K states November 19, 2026 [`FY25 10-K, MD&A`; `FY26 10-K, MD&A, p.44`]. By contrast the cost ratios moved only 205bp (sales-linked costs) and 363bp (content spend as a share of revenue) between FY25 and FY26. The volatility is in what each unit earns and when it ships, not in what it costs to make or in its price.



---

## business-model / 05_customer-geography.md

_Source: `05_customer-geography.md`_

# Customer And Geography Map — TTWO

**Regime:** US / SEC domestic filer (NasdaqGS:TTWO, Delaware), US GAAP, reporting currency USD, fiscal year ends **March 31** — "FY2026" means the year to March 31, 2026 [`FY26 10-K, cover page`; `00_data-triage.md §2A`]. All dollar figures are USD millions as the filing states them.

**What "customer" means here.** Take-Two does not sell most of its games to players directly. It sells through the companies that own the storefronts and consoles — Apple's App Store, the Google Play Store, Sony's PlayStation network, Microsoft's Xbox network, Valve's Steam — plus physical retailers and distributors. Those platform owners are the company's accounting customers, and that is why four of them each exceed 10% of revenue. [`FY26 10-K, Item 1 — Sales, p.4`; `FY26 10-K, Note 1 (Concentration of Credit Risk and Accounts Receivable), p.68`]

---

## 1. Customer Map

The FY26 10-K discloses the concentration two ways that do not join up: the **risk factor and Item 1 name** the four customers above 10% (Apple, Sony, Google, Microsoft) without percentages, and **Note 1 gives four unnamed percentages** without names. The filing never maps a name to a number, so neither do I.

| Customer Type | Importance (% of revenue if disclosed) | Long-term Contract? (Y/N/Not disclosed) | Evidence | Risk |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Five largest customers, combined** | **80.6%** of FY26 net revenue; 81.0% FY25; 79.8% FY24. **80.8%** of H1 FY27 net revenue (six months to Sep 30, 2026); 81.5% prior-year H1 | **N — "Our sales are made primarily without long-term agreements or other commitments, and our customers may terminate their relationship with us at any time"** | `FY26 10-K, Note 1 (Concentration of Credit Risk), p.68`; `Q2 FY27 10-Q, Concentration of Credit Risk note`; quote from `FY26 10-K, Item 1A Risk Factors, p.23` | Four-fifths of revenue passes through five counterparties that can walk away at will |
| Customer #1 (unnamed in the note) | **24.9%** FY26; 22.8% FY25; 23.2% FY24 | N (same risk-factor language) | `FY26 10-K, Note 1, p.68` | Single largest relationship is a quarter of revenue |
| Customer #2 (unnamed) | **22.5%** FY26; 24.4% FY25; 21.1% FY24 | N | `FY26 10-K, Note 1, p.68` | Second >20% relationship |
| Customer #3 (unnamed) | **15.0%** FY26; 17.4% FY25; 18.6% FY24 — falling three years running | N | `FY26 10-K, Note 1, p.68` | Declining share; the filing gives no reason |
| Customer #4 (unnamed) | **12.1%** FY26; 10.1% FY25; 12.3% FY24 | N | `FY26 10-K, Note 1, p.68` | Fourth relationship above the 10% disclosure line |
| Customer #5 (unnamed) | **~6.1%** FY26 — derived as 80.6% top-five less the 74.5% the four named percentages sum to (24.9 + 22.5 + 15.0 + 12.1). *My arithmetic from the filing's own figures, not a disclosed number.* | N | Derived from `FY26 10-K, Note 1, p.68` | Below the 10% disclosure threshold |
| **Named >10% customers (names only, no individual percentages)** | Each of **Apple, Sony, Google, Microsoft** "accounting for more than 10.0% of our net revenue" in FY26 — same four names in FY25 | Platform licences exist with Sony and Microsoft (see next two rows). **Apple and Google: N** — the company is "subject to the standard policies and terms of service of third-party platforms," and "each platform provider has broad discretion to change and interpret its terms of service" | `FY26 10-K, Item 1 — Sales, p.4` and `Item 1A, p.23`; `FY25 10-K, Item 1A Risk Factors (same disclosure, FY25 top five 81.0%)`; Apple/Google terms language from `FY26 10-K, Item 1A, p.11` | Two of the four >10% customers are governed by terms of service the customer writes and can rewrite |
| — Sony, as platform licensor and customer | Inside the four >10% names; individual share not disclosed | **Y, but short and cancellable:** PlayStation Global Developer and Publisher Agreement (effective Mar 23, 2017, PS5 Amendment Sep 30, 2020) **expires March 31, 2027**, then automatic one-year renewals; **"Sony may terminate the agreement for any or no reason upon 30 days' notice"** | `FY26 10-K, Item 1 — Arrangements with Platform Manufacturers, p.3` | A 30-day at-will termination right held by a >10% customer |
| — Microsoft, as platform licensor and customer | Inside the four >10% names; individual share not disclosed | **Y, short:** Xbox 360 Publisher Licence Agreement (Nov 17, 2005) and Xbox Console Publisher Licence Agreement (Jul 1, 2020) both **expire March 31, 2027**, then automatic one-year renewals unless either side gives notice | `FY26 10-K, Item 1 — Arrangements with Platform Manufacturers, p.4` | Both console licences expire on the same date, 4.4 months after this report's date |
| Mobile revenue routed through Apple and Google | **91.0% of mobile revenue** in FY26 ran through Apple and Google platforms; mobile was **50.1% of total FY26 net revenue** — so roughly **46%** of group revenue reaches the company through two app stores (*my arithmetic: 0.910 × 0.501; not a disclosed figure*) | N (terms of service, as above) | `FY26 10-K, Item 1A, p.11` (91.0%); `FY26 10-K, MD&A (Results of Operations — net revenue by platform) — "Net revenue from mobile increased by $391.0 and accounted for 50.1% of our total net revenue"` | Two companies control the storefront, the payment rails, and the fee on about half the business |
| Console platform channel (Sony PlayStation + Microsoft Xbox hardware) | Console revenue by product platform = **39.0%** of FY26 net revenue (37.3% FY25) | Y via the two licences above, both expiring Mar 31, 2027 | `FY26 10-K, Item 1A, p.11`; `FY26 10-K, MD&A (console 39.0% vs 37.3%)` | Needs those licences renewed to publish at all |
| Physical retailers and third-party distributors | **3.0%** of FY26 net revenue came from channels other than digital online (digital online = 97.0%); down from 3.6% in FY25. No individual retailer disclosed | Not disclosed | `FY26 10-K, MD&A — "Net revenue from digital online channels … accounted for 97.0% … as compared to 3.0%… / 3.6% for the prior year period"` | Shrinking channel; the filing still flags that bankruptcies or consolidation of large retail customers "could seriously hurt our business" [`FY26 10-K, Item 1A, p.23`] |
| Named outside parties on the customer side of the vendor graph (existence only, no size) | **Not disclosed** — the export never says what share of revenue any relationship is | Not disclosed | `CIQ Customers export (TTWO), retrieved 2026-11-19 — vendor export`, scope: *"Recently disclosed customers only (within the last two years)"*, *"Include Customers for: Current subsidiaries"* | Confirms who, never how much |

**Named customer-side counterparties from the vendor graph** — existence only, never size (`CIQ Customers export (TTWO), retrieved 2026-11-19 — vendor export`). Only `third_party` rows are outside parties:

| Named counterparty | Listing | Industry | Role | Who disclosed it | Disclosing document (graph `source_ref`) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Microsoft Corporation | NASDAQGS:MSFT | Systems Software | Distributor | Take-Two itself | TTWO 2026 Form 10-K |
| Apple Inc. | NASDAQGS:AAPL | Technology Hardware | Distributor | Take-Two itself | TTWO 2026 Form 10-K |
| Google LLC | unlisted | Application Software | Distributor | Take-Two itself | TTWO 2026 Form 10-K |
| Sony Group Corporation | TSE:6758 | Consumer Electronics | Customer | Take-Two itself | TTWO 2015 Form 10-K |
| Nintendo Co., Ltd. | TSE:7974 | Interactive Home Entertainment | Customer | Take-Two itself | TTWO 2026 Form 10-K |
| Valve Corporation | unlisted | Interactive Home Entertainment | Distributor (Steam) | Take-Two itself | TTWO 2026 Form 10-K |
| Meta Platforms, Inc. | NASDAQGS:META | Interactive Media and Services | Distributor | Take-Two itself | TTWO 2026 Form 10-K |
| Snap Inc. | NYSE:SNAP | Interactive Media and Services | Distributor | Take-Two itself | TTWO 2026 Form 10-K |
| Alliance Entertainment Holding Corporation | NASDAQCM:AENT | Distributors | Distributor | **The counterparty** — Alliance named Take-Two in its own 10-K | Alliance Entertainment (NasdaqCM:AENT) 2026 Form 10-K |
| Thunderful Group AB | unlisted in export | Technology Distributors | Distributor | **The counterparty** | Thunderful Group AB 2024 filing |

Three graph rows on the customer side are flagged `likely_group` and are therefore **excluded from every concentration claim above**: *Sony Interactive Entertainment Inc.*, *Epic Games, Inc.*, and *Prima Interactive (Pty) Ltd*. Two of those flags are brand-matching artefacts of the vendor parser rather than real group membership — the FY26 10-K's own exhibit index lists licence agreements with "Sony Interactive Entertainment America LLC" and "Sony Interactive Entertainment Europe Ltd" as outside counterparties, and the filing wins over the vendor export (CLAUDE.md §4). *Prima Interactive* was disclosed only by a third issuer (Deneb Investments Limited, JSE:DNB), never by a Take-Two filing, so it stays flagged as related until a filing says otherwise. The graph reports **0 intra-group rows (0.0% intragroup row share)**, so there is no related-party trading pattern to route onward from this export.

**Scope limit, carried as required:** the graph covers *"Recently disclosed customers only (within the last two years)"* for *"Current subsidiaries"*. It is not Take-Two's full customer list, a counterparty's absence from it proves nothing, and it never states the size of any relationship. The percentages in the table above all come from the 10-K and 10-Q, never from the export.

---

## 2. Geography Map

The filing gives exactly two geographic buckets — United States and International — attributed "based on software product destination" [`FY26 10-K, Note 21 (Segment Reporting and Geographic Information), p.102`]. **No country-level or region-level split is disclosed anywhere in the pool.** Shares sum to 100.0%; there is no "Other" bucket to note.

| Geography | % of Revenue | Trend (Growing / Stable / Declining / Unknown) | Evidence | Risk |
|---|---:|---|---|---|
| **United States** | **59.2%** of FY26 (USD 3,940.4m of 6,656.4m) | **Growing in dollars, shrinking in share:** 3,279.2 (FY24) → 3,406.8 (FY25) → 3,940.4 (FY26); share 61.3% → 60.5% → 59.2%. **H1 FY27 reversed in dollars: 1,808.9 vs 1,936.5 a year earlier (−6.6%)** at 59.7% share | `FY26 10-K, Note 21, p.102`; `Q2 FY27 10-Q, Note — Geography` | One country is the majority of revenue. US-specific regulation on loot boxes, age ratings, and app-store rules hits most of the business at once |
| **International** (Europe, Asia, Australia, Canada, Latin America — "primarily in Europe"; no individual country disclosed) | **40.8%** of FY26 (USD 2,716.0m) | **Growing in dollars and share:** 2,070.4 (FY24) → 2,226.8 (FY25) → 2,716.0 (FY26); share 38.7% → 39.5% → 40.8%. **H1 FY27 also down in dollars: 1,222.0 vs 1,341.1 (−8.9%)**; Q2 FY27 standalone 40.6% of revenue earned outside the US vs 41.6% a year earlier | `FY26 10-K, Note 21, p.102` and `FY26 10-K, Item 1A Risk Factors (international trade / FX) — "Sales in international markets, primarily in Europe, have accounted for a significant portion of our net revenue… 40.8%"`; `Q2 FY27 10-Q, Item 1A / Quantitative and Qualitative Disclosures` | Currency exposure sized by the company: "a hypothetical 10.0% increase in the value of the U.S. dollar against all currencies" is run against the 40.8% of revenue generated outside the US [`FY26 10-K, Item 7A (Quantitative and Qualitative Disclosures About Market Risk — FX sensitivity analysis)`] |
| Total | **100.0%** | — | `FY26 10-K, Note 21, p.102` (6,656.4m total) | — |
| *Cross-check* | Vendor read agrees: "United States 3,940 (59%); International 2,716 (41%) of Total 6,656" for the 12 months to Mar-31-2026 | — | `ciq_facts.json → geographic` (`CIQ Financials → Segments (Geographic Segment → Revenues)`) — tier-5 vendor export, reconciled to the filing with no material gap | — |
| *Where the assets sit (not revenue)* | Fixed assets, net: US USD 274.5m (61.6%) / International USD 170.9m (38.4%) at Mar 31, 2026 | Stable (263.5 / 180.3 at Mar 31, 2025) | `FY26 10-K, Note 21, p.103` | Shown so the revenue split is not mistaken for the footprint |

Note on the H1 FY27 declines in both buckets: *Grand Theft Auto VI* released **November 19, 2026** — after the September 30, 2026 quarter end — so the first half of FY27 is a pre-release trough, not a geographic shift [`Q2 FY27 10-Q, which states Grand Theft Auto VI releases November 19, 2026`; `00_data-triage.md §1`]. Reading the H1 geography move as a trend in either market would be wrong.

---

## 3. Concentration Flags

| Concentration Flag | Triggered (Y/N) | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| **One customer >20% of revenue** | **Y** | Two customers clear 20% in the same year: 24.9% and 22.5% of FY26 net revenue; in H1 FY27 the largest reached 27.1% and the second 20.7% [`FY26 10-K, Note 1 (Concentration of Credit Risk), p.68`; `Q2 FY27 10-Q, Concentration of Credit Risk note`] |
| **Top 3 customers >40% of revenue** | **Y** | **62.4%** of FY26 net revenue (24.9% + 22.5% + 15.0%); **62.5%** of H1 FY27 net revenue (27.1% + 20.7% + 14.7%). Top five = 80.6% FY26 / 80.8% H1 FY27 [`FY26 10-K, Note 1, p.68`; `Q2 FY27 10-Q, Concentration of Credit Risk note`] |
| **One geography >50% of revenue** | **Y** | United States = **59.2%** of FY26 net revenue (USD 3,940.4m of 6,656.4m); 59.7% of H1 FY27 [`FY26 10-K, Note 21 (Geographic Information), p.102`; `Q2 FY27 10-Q, Note — Geography`] |
| **One customer or geography >30% with no long-term contract disclosed** | **Y — on the geography limb, and on the top-five customer group** | The **geography limb triggers**: the US is 59.2% of FY26 revenue, and the company states "our sales are made primarily without long-term agreements or other commitments, and our customers may terminate their relationship with us at any time" [`FY26 10-K, Item 1A Risk Factors, p.23`; `Note 21, p.102`]. The **single-customer limb does not** on its own: the largest disclosed individual customer is 24.9% in FY26 and 27.1% in H1 FY27 — both above 20% but below 30% [`FY26 10-K, Note 1, p.68`; `Q2 FY27 10-Q`]. Taken as a group, the top five are 80.6% of revenue on the same no-long-term-agreement footing, which is the same exposure the flag is designed to catch |
| *Added flag — contract-expiry cluster (not in the standard four)* | **Y** | Both console platform licences — the Sony PlayStation Global Developer and Publisher Agreement and both Microsoft Xbox Publisher Licence Agreements — **expire on the same date, March 31, 2027**, 4.4 months after this report. Each then renews automatically for one year unless a party gives notice, and **Sony may terminate "for any or no reason upon 30 days' notice"** [`FY26 10-K, Item 1 — Arrangements with Platform Manufacturers, pp.3–4`]. Those licences cover the 39.0% of FY26 revenue earned on consoles [`FY26 10-K, Item 1A, p.11`] |

All four standard flags trigger Y. No flag was softened.

---

## 4. Read

This is a concentrated business on both sides, and the customer side is the sharper of the two: five customers are **80.6% of FY26 net revenue** and two of them individually clear 22% [`FY26 10-K, Note 1, p.68`], while one country — the United States — is **59.2%** of revenue [`FY26 10-K, Note 21, p.102`]. The concentration is close to naked rather than contractually secured: the company states in its own risk factors that sales "are made primarily without long-term agreements or other commitments, and our customers may terminate their relationship with us at any time" [`FY26 10-K, Item 1A, p.23`], and two of the four >10% customers — Apple and Google — are governed only by platform terms of service the customer itself can rewrite [`FY26 10-K, Item 1A, p.11`]. What contracts do exist are thin cover: both the Sony and Microsoft console licences expire March 31, 2027 with one-year automatic renewals, and Sony holds a right to terminate "for any or no reason upon 30 days' notice" [`FY26 10-K, Item 1 — Arrangements with Platform Manufacturers, pp.3–4`].

**The single biggest dependency for the synthesizer:** Take-Two reaches its players almost entirely through four gatekeepers — Apple, Sony, Google and Microsoft, each individually above 10% of net revenue and together inside a top five that is 80.6% of it [`FY26 10-K, Item 1 — Sales, p.4`; `Note 1, p.68`] — on terms those gatekeepers set, can change, and in Sony's case can cancel on 30 days' notice. Every one of them also publishes or distributes competing games, which the filing itself flags: "because some of our customers are also publishers of games for their own hardware platforms… such customers may give priority to their own products or those of our competitors" [`FY26 10-K, Item 1A, p.23`].

**Not proven from available data:** which named customer corresponds to which disclosed percentage (the 10-K separates the names from the numbers); any country-level revenue split inside the 40.8% International bucket; and the size of any relationship named in the Capital IQ Customers export, which proves existence only.



---

## business-model / 06_value-chain.md

_Source: `06_value-chain.md`_

# Value Chain Position — TTWO

**Regime:** US SEC domestic filer (NasdaqGS:TTWO, Delaware), US GAAP, reporting currency USD (millions as the filing states them), fiscal year ends **March 31** — "FY2026" = year to March 31, 2026 [`FY26 10-K, cover page`; `00_data-triage.md §2A`]. Upstream inputs read: `02_business-identity.md`, `00_data-triage.md`, `03_segment-map.md`, `05_customer-geography.md`, `10_external-dependency.md`.

**Where the chain actually bites.** Take-Two owns the content and the brands but owns none of the doors to the player. It buys permission to publish from Sony and Microsoft, pays Apple and Google a fee they alone set to reach phones, and sells to a top five that is **80.6% of FY26 net revenue** [`FY26 10-K, Item 1 — Sales, p.4`]. So this report splits the question in two: the company's power over **price** (which it has, and has chosen not to use fully) versus its power over **terms** (which it does not have at all).

**One data-quality note before the numbers.** The vendor workbook reads gross margin as 58% for both FY25 and FY26, while the 10-K's own cost-of-revenue table reads 54.3% then 57.2%; the vendor flags FY25 as reclassified [`ciq_facts.json → margin_trend` (`CIQ Financials → Income Statement`) — tier-5 vendor export]. The gap on FY25 is 3.7 percentage points and is a basis difference, not a misread of either source. Every margin calculation below uses the 10-K's own table (CLAUDE.md §4, §5), and I flag the vendor difference rather than override it silently.

---

## 1. Stages Occupied

Take-Two does **not** occupy the raw-material or component-supply stages: there is no commodity input. Its real input is developer time, which arrives as payroll and capitalised software, not as a purchased material — R&D was $1,074.6m in FY26, and 9,998 of 12,909 full-time staff sit in development studios [`FY26 10-K, Item 7 (MD&A) — Operating Expenses`; `FY26 10-K, Item 1 — Human Capital Management`].

Bargaining power bands used: **Strong** = sets price, dictates terms, has alternatives. **Mid** = negotiated outcomes, no extreme leverage either way. **Weak** = price-taker, terms imposed, few alternatives. Higher is better; this is not an inverted scale.

| Value Chain Stage | Company Role (1 sentence) | Bargaining Power vs Upstream | Bargaining Power vs Downstream | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Buyer of licensed IP** (sports leagues, players' associations, music, car brands) | It rents the real-world names that 2K's sports games are built on, under fixed-term licences with guaranteed minimums. | **Weak** | n/a — internal transfer to its own publishing arm | Licences cost $463.5m in FY26, 7.0% of net revenue, up 26.7% year on year; "Competition for these licenses is intense… may also increase the advances, guarantees and royalties that we must pay," and on certain licences "we are subject to guaranteed minimum payments, royalties or standards of performance and may not be able to terminate these agreements prior to their stated expiration" [`FY26 10-K, Item 7 (MD&A) — Cost of revenue table, FY26 vs FY25`; `FY26 10-K, Item 1A Risk Factors — ability to acquire and maintain licenses`] |
| **Buyer of outside development and production services** | It contracts part of its game-making and cinematic/production work to third parties. | **Mid** | n/a — internal | "We rely on third-party software developers for the development of some of our titles. Quality third-party developers are continually in high demand… Due to the limited availability of third-party software developers and the limited control that we exercise over them…" and "royalties payable to software developers and third-party licensors have continued to increase and reduce potential profits" [`FY26 10-K, Item 1A Risk Factors — third-party development arrangements`]. Sized: third-party software development costs and royalties were $439.8m, 6.6% of FY26 net revenue, up 161.6%. Contractual minimum software-development-and-licensing commitments are only $175.8m in total [`FY26 10-K, Note 14 (Commitments and Contingencies), p.92`] — so most of the spend is discretionary, which is where the Mid rather than Weak comes from |
| **Manufacturer / processor — own-studio game developer** | It writes most of its own games in its own studios and owns the resulting IP outright. | **Mid** (buys labour in an "extremely competitive" market) | **Strong** — it owns Grand Theft Auto, Red Dead, Borderlands, Civilization, Toon Blast and needs no licensor's permission to make them | "Our business is highly dependent on the creation, acquisition, licensing, and protection of intellectual property. We believe that content ownership facilitates our internal product development efforts and maximizes profit potential"; "The market for highly-skilled workers and leaders in our industry is extremely competitive"; GTA series >465m units lifetime [`FY26 10-K, Item 1 — Intellectual Property`; `FY26 10-K, Item 1A — attracting and retaining talent`; `FY26 10-K, Item 1`] |
| **Licensed producer on someone else's hardware** | It cannot publish a console game at all without a per-title approval from Sony and Microsoft, who are also its competitors. | **Weak** | n/a — the licence is the gate, not a sale | "We are required to obtain licenses from certain of our competitors, including Sony and Microsoft… Our existing platform licenses require that we obtain approval for the publication of new titles on a title-by-title basis"; both console licences **expire March 31, 2027**, and "Sony may terminate the agreement for any or no reason upon 30 days' notice"; physical copies "are required to be manufactured by Sony-approved manufacturers" / "Microsoft-approved manufacturers" [`FY26 10-K, Item 1A — hardware licensors who are also competitors`; `FY26 10-K, Item 1 — Arrangements with Platform Manufacturers, p.3`] |
| **Wholesaler into digital storefronts** | It hands finished games to four storefronts that control discovery, the payment rails, and the fee. | n/a | **Weak** | 97.0% of FY26 net revenue came through digital online channels; five customers = 80.6% of net revenue with Apple, Sony, Google and Microsoft each above 10.0%; 91.0% of mobile revenue ran on Apple and Google; "Apple and Google generally have the discretion to set the amounts of their platform fees"; "The networks provided by these platform providers are the exclusive means of selling and distributing our content on these platforms"; and the company's own words — "Increased competition for digital 'shelf space' has put channel partners in more favorable bargaining positions in relation to such terms of distribution" [`FY26 10-K, Item 7 (MD&A) — net revenue by distribution channel`; `FY26 10-K, Item 1 — Sales, p.4`; `FY26 10-K, Item 7 (MD&A) — Online Content and Digital Distribution`; `FY26 10-K, Item 1A — limited number of channel partners`] |
| **Retailer / platform — own direct-to-consumer commerce** | It sells in-game items straight to mobile players through its own store, paying no app-store fee on those sales. | **Strong** — it is its own supplier here | **Mid**, and the size is not disclosed | "We manage a direct-to-consumer platform, primarily for our mobile business"; "We are also continuing to expand our direct-to-consumer efforts more meaningfully across our mobile portfolio to enhance profitability"; "There is no doubt that our direct-to-consumer business has had a material effect positive on our margins in the Mobile business" — but "We have not described the percentage of our business that comes direct to consumer mobile. It is significant, and it is growing" [`FY26 10-K, Item 1 — Sales, p.4`; `FY26 10-K, Item 7 (MD&A) — Online Content and Digital Distribution`; `Q1 FY27 earnings call, Aug 7 2026, Q&A (Zelnick)`; `Q2 FY27 earnings call, Nov 9 2026, Q&A (Zelnick)`] |
| **Platform / marketplace — in-game advertising seller** | It sells ad space inside its own mobile games to advertisers, using outside ad-tech to fill it. | **Mid** (ad-mediation suppliers named in §2A) | **Mid** | "Our advertising business improved sequentially as we successfully attract new partners and increase ad yields"; advertising sits inside recurrent consumer spending, which was 81% of Q2 FY27 Net Bookings [`Q2 FY27 earnings call, Nov 9 2026, prepared remarks`; `Q2 FY27 earnings release, Nov 9 2026, p.1`] |
| **End-customer-facing brand** | Rockstar, 2K and Zynga are the names players actually want, and the player cannot buy the substitute from anyone else. | n/a | **Strong** | GTA products alone were 12.4% of FY26 net revenue and the top five franchises 54.3%; GTA Online still at "record-setting engagement at times" thirteen years after launch; the company set GTA VI's base price at **$80** against the $70 AAA norm [`FY26 10-K, Item 1A — dependence on key products`; `Q1 FY27 earnings call, Aug 7 2026, Q&A (Zelnick)`] |

The pattern is the point: **Strong where it faces the player, Weak everywhere a gatekeeper stands between the two.**

---

## 2. Input Cost Pass-Through

| Axis | What it asks | Verdict | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Contractual pass-through** | Is there an escalator, indexed-pricing, or cost-plus clause that moves price automatically? | **No — 0% of revenue sits under a disclosed escalator, indexed-pricing or cost-plus clause.** And the contractual asymmetry runs *against* the company on both sides. | There is no contract on the output side to carry an escalator: "Our sales are made primarily without long-term agreements or other commitments, and our customers may terminate their relationship with us at any time" [`FY26 10-K, Item 1A — dependence on a limited number of customers`]. On the input side the counterparties hold the pricing pen: "Some of these platforms have retained the right to change the fee structures for online distribution of both paid content and free content… and their ability to set or influence commission rates and service fees may increase our costs, which could negatively affect our operating margins"; "Apple and Google generally have the discretion to set the amounts of their platform fees"; and on IP, "we are subject to guaranteed minimum payments, royalties or standards of performance and may not be able to terminate these agreements prior to their stated expiration" [`FY26 10-K, Item 1A — limited number of channel partners`; `FY26 10-K, Item 7 (MD&A) — Online Content and Digital Distribution`; `FY26 10-K, Item 1A — ability to acquire and maintain licenses`] |
| **Realised recovery** | Of a given input-cost increase, how much did the company actually recover — through price, mix, hedging, re-sourcing, or a cost programme? | **H1 FY27: 56% recovered as reported; ~106% (fully recovered) once a one-off stock-compensation swing is removed. FY26: ~171%, i.e. more than fully recovered — but the dominant FY26 offset was an accounting one, not an operating one.** Not zero on any basis. | Built below from the 10-K's and 10-Q's own cost-of-revenue tables [`FY26 10-K, Item 7 (MD&A) — Cost of revenue, FY26 vs FY25`; `Q2 FY27 10-Q, Item 2 (MD&A) — Six Months Ended September 30, 2026 Compared to September 30, 2025`] |

**The arithmetic, shown.** Two cost lines rose; the rest of the table and the margin tell you how much of the rise actually landed.

*Six months to September 30, 2026 (the most recent reported period):*
- Licences: 6.9% → **7.6%** of net revenue = **+70bps**
- Third-party software development costs and royalties: 6.2% → **8.0%** = **+180bps**
- Gross pre-mitigation input-cost increase = **+250bps of revenue**
- Gross margin in the event: 58.7% → **57.6%** = **−110bps**
- `realised recovery = 1 − (110 ÷ 250) = 0.56` → **56% recovered**

*Same period, stripping a distortion the filing itself flags.* The development-cost line carries $6.5m of stock-based compensation in FY27 H1 against a $(36.2)m credit in FY26 H1 — a $42.7m swing worth about 141bps on $3,030.9m of revenue, which is most of the apparent 180bps rise [`Q2 FY27 10-Q, Item 2 (MD&A), footnote (1)`]. Ex-stock-compensation, the development line rose only ~53bps and licences ~76bps, so the gross increase was **+129bps**, while gross margin ex-stock-compensation went 57.64% → 57.72%, i.e. **+8bps better**. `1 − (−8 ÷ 129) ≈ 1.06` → **~106% recovered**.

*FY26 full year:*
- Licences 6.5% → 7.0% = +50bps; third-party development costs and royalties 3.0% → 6.6% = +360bps → gross increase **+410bps**
- Gross margin in the event: 54.3% → **57.2%** = **+290bps better**
- `1 − (−290 ÷ 410) ≈ 1.71` → **~171% recovered**. **But read the honest qualifier:** the single largest offset was game-intangible amortisation falling 14.4% → 9.9% of revenue (−450bps), which the filing attributes to "lower amortization of intangible assets primarily due to higher impairments in the prior year" — a consequence of the FY25 $3,545.2m goodwill impairment cycle, not an operating recovery. The *operating* offsets were product costs −160bps and internal royalties −90bps [`FY26 10-K, Item 7 (MD&A) — Cost of revenue and gross profit discussion`].

**What the paragraph actually says.** Take-Two has **no contractual pass-through** — there is no escalator clause anywhere in the pool, and the platforms hold the unilateral right to change the fee. But it has recovered most or all of its rising input costs anyway, and it has done so through **cost absorption rather than price pass-through**. The absorbing levers the filings name, with what each has done: (i) **direct-to-consumer commerce**, which bypasses the Apple/Google fee entirely and which management calls "a significant driver of revenue and margins" with "a material effect positive on our margins in the Mobile business" — real, but its share of revenue is **not disclosed**, so I cannot size it [`Q2 FY27 earnings call, prepared remarks`; `Q1 FY27 earnings call, Q&A (Zelnick)`]; (ii) **platform mix**, where mobile fell from 52.2% to 50.1% of FY26 net revenue and console rose from 37.3% to 39.0%, and product costs (where the app-store fee sits) fell 160bps in FY26 and a further 110bps in H1 FY27 — mobile "generally generates a lower gross margin percentage than our Console or PC revenue" because of the platform fee, so moving mix away from mobile mechanically lifts margin [`FY26 10-K, Item 7 (MD&A) — net revenue by platform; Online Content and Digital Distribution`; `Q2 FY27 10-Q, Item 2 (MD&A)`]; (iii) **lower internal royalties**, 7.2% → 6.3% of FY26 revenue and 5.1% → 4.8% in H1 FY27; (iv) **currency forwards**, which are balance-sheet only — $349.2m of contracts to sell foreign currency and $140.3m to buy at March 31, 2026, not designated as hedges, producing a $5.5m FY26 loss, and the filing's own limit: "Our hedging programs are designed to reduce, but do not entirely eliminate, the effect of currency exchange rate movements" [`FY26 10-K, Item 7A — Balance Sheet Hedging Activities`]. The timing and lag: there is no lag to measure because there is no price mechanism — the recovery shows up in the same period the cost rises, because it comes from the cost side.

Two cautions attach. First, **cost absorption can run out in a way a price clause would not**: the FY26 recovery leaned on an impairment-driven amortisation drop that will not repeat, and the CFO has already guided FY27 gross margin slightly lower — "it's due to the mix of the business, and it's being offset by some changes in the operating expenses" [`Q2 FY27 earnings call, Nov 9 2026, Q&A (Goldstein)`]. Second, the company's one clear price action — the $80 GTA VI base price — is a **price rise on a new product, not a recovery of a cost increase**, and management explicitly disavows cost-based pricing: "Our goal is not to maximize price" [`Q1 FY27 earnings call, Aug 7 2026, Q&A (Zelnick)`]. Do not restate this section as "no pass-through". The accurate short form is: **no contractual pass-through; 56%–106% realised recovery in the latest half, achieved on the cost side.**

**Supplier / input concentration (quantify).** **No supplier's share of cost of revenue or of purchases is disclosed anywhere in the pool** — not for the largest supplier, not for the top three, and not for any platform. That is a real gap and I do not invent a percentage. What *is* quantified is the shape of the input bill: FY26 cost of revenue was $2,846.7m, 42.8% of net revenue, made of product costs $863.8m (13.0%), game intangibles $662.2m (9.9%), licences $463.5m (7.0%), third-party software development costs and royalties $439.8m (6.6%) and internal royalties $417.4m (6.3%) [`FY26 10-K, Item 7 (MD&A) — Cost of revenue table`]. Contractually locked minimums are small: $740.1m in total across all future years, of which $323.7m falls in FY27 — 4.9% of FY26 net revenue — split software development and licensing $175.8m, marketing $206.9m (which "primarily reflect our agreements with major sports leagues and players' associations") and purchase obligations $357.4m [`FY26 10-K, Note 14 (Commitments and Contingencies), p.92`].

The material supply-side dependency here is **permission, not materials**, and there are four single-source inputs with no alternative:

1. **The Sony PlayStation publisher licence** — sole source of the right to publish on PlayStation, expiring March 31, 2027 with one-year automatic renewals, terminable by Sony "for any or no reason upon 30 days' notice" [`FY26 10-K, Item 1 — Arrangements with Platform Manufacturers, p.3`]. **Material** — the console channel it is half of was 39.0% of FY26 net revenue.
2. **The two Microsoft Xbox publisher licences** — same function, both expiring March 31, 2027 [`FY26 10-K, Item 1 — Arrangements with Platform Manufacturers, p.3`]. **Material**, same channel.
3. **Apple's and Google's payment rails** — "most of the virtual currency we sell is purchased using these platform providers' payment processing systems," on terms of service the platform writes and reinterprets at its "sole discretion," covering 91.0% of mobile revenue where mobile is 50.1% of the group [`FY26 10-K, Item 1A — third-party mobile and web platforms`; `FY26 10-K, Item 7 (MD&A)`]. **Material** — roughly 46% of group revenue reaches the company through two app stores (*my arithmetic, 0.910 × 0.501; not a disclosed figure*).
4. **Sports-league and players'-association licences** for the 2K label, with the National Basketball Association named as a licensor in the vendor graph. No term, renewal date, or per-licence cost is disclosed in the pool. **Material at 7.0% of revenue and rising 26.7% year on year**, with the filing warning that losing or failing to renew them means "our revenue and profitability could decline significantly" [`FY26 10-K, Item 1A — ability to acquire and maintain licenses`].

One further supplier-side dependency the filing itself raises, routed on rather than judged here: the Executive Chairman/CEO and the President are **partners of ZMC Advisors, L.P.**, and the 10-K lists ZMC alongside its own executive officers as a loss that "could significantly harm our business," with a management agreement with ZelnickMedia Corporation in the exhibit index [`FY26 10-K, Item 1A — we depend on our key management and product development personnel`; `FY26 10-K, Exhibit Index`]. That is a related-party observation for `capital-allocation-governance` (CLAUDE.md §13), not a value-chain conclusion. Note also that the DEF 14A export in this pool is an empty shell, so the proxy-level detail on that arrangement is not readable here [`Take-Two_Interactive_Software_Inc_-_Form_DEF_14A-Jul-27-2026__No-Data` — empty export].

---

## 2A. Named Counterparties

**Scope, quoted first:** the export covers *"Recently disclosed suppliers only (within the last two years)"* and *"Recently disclosed customers only (within the last two years)"*, in both cases *"Include Suppliers/Customers for: Current subsidiaries"* [`CIQ Suppliers / Customers export (TTWO), retrieved 2026-11-19 — vendor export`]. It is not the full supplier or customer base, an absent counterparty proves nothing, and it never states the size of any relationship.

**Supplier side — the input limb this agent owns.** Only `third_party` rows appear; strongest link first (strongest = disclosed by the counterparty's own filing, or named in Take-Two's own 10-K).

| Counterparty | Listing | What it supplies / buys | Which group entity | Who disclosed it | Why it matters here |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| National Basketball Association, Inc. | *unlisted* | Movies and Entertainment — licensor of the NBA rights behind NBA 2K | Take-Two Interactive Software, Inc. | Take-Two's own filing (`TTWO 2025 Form 10-K`) | Puts a name on the licence line that is 7.0% of revenue and rising 26.7%; NBA 2K was the single largest FY26 revenue driver (+$416.9m), and the licensor holds a fixed term the pool does not disclose |
| Sony Interactive Entertainment (licensor) | — | Licensor of the right to publish on PlayStation | Take-Two Interactive Software, Inc. | Take-Two's own filing (`TTWO 2026 Form 10-K`) | See the note below — the graph flags this node `likely_group`, so it is deliberately excluded from the counts in this section |
| Microsoft Corporation | NASDAQGS:MSFT | Systems Software — licensor (and, separately, a distributor on the customer side) | Take-Two Interactive Software, Inc. | Neither side in the export — the row's only basis is a `Key Developments / Client Announcements` note dated Sep-30-2026 | The weakest-evidenced row in the graph; Microsoft's licensor role is nonetheless proven independently by the 10-K's own Xbox Publisher Licence Agreements [`FY26 10-K, Item 1 — Arrangements with Platform Manufacturers, p.3`], so cite the filing, not the export |
| Technicolor Creative Studios | *unlisted* | Movies and Entertainment — production/creative services supplier | 2K Sports Inc. **and** Rockstar Games, Inc. (two separate rows) | **The counterparty** (`Technicolor Creative Studios 2022 Form Doc`) | The only outside supplier named into two different Take-Two studios — a shared production dependency, size not disclosed |
| ironSource Ltd. | *unlisted in export* | Systems Software — mobile ad mediation / user-acquisition technology | Zynga Inc. | **The counterparty** (`ironSource Ltd. 2022 Form 20-F`) | Sits directly under the in-game advertising line and the player-acquisition spend inside the $1,770.8m FY26 selling-and-marketing line |
| Remedy Entertainment Oyj | HLSE:REMEDY | Interactive Home Entertainment — licensor to Rockstar | Rockstar Games, Inc. | **The counterparty** (`Remedy Entertainment Oyj 2026 Form`) | An outside studio named a Rockstar relationship material enough to disclose in its own filing |
| Gaming Realms plc | AIM:GMR | Interactive Home Entertainment — licensor to Zynga | Zynga Inc. | **The counterparty** (`Gaming Realms plc Form`) | Licensed game mechanics into the mobile portfolio; immaterial in size as far as the pool shows |
| Bidstack Group Plc | *unlisted in export* | Advertising — in-game advertising supply technology | Nordeus Limited **and** Take-Two Interactive Software, Inc. | **The counterparty** (`Bidstack Group Plc Form`) | Second ad-tech supplier named into the group; the in-game advertising limb depends on outside fill |
| Punktid Technologies AS | *unlisted* | Interactive Media and Services — supplier to Rockstar | Rockstar Games, Inc. | **The counterparty** (`Punktid Technologies AS Form`) | Smallest named supplier row; nothing in the pool sizes it |
| ZMC Advisors, L.P. | *unlisted* | Asset Management and Custody Banks — management services vendor | Take-Two Interactive Software, Inc. | Take-Two's own filing (`TTWO 2026 Form 10-K`) | The CEO and President are partners of ZMC; a supplier-side related-party line to route to `capital-allocation-governance` |
| ZelnickMedia Corporation | *unlisted* | Asset Management and Custody Banks — management services vendor | Take-Two Interactive Software, Inc. | Take-Two's own filing (`TTWO 2025 Form 10-K`) | Same arrangement, earlier filing; the exhibit index carries the management agreements |

**Customer side — not re-tabled here.** `05_customer-geography.md` already tables the ten outside customer-side counterparties (Microsoft, Apple, Google LLC, Sony Group Corporation, Nintendo, Valve, Meta, Snap, Alliance Entertainment Holding Corporation, Thunderful Group AB). I adopt that table rather than duplicate it.

**Three `likely_group` nodes are excluded from every count in this section**: *Sony Interactive Entertainment Inc.*, *Epic Games, Inc.* and *Prima Interactive (Pty) Ltd*. The Sony Interactive flag is a brand-matching artefact of the vendor parser, not real group membership — the FY26 10-K's own exhibit index and platform-arrangements section treat Sony Interactive Entertainment entities as outside counterparties, and the filing beats the vendor export (CLAUDE.md §4). Epic Games and Prima Interactive stay treated as related until a filing says otherwise.

- **Concentration of disclosure.** 28 relationship rows name 28 entities: 19 outside parties, of which 9 are listed, across 6 markets (NASDAQGS, NASDAQCM, NYSE, TSE, HLSE, AIM); 2 listed on the supplier side and 7 on the customer side. **Intra-group rows: 0 (0.0% intragroup row share)** — so there is no related-party trading pattern to route onward from this export, and essentially everything shown here is arm's-length. The six `group` nodes (2K Games, 2K Sports, Nordeus, Rockstar Games, Take 2 Interactive Software Pty Ltd, Zynga) appear only as Take-Two's own side of a relationship.
- **Where the spend clusters.** Three supplier-side industry clusters carry two or more outside parties: **Movies and Entertainment** (National Basketball Association + Technicolor Creative Studios) — licensed sports IP plus the production work to build it; **Systems Software** (Microsoft + ironSource) — console permission plus mobile ad technology; and **Asset Management and Custody Banks** (ZMC Advisors + ZelnickMedia) — the management-services spend, which is a related-party cluster rather than an operating one. A fourth, **Interactive Home Entertainment**, carries four outside parties (Nintendo, Gaming Realms, Remedy, Valve) spanning both sides of the chain at once — the same industry supplies it, distributes for it, and buys from it.
- **The honest limit.** The export names relationships; it never says what share of either side's business they represent. No percentage in this report comes from it.

---

## 3. Customer Pricing Power

Take-Two can raise prices, and in the last 24 months it did so once, deliberately and above the industry norm — and then chose not to on the title where it could have. It set **Grand Theft Auto VI's base price at $80** against the long-standing $70 AAA price point (a ~14% rise), while leaving **NBA 2K27's base price at $70** [`Q1 FY27 earnings call, Aug 7 2026, Q&A (Pitz / Zelnick)`]. Management's stated reason is not weakness but choice: "the real cost of a AAA video game is a whole lot lower today than it was 20 years ago. Pricing has not kept pace with inflation… Our goal is not to maximize price" [same source]. It also raises *realised* price without touching the headline, through edition laddering — the premium above the $80 standard edition for GTA VI's Ultimate Edition is booked into recurrent consumer spending, as the CFO confirmed: "the add-on content can be also purchased separately, so it's allocated to RCS" [`Q2 FY27 earnings call, Nov 9 2026, Q&A (Handler / Goldstein)`]. **The volume reaction to the $80 price is not yet measurable** — GTA VI released today, November 19, 2026, and the only read available is a qualitative one management refuses to quantify: pre-orders are "unprecedented and extraordinary" and "tracking far in excess of our initial expectations," with Zelnick declining to put them into guidance because "we're days away from the launch… we tend to avoid claiming success before it occurs" [`Q2 FY27 earnings call, Nov 9 2026, prepared remarks and Q&A`]. Two facts cut the other way and belong in the same paragraph. First, the company's standing practice is to **cut** price, not hold it: it stimulates sales by "reducing the wholesale prices of our products to retailers, digital storefronts, and platform providers," with price protection "typically… three to nine months after a product's initial launch," plus "volume rebates" in certain international markets [`FY26 10-K, Item 1 — Marketing, p.4`]. Second, the in-game spending limb — 81% of Q2 FY27 Net Bookings — went backwards at unchanged prices: recurrent consumer spending Net Bookings fell 9% and GAAP recurrent consumer spending fell 5% in the September 2026 quarter [`Q2 FY27 earnings release, Nov 9 2026, p.1`]. Read together: **pricing power on the headline price of a new premium release is evidenced and partly unused; pricing power to hold up spend between releases is not evidenced.**

---

## 4. Economic Control Verdict

**Mixed.**

The company controls price and content but not terms or access. On the strong limb: it owns the IP behind 54.3% of FY26 revenue in five franchises and needs no licensor's consent to make it [`FY26 10-K, Item 1A — dependence on key products`; `FY26 10-K, Item 1 — Intellectual Property`]; it priced GTA VI at $80 above the industry's $70 and held NBA 2K27 at $70 by choice [`Q1 FY27 earnings call, Aug 7 2026, Q&A`]; and it recovered 56% as reported — ~106% ex-stock-compensation — of a 250bps input-cost rise in the latest reported half without any escalator clause [`Q2 FY27 10-Q, Item 2 (MD&A)`]. On the weak limb: four counterparties that are each above 10.0% of its revenue, together inside a top five that is 80.6% of it, also own the only doors to the player, set the fee at their "sole discretion," and in Sony's case can terminate "for any or no reason upon 30 days' notice" [`FY26 10-K, Item 1 — Sales, p.4`; `FY26 10-K, Item 1 — Arrangements with Platform Manufacturers, p.3`]; the company itself concedes those partners are gaining ground — "Increased competition for digital 'shelf space' has put channel partners in more favorable bargaining positions" [`FY26 10-K, Item 1A — limited number of channel partners`]. It is not **Squeezed**, because it is demonstrably not a price-taker on outputs and its measured recovery on inputs is well above zero. It does not **Control economics**, because the fee schedule on roughly 46% of its revenue and the licence on another 39.0% are written by someone else.

---

## 5. The Single Biggest Bargaining Risk

The **Sony PlayStation Global Developer and Publisher Agreement** — a single-source permission covering part of the 39.0% of FY26 revenue earned on consoles, held by a counterparty that is itself above 10.0% of net revenue, that publishes competing games, that expires March 31, 2027, and that may terminate "for any or no reason upon 30 days' notice" [`FY26 10-K, Item 1 — Arrangements with Platform Manufacturers, p.3`; `FY26 10-K, Item 1A, Item 7 (MD&A)`]. (The Apple/Google fee limb is the larger-dollar exposure at roughly 46% of revenue, but it degrades by degrees rather than by cancellation.)
