## business-model / 07_business-quality.md

_Source: `07_business-quality.md`_

# Business Quality — TTWO

**Regime:** US / SEC domestic filer (NasdaqGS:TTWO, Delaware), US GAAP, reporting currency USD, fiscal year ends **March 31** — "FY2026" = year ended March 31, 2026 [`00_data-triage.md §2A`; `FY26 10-K, cover page`]. All dollar figures are USD millions as the filings state them. No upstream input was missing: `02_business-identity.md`, `03_segment-map.md` and `05_customer-geography.md` were all read, plus `00_data-triage.md`, `10_external-dependency.md` for cross-checks.

**Sector overlay (step 2b):** `02_business-identity.md §3a` classifies TTWO as a *hit-driven owner-operator of entertainment intellectual property — a premium console/PC franchise publisher bolted onto a free-to-play mobile microtransaction business, selling almost entirely through four platform gatekeepers.* **No sector overlay for hit-driven owner-operator of entertainment IP — generic 11-factor scoring applies.** No row in `frameworks/SECTOR_OVERLAYS.md` matches: it is not SaaS/subscription (recurrent consumer spending is per-item microtransaction and in-game advertising, not a contracted fee, so there is no ARR / RPO / net-retention grammar to score "recurring revenue" on), not a bank, REIT, insurer, miner, oil & gas, retailer, telecom, asset manager or pharma. The overlay's **Generic operating company** row governs. One consequence is carried forward: the industry's own de-facto KPI grammar — MAU / DAU, paying-user count, ARPDAU, retention — is **entirely absent from the pool** [`02_business-identity.md §3a`], which hard-caps the recurring-revenue and customer-stickiness rows below.

**Two pool items excluded by triage instruction and not cited as TTWO evidence anywhere below:** the DEF 14A export (July 27, 2026) is an empty "no tabular data" shell, and `Alphabet-Inc-NasdaqGS-GOOGL-Key-Developments` is a misfiled export for a different issuer [`00_data-triage.md §1, §3`].

**One vendor-vs-filing reconciliation, flagged not overridden:** `ciq_facts.json → margin_trend` reports gross margin of 58% for both FY2025 and FY2026 on a reclassified ("(R)") basis. The FY26 10-K's own figures are **57.2% (FY26) and 54.3% (FY25)** [`FY26 10-K, MD&A, p.43 and gross-profit discussion`]. The ~290bp FY25 gap is a vendor reclassification, not a misread of the filing; the audited filing wins and is used throughout (§4, §5). The vendor's ten-year series is used only for the long sweep, labelled as the tier-5 export it is.

---

## 1. Quality Factor Table

Direction is stated in each row label. Six rows are **reverse-mapped**: low intensity / low dependence / low cyclicality / low rate-of-change earns a HIGH score. Bands per `CLAUDE.md` §12: 0–20 very weak · 21–40 weak · 41–60 mixed/average · 61–80 strong · 81–100 very strong.

| Quality Factor | Score /100 | Evidence | Comment |
|---|---:|---|---|
| **Pricing power** *(higher = better)* | **58** | Grand Theft Auto VI base edition priced at **$80** against the industry's standard **$70** AAA price point, with a higher-priced Deluxe Edition alongside it; NBA 2K27 base pricing **held at $70** [`Q1 FY27 earnings call transcript, Aug 7 2026, Q&A (Pitz / Zelnick)`]. Against that, the CEO states the policy plainly: *"Our goal is not to maximize price"* and *"Pricing has not kept pace with inflation"* [`Q1 FY27 transcript, Aug 7 2026, Q&A (Zelnick)`]. The company also discounts by design — *"reducing the wholesale prices of our products… Price protection may occur at any time in a product's life cycle but typically occurs three to nine months after a product's initial launch"*, plus volume rebates in certain international markets [`FY26 10-K, Item 1 — Marketing, p.5`]. A geo-pricing tool built in-house flexes price by market ability to pay [`Q1 FY27 transcript, Aug 7 2026, Q&A (Zelnick)`]. The filing itself warns competitors using AI may reduce *"our ability to charge the same prices we have historically charged for our products"* [`FY26 10-K, Item 1A — Our industry is highly competitive, p.11`] | The capability is **proven** — a 14% price rise taken on the single biggest launch in the industry, and it set the price of every unit of virtual currency it sells. The limits are equally proven: the company deliberately declines to use it, it cuts wholesale price on a 3-to-9-month clock, and it does not set the platform fee (Apple and Google *"have broad discretion"*) [`FY26 10-K, Item 1A, p.11`]. Real brand pricing power, self-restrained and partly surrendered to the gatekeeper |
| **Repeat / recurring revenue** *(higher = better)* | **55** | Recurrent consumer spending (virtual currency, add-on content, in-game purchases, in-game advertising) was **$5,196.6m = 78.1% of FY26 net revenue**, and rose to **82.5%** of the six months to Sep 30, 2026 ($2,500.0m of $3,030.9m) [`FY26 10-K, MD&A, p.44`; `Q2 FY27 10-Q, MD&A, p.24`]. Longevity evidence: *"we're 13 years after GTA Online was launched and people are still highly engaged and we have record-setting engagement at times"* [`Q1 FY27 transcript, Aug 7 2026, Q&A (Zelnick)`]. **But it is not contracted and it fell:** RCS was **−9%** in Q2 FY27 against guidance of −5% [`Q2 FY27 investor deck, Nov 9 2026, slide 4`], and FY27 guidance implies RCS dropping to roughly **65% of net bookings** from 78% [`Q2 FY27 investor deck, slides 5 and 8`]. No MAU/DAU, paying-user count, ARPDAU or retention figure exists anywhere in the pool [`02_business-identity.md §3a`] | Large and repeat by behaviour, **not recurring by contract** — every dollar is an optional purchase a player can stop making at will, and the 10-K says reduced consumer spending *"has"* already cut demand [`FY26 10-K, Item 1A — Declines in consumer spending`]. The 13-year GTA Online record is genuine evidence of repeat behaviour; its *durability* is **"Not proven from available data"** because no retention metric is disclosed. Note also that "recurrent consumer spending" is a company-defined measure that **pays management** through performance RSUs [`FY26 10-K, Exhibit — form of performance-based RSU award agreement`] — a metric to verify, not accept |
| **Customer stickiness** *(higher = better)* | **45** | Two different customers, both weak contractually. **Accounting customers (the gatekeepers):** five customers = **80.6% of FY26 net revenue** (81.0% FY25, 79.8% FY24), Apple / Sony / Google / Microsoft each above 10.0%, sold *"primarily without long-term agreements or other commitments, and our customers may terminate their relationship with us at any time"*; Sony *"may terminate the agreement for any or no reason upon 30 days' notice"*, and the Sony plus both Microsoft console licences all expire **March 31, 2027** [`FY26 10-K, Note 1 (Concentration of Credit Risk), p.68`; `FY26 10-K, Item 1A, p.23`; `FY26 10-K, Item 1 — Arrangements with Platform Manufacturers, pp.3–4`]. **Players:** Grand Theft Auto series >465m units lifetime, GTA V >225m, Red Dead Redemption 2 >80m, Zynga portfolio >10bn downloads [`FY26 10-K, Item 1, pp.1–6`]. Churn is visible on the mobile half — mobile revenue fell **$90.5m year on year** in the Sep-2026 quarter to $731.1m [`Q2 FY27 10-Q, MD&A, p.24`] | The player-side stickiness on the Rockstar franchises is real but **unmeasurable from this pool** — zero retention or engagement metrics. The customer-side stickiness is close to nil on paper: four-fifths of revenue runs through five counterparties that can walk away at will, and three of them (Sony, Microsoft, and Apple/Google as publishers) also publish competing games, which the filing flags — *"such customers may give priority to their own products or those of our competitors"* [`FY26 10-K, Item 1A, p.23`]. Scored at the middle of the band because the practical lock is mutual (a platform wants Grand Theft Auto) while the contractual lock is one-sided |
| **Margin stability** *(higher = better)* | **30** | **Gross margin is stable; everything below it is not.** Gross margin 57.2% FY26 vs 54.3% FY25 [`FY26 10-K, MD&A, p.43`], and the ten-year vendor series reads 44→50→43→50→56→58→53→53→58→58%, 58% LTM Sep-2026 [`ciq_facts.json → margin_trend` (`CIQ Financials→Income Statement`) — tier-5 vendor export, reclassified basis]. Below gross profit: **operating margin was negative in each of the last three audited years** — (1.5)% FY26, (78.0)% FY25, (67.1)% FY24 — with net losses of $(298.2)m FY26 [`FY26 10-K, MD&A, p.43`]. EBITDA margin swung 8%→22%→7%→12% and absolute EBITDA $757m (FY2021) → $380m (FY2025) → $788m (FY2026) → $725m LTM [`ciq_facts.json → margin_trend, multi_year_trajectory` — vendor export]. **ROIC has been negative five years running: −4.3%, −3.1%, −4.2%, −0.8% (FY2026), −1.0% LTM Sep-2026** [`ciq_facts.json → multi_year_trajectory` (`CIQ Financials→Ratios`) — vendor export] | **Adjudicating the number that disagrees, by name** (§3): gross margin genuinely points the other way — it is up 290bp year on year and has sat in a 53–58% band for four years. It does not overturn the Weak score, because the volatility lives entirely in the lines below it: development amortisation, the $1,770.8m selling-and-marketing line (26.6% of revenue) and $1,074.6m R&D (16.1%) absorb the whole gross profit [`FY26 10-K, MD&A, pp.43–44`]. An owner is not paid in gross margin. Three straight operating losses and five straight years of negative return on capital (what it earns on each $100 invested) is the opposite of margin stability |
| **Capital intensity** *(low intensity = high score)* | **42** | **Light in plant, heavy in content.** Fixed-asset capex was only **$162.8m in FY26 = 2.45% of net revenue** ($169.4m FY25, $141.7m FY24), with net fixed assets of $445.4m [`FY26 10-K, Consolidated Statements of Cash Flows`; `FY26 10-K, Note 21, p.102`]. The real capital is game development, and it runs through **operating** cash flow: software development costs and licences of **$(688.9)m FY26, $(691.6)m FY25, $(603.4)m FY24** [`FY26 10-K, Consolidated Statements of Cash Flows`]. Combined cash invested in content plus fixed assets was **$851.7m = 12.8% of FY26 revenue** (*my arithmetic from the filing's own figures: 688.9 + 162.8 ÷ 6,656.4*; FY25 $861.0m = 15.3%; FY24 $745.1m = 13.9%). The balance sheet carries **$2,346.3m** of capitalised software development costs and licences, of which **$2,149.7m relates to titles not yet released** [`FY26 10-K, Note 7, p.83`]. That capital is provably at risk: impairments of software development costs of **$77.5m (FY25)** and **$109.9m (FY24)** [`FY26 10-K, Note 7, p.83`] | Scored at the middle, not at the asset-light 70s, for three reasons the numbers show. First, the spend is real capital whatever line it sits on — 12.8–15.3% of revenue a year. Second, it is committed **years before any revenue**: $2,149.7m is sunk into titles nobody has bought yet. Third, it is the direct cause of operating cash flow being **negative in two of the last four years** (FY2024 $(16.1)m, FY2025 $(45.2)m, against $624.3m FY26 and $367.1m LTM Sep-2026) [`FY26 10-K, Consolidated Statements of Cash Flows`; `ciq_facts.json → ltm_ocf_m` — vendor export]. No inventory, no plant, no freight (97.0% digital delivery) keeps it out of the Weak band |
| **Competitive intensity** *(low intensity = high score)* | **25** | The company's own risk-factor heading: *"The interactive entertainment software industry is highly competitive."* Named competitors: **Electronic Arts, Embracer Group, Epic Games, Microsoft, Nintendo, Playrix, Playtika, Roblox, Savvy Games, Sony, Tencent, Ubisoft** [`FY26 10-K, Item 1 — Competition, p.6`; `FY26 10-K, Item 1A, p.11`]. Three of those are its own >10% customers. On barriers: *"As there are relatively low barriers to entry to develop a mobile or online game, we expect new game competitors to enter the market… We also compete or will compete with a vast number of small companies and individuals who are able to create and launch games… using relatively limited resources, including AI resources, and with relatively limited start-up time or expertise"* [`FY26 10-K, Item 1A, p.11`]. Sony, Microsoft and Nintendo *"ha[ve] the financial resources to withstand significant price competition and to implement extensive advertising"* [`FY26 10-K, Item 1 — Competition, p.6`]. It also competes for attention with *"other forms of entertainment and leisure activities"* [`FY26 10-K, Item 1A, p.12`] | Among the most intense competitive settings the engine scores: a hit-driven industry where *"a relatively small number of hit titles account for a large portion of total revenue"* [`FY26 10-K, Item 1A, p.12`], the gatekeepers are also the rivals, and the company states in writing that barriers to entry on half its revenue base (mobile, 50.1% [`FY26 10-K, MD&A, p.44`]) are **low**. The consequence shows in the cost line, exactly as the filing predicts — *"increasing player acquisition and retention costs"* against $1,770.8m of selling and marketing [`FY26 10-K, Item 1A, p.11`; `MD&A, p.43`] |
| **Industry rate-of-change / disruption risk** *(low rate-of-change = high score)* | **35** | **The disruption already happened and is measured in the accounts.** Goodwill impairments of **$3,545.2m (FY25)** and **$2,342.1m (FY24)** were taken against *one of two unnamed reporting units*, caused by *"a reduction in the forecasted performance of the reporting unit due to industry conditions and changes in our strategies for games within the reporting unit"* — leaving accumulated impairments of **$5,887.3m against gross goodwill of $6,949.2m, i.e. ~85% of goodwill ever recorded written off** [`FY26 10-K, Critical Accounting Policies — Goodwill`; `FY26 10-K, Note 9, pp.83–84`]. Forward-looking vectors: *"our internal development studios must anticipate and adapt to rapid technological changes"* [`FY26 10-K, Item 1A, p.12`]; on AI, *"we may face significant risks to our competitive position, financial performance, and long-term growth prospects"* [`FY26 10-K, Item 1A, p.12`]; console generation transitions make *"operating results… more volatile and difficult to predict"* on the 39.0–41.4% of revenue earned on console [`FY26 10-K, Item 1A, p.11`; `Q2 FY27 10-Q, MD&A, p.23`]; and the distribution layer is moving — PC went from *"1% to 2% of the overall sales"* at a console launch twenty years ago to *"40% or 50% in a similar situation"* today, with *"the world continu[ing] to move to open systems"* [`Q1 FY27 transcript, Aug 7 2026, Q&A (Zelnick)`]. Hit turnover inside twelve months is visible: Color Block Jam added **+$206.6m** to FY26 revenue and by FY27 guidance mobile is *"down"* as that title laps [`FY26 10-K, MD&A, p.44`; `Q2 FY27 investor deck, slide 8`] | **Split read, and the weaker half is half the revenue.** Premium console/PC AAA (49.9% of revenue) has a competitive set that has barely changed in twenty years and a 25-year-old flagship franchise — that end is slow-changing. Free-to-play mobile (**50.1%** of FY26 revenue) is the opposite: the filing says barriers to entry are *low*, hit lifecycles run in quarters, and the company has already written off $5,887.3m of acquired value because of *"industry conditions."* Weighting the two roughly 50/50 and adding the AI and open-systems vectors the company itself flags, this scores **Weak (≤40)**. **Filter 5 trips** — see Section 4 |
| **Regulatory dependence** *(low dependence = high score)* | **45** | TTWO needs no operating licence from a state and is not rate-regulated. But regulation points directly at the **78.1% of revenue** that is in-game monetisation. The 10-K names **thirteen of its own games** whose mechanics *"may be deemed as 'loot boxes'"*, including NBA 2K, WWE 2K and Zynga Poker. **Already in force:** Australia's Phase 2 Industry Codes (age assurance), Australia's and Brazil's *"more stringent regulations"* on simulated gambling and loot boxes, and the E.U. Consumer Protection Cooperation Network's *"Key Principles on In-game Virtual Currencies."* **Pending:** a U.K. government review, the E.U. Digital Fairness Act draft, and Spanish (Mar 2025) and Portuguese (Feb 2026) bills. The live enforcement channel is proven — the FTC's January 2025 action against another developer settled at a **$20m fine plus a ten-year compliance monitoring programme** [`FY26 10-K, Item 1A — loot boxes / virtual currencies`]. Separately, *"in order to operate in China, all games must have regulatory approval"* and enforcement *"remains uncertain"*, with no China revenue figure disclosed anywhere in the pool [`FY26 10-K, Item 1A — international operations`]. Content ratings (ESRB, PEGI in 35+ countries, IARC) gate where a title can be sold [`FY26 10-K, Item 1, p.5`] | Mixed, with a severe tail — consistent with `10_external-dependency.md`, which scored in-game monetisation regulation *"Mid today, with a High-severity tail."* Nothing in the pool quantifies a realised revenue or cost impact on TTWO from any of these measures, which is why this is not Weak; the exposed base being 78.1% of revenue, with live rules in two countries and bills in four more, is why it is not Strong. The China exposure is explicitly **not sized** — no China revenue figure exists in the pool |
| **Commodity dependence** *(low dependence = high score)* | **85** | The company's own market-risk section names **only two** exposures — interest rates and foreign exchange — and **no commodity** [`FY26 10-K, Item 7A, opening paragraph`]. **97.0% of FY26 net revenue came through digital online channels** (physical retail and other 3.0%, down from 3.6%), so there is no bill of materials, no freight rate and no commodity input in a download [`FY26 10-K, MD&A, p.44`]. The real input is developer time — 9,998 of 12,909 full-time staff work in development studios — which shows up as the $1,074.6m R&D line and capitalised software amortisation, not as a purchased material [`FY26 10-K, Item 1, p.1`; `FY26 10-K, MD&A, p.43`] | The one indirect channel is the console hardware its games run on: the 10-K ties console availability and pricing to *"an increase in tariffs or trade restrictions on component parts"* [`FY26 10-K, Item 1A, p.11`], and on rising chip costs the CEO says *"the rising cost of hardware is not a good thing… equally, we don't see it as a headwind either"* [`Q1 FY27 transcript, Aug 7 2026, Q&A (Zelnick)`]. That is two steps removed and unquantified anywhere in the pool. Not scored 95+ because that indirect console-cost channel is real and sits under 39.0–41.4% of revenue |
| **Cyclicality** *(low cyclicality = high score)* | **22** | **Two cycles stack on top of each other.** (1) *Release-slate cycle, the dominant one:* FY26 net revenue rose $1,022.8m (+18.2%) on five named franchise moves — NBA 2K +$416.9m, Borderlands +$210.3m, Color Block Jam +$206.6m, Toon Blast +$121.9m, Grand Theft Auto +$115.1m — while Q2 FY27 net bookings fell **18%** to $1,601.3m simply because the company sat between launches [`FY26 10-K, MD&A, p.44`; `Q2 FY27 earnings release, Nov 9 2026, p.1`]. Absolute EBITDA ran $757m (FY2021) → $380m (FY2025) → $788m (FY2026) → $725m LTM [`ciq_facts.json → margin_trend` — vendor export]. (2) *Consumer discretionary cycle:* *"Most of our products involve discretionary spending on the part of consumers… This makes our products particularly sensitive to general economic conditions and economic cycles,"* and *"Reduced consumer spending **has** and may in the future continue to result in reduced demand"* [`FY26 10-K, Item 1A — Declines in consumer spending`]. (3) *Third-party console hardware cycle* on 39.0–41.4% of revenue, where *"business and operating results may be more volatile and difficult to predict during console transitions"* [`FY26 10-K, Item 1A, p.11`] | Near the bottom of the Weak band, and this is structural rather than a defect to fix: the whole business is organised around when a small number of titles ship. **Adjudicating the number that disagrees, by name** (§3): management argues the swing is product, not economy — *"it's really not what we're seeing… we don't feel like the consumer is pulling back at all"* [`Q1 FY27 transcript, Aug 7 2026, Q&A (Creutz / Zelnick)`], and the Q2 FY27 RCS miss was attributed to NBA 2K content, not the consumer [`Q2 FY27 transcript, Nov 9 2026, prepared remarks (CFO)`]. That does not lift the score: it relabels the cycle from macro to release-slate, and the release-slate cycle is the larger of the two in the observed numbers. The filing wins on the structural point (§4) |
| **Disclosure quality** *(higher = better)* | **45** | **Clean where it is audited, deliberately thin where it matters most.** Strong: Ernst & Young, **unqualified** opinions for FY2026 and FY2025 (and across the 1996–2026 history in the vendor export), management concluded internal control over financial reporting *"was effective as of March 31, 2026"* with an unqualified ICFR opinion, and zero extraction failures across 30 sources and 62 tabs [`FY26 10-K, Report of Independent Registered Public Accounting Firm`; `FY26 10-K, Item 9A, p.—`; `CIQ Auditors export — vendor export`; `00_data-triage.md`]. Four independent revenue cuts (content, platform, channel, geography) each summing to $6,656.4m; concentration percentages given in Note 1; stock-based compensation itemised by expense line; a published FX sensitivity; title-level guidance assumptions and a dated release schedule [`FY26 10-K, MD&A, p.44`; `Note 1, p.68`; `Item 7A`; `Q2 FY27 investor deck, slides 8 and 13`]. Weak: **one reportable segment with no profit disclosure below the consolidated total** — the segment measure of profit *is* consolidated net income (loss) [`FY26 10-K, Note 21, p.102`]; the **two goodwill reporting units are never named** despite one absorbing $5,887.3m of impairment [`FY26 10-K, Note 9, pp.83–84`]; Rockstar / 2K / Zynga get no revenue or profit; **zero engagement metrics** in a business 50.1% mobile; player-acquisition cost qualitative only; direct-to-consumer share not quantified [`02_business-identity.md §3a`; `10_external-dependency.md §1`] | Mixed, and the gaps are not accidental — they sit exactly where an outsider would price the business. The headline operating metric, **Net Bookings**, is company-defined and non-GAAP, and the same metric family (recurrent consumer spending) **vests management's performance RSUs** [`FY26 10-K, Exhibit — form of performance-based RSU award agreement`; `Q2 FY27 earnings release, Nov 9 2026, footnote *`]. Note what is **not** counted against the company: the DEF 14A's absence is a *pool* gap (an empty vendor export), not a disclosure failure by TTWO, and all 73 extracts are English, so no language discount applies (§27) |

---

## 2. Aggregate Quality Score

# 38 / 100 — Weak

**Caps applied, stated explicitly:**
- **MODULE_RULES §24 Filter 5 cap:** the industry rate-of-change row scored **35 (≤40)**, so the business-quality aggregate is capped at **65** and the thesis is flagged as a sector / technology-cycle bet (Section 4).
- **Band anchor (F43):** the second-lowest row is competitive intensity at **25**, so the aggregate may not exceed **45**. This is the binding constraint — 38 sits inside it.

**How the weighting works for this business, with the weights named.** For a hit-driven owner of entertainment IP, the four factors that decide whether a holder actually earns anything across a full release cycle dominate, and they carry **57%** of the weight between them: cyclicality **18%** (×22), industry rate-of-change **15%** (×35), competitive intensity **12%** (×25), margin stability **12%** (×30). The two factors that make an IP owner attractive at all carry **24%**: repeat / recurring revenue **14%** (×55) and pricing power **10%** (×58). The remainder is secondary because it is either small or unmeasurable here: customer stickiness **8%** (×45), capital intensity **5%** (×42), disclosure quality **3%** (×45), regulatory dependence **2%** (×45), commodity dependence **1%** (×85). Weights sum to 100% and the weighted total is **38.1**, rounded to **38**. Commodity dependence is deliberately weighted at 1% — a download has no bill of materials, so scoring 85 there is true but tells an owner almost nothing, and letting it carry real weight would manufacture quality out of an irrelevance.

---

## 3. Strongest Factor & Weakest Factor

| | Factor | Score | Why |
|---|---|---:|---|
| **Strongest** | Commodity dependence *(low dependence = high score)* | **85** | Item 7A names only interest rates and FX, no commodity, and 97.0% of FY26 net revenue was delivered digitally — there is no purchased input to inflate [`FY26 10-K, Item 7A`; `MD&A, p.44`]. **Stated plainly: this is the strongest row by score but the least informative.** The strongest factor that an owner is actually *paid* for is **pricing power (58)** — a proven $80 price on GTA VI against the industry's $70, on a franchise that has sold >465m units [`Q1 FY27 transcript, Aug 7 2026, Q&A (Pitz / Zelnick)`; `FY26 10-K, Item 1, p.1`] |
| **Weakest** | Cyclicality *(low cyclicality = high score)* | **22** | Revenue and profit swing on *when* five titles ship, not on steady volume or price: +18.2% revenue in FY26 off five named franchises, then net bookings −18% in Q2 FY27 for sitting between launches, with absolute EBITDA moving $380m → $788m → $725m in consecutive periods [`FY26 10-K, MD&A, p.44`; `Q2 FY27 earnings release, Nov 9 2026, p.1`; `ciq_facts.json → margin_trend` — vendor export]. Competitive intensity (25) is a close second and shares the cause: *"relatively low barriers to entry"* on half the revenue base [`FY26 10-K, Item 1A, p.11`] |

---

## 4. Read

**This is a cyclical, hit-driven owner of entertainment intellectual property — not a durable compounder on the evidence in this pool.** It owns one genuinely scarce asset (a 25-year franchise with >465m lifetime units and a live-service tail still running 13 years after launch) bolted onto a free-to-play mobile business that is 50.1% of revenue and sits in a market whose own filing describes barriers to entry as *"relatively low"* [`FY26 10-K, Item 1, p.1`; `Q1 FY27 transcript, Aug 7 2026`; `FY26 10-K, Item 1A, p.11`; `MD&A, p.44`]. The industry rate-of-change row scored **35 — Weak**, so, per `CLAUDE.md` §24 Filter 5: **this thesis is closer to a sector / technology-cycle bet than to a durable compounder, conviction is capped, and moat durability must be discounted downstream.** The strongest proof is in the company's own accounts, not in a forecast — it has written off **$5,887.3m of accumulated goodwill impairment against $6,949.2m of gross goodwill (~85% of all goodwill ever recorded)** because of *"industry conditions and changes in our strategies for games within the reporting unit"* [`FY26 10-K, Note 9, pp.83–84`; `Critical Accounting Policies — Goodwill`].

RF-BQ-005 (fast-changing industry: rate-of-change ≤40)

**Anchoring the headline returns, and reconciling them with the Cyclicality row.** The usual trap runs the other way round here: TTWO's reported returns are at a cyclical **trough**, not a peak. ROIC (what it earns on each $100 of capital invested) has been **negative five years running — −4.3%, −3.1%, −4.2%, −0.8% (FY2026), −1.0% LTM to Sep-30-2026** — against a FY2021 peak of +13.4%, and LTM EBITDA of $724.9m is *below* the FY26 figure of $788m [`ciq_facts.json → multi_year_trajectory, ltm_ebitda_m` (`CIQ Financials→Ratios` / `→Income Statement`) — tier-5 vendor export]. The through-cycle anchor, computed across the ten years FY2017–FY2026, is an EBITDA margin of roughly **12%** (average EBITDA $471.8m on average revenue $3,919.8m — *my arithmetic from the vendor series*), which is almost exactly where FY26 (12%) and the LTM (11%) sit. So the low Cyclicality score (22) and today's weak returns are the **same fact**, not a contradiction: the business is between launches, and the audited record is three straight operating losses on a through-cycle margin near 12%. The forward trap is therefore symmetric and must be flagged for the moat and DCF layers: Grand Theft Auto VI launched **November 19, 2026**, and FY27–FY28 will print this cycle's **peak**. A peak-year ROIC or margin off that launch is not the through-cycle level any more than today's −1.0% is; the vendor workbook already shows the distortion, with TEV/LTM EBITDA at **52.2x** on a trough denominator the day before the launch [`ciq_facts.json → ev_ebitda_current_x, ltm_ebitda_m` — vendor export].

**The single quality factor a buyer should keep their eye on over the next 24 months: whether recurrent consumer spending on the new Grand Theft Auto base builds a second durable live-service annuity, or whether the business reverts to the FY2024–FY2026 pattern of negative return on capital between launches.** That is the one factor that would move this scorecard materially, because it drives three rows at once (repeat revenue 55, margin stability 30, cyclicality 22). The measurable tests already exist in the company's own reporting: RCS as a share of net bookings against the guided fall to ~65% in FY27 from 78% in FY26, and RCS growth against the −9% it printed in Q2 FY27 versus guidance of −5% [`Q2 FY27 investor deck, Nov 9 2026, slides 4, 5 and 8`]. Two near-dated items sit alongside it and belong to other agents' scorecards, noted here only because they land inside the same window: both console publisher licences — Sony's and both Microsoft agreements, covering the 39.0% of FY26 revenue earned on console — **expire March 31, 2027**, with Sony able to terminate *"for any or no reason upon 30 days' notice"* [`FY26 10-K, Item 1 — Arrangements with Platform Manufacturers, pp.3–4`; `Item 1A, p.11`]; and the loot-box and in-game-currency rulemaking in six jurisdictions points at the 78.1% of revenue that is in-game monetisation [`FY26 10-K, Item 1A — loot boxes / virtual currencies`].

**Not proven from available data:** player retention, MAU/DAU, paying-user count and ARPDAU (zero occurrences anywhere in the pool) — so the durability of the 78.1% recurrent-spending base is asserted by the company and unverifiable by an outsider; player-acquisition cost and mobile payback; the direct-to-consumer share of mobile revenue; profit or margin for mobile vs console vs PC, for RCS vs full game, or for Rockstar vs 2K vs Zynga; and the identity and size of either of the two goodwill reporting units.

## business-model / 08_competitive-map.md

_Source: `08_competitive-map.md`_

# Competitive Map — TTWO

**Upstream inputs read:** `analyses/TTWO_2026-11-19/business-model/02_business-identity.md`, `03_segment-map.md`, `00_data-triage.md`, `05_customer-geography.md`. Nothing missing.

**Regime:** US / SEC domestic filer, US GAAP, USD, fiscal year ends March 31. FY2026 = year ended March 31, 2026. All dollar figures in millions unless stated. Peer financial figures come from the pool's Capital IQ **Company Comparable Analysis** export (stated As-Of Date 2026-11-19) — a **tier-5 vendor export, not a filing**. Peer figures are the vendor's own normalised read of each peer's accounts, not that peer's audited filing, and each peer's "LTM" window ends on a different date (listed per peer below). Two pool limits carried from triage: the DEF 14A export is an empty shell, and `Alphabet-Inc-NasdaqGS-GOOGL-Key-Developments__Key-Developments.txt` is a misfiled export for a different issuer — neither was read as TTWO evidence.

---

## 1. Dominant Segment

One operating and reportable segment — "sale of software titles", 100% of $6,656.4 of FY26 net revenue — so the segment competitors are profiled against is the whole consolidated interactive-entertainment publishing business: a hit-driven premium console/PC franchise publisher (Rockstar Games, 2K) bolted onto a free-to-play mobile microtransaction business (Zynga), with 78.1% of FY26 revenue from recurrent consumer spending and 50.1% of it from mobile [`FY26 10-K, Note 21 (Segment Reporting and Geographic Information), p.102`; `FY26 10-K, Item 7 — net revenue by content and by platform`; upstream `03_segment-map.md §2`, `02_business-identity.md §3`].

---

## 2. Named Competitors

Take-Two names its competitors twice in its own FY26 10-K, in the same list both times: **Electronic Arts, Embracer Group, Epic Games, Playrix, Playtika, Roblox, Savvy Games, Tencent, and Ubisoft** as publisher rivals, plus **Sony, Microsoft, and Nintendo** "for the sale of interactive entertainment software", plus **Apple, Google, and Microsoft** as "high-profile companies with significant online presences with new and expanded mobile gaming offerings" [`FY26 10-K, Item 1 — Competition`; `FY26 10-K, Item 1A — "The interactive entertainment software industry is highly competitive"`]. Nothing below is invented: every name is in that list, and the three profiled names are also in the Capital IQ comp set, so they carry comparable numbers.

### Competitor A — Electronic Arts Inc.

- **Ticker / listing:** **No longer listed.** The vendor comp export carries no ticker, no day-close price, no shares outstanding, no market capitalisation and no trading multiples for Electronic Arts — every one of those cells is blank, while all nine other peers are populated [`CIQ Comps → Financial Data` and `→ Trading Multiples`, As-Of 2026-11-19 — vendor export]. The same export shows an S&P issuer credit rating of **BB-** (below investment grade, versus TTWO's BBB) [`CIQ Comps → Credit Health Panel`, As-Of 2026-11-19 — vendor export]. Reason, from outside the pool: EA was taken private on **August 4, 2026** in a ~$55bn buyout by Saudi Arabia's Public Investment Fund, Silver Lake and Affinity Partners, including ~$20bn of debt financing, and delisted from Nasdaq [`EA Form 8-K Ex.99.1, filed 2026-08-04 (SEC EDGAR, CIK 0000712515)`; `Web: thewrap.com / esportsadvocate.net, Aug 2026 — dated, unverified`]. The pool itself does not state why EA is unlisted — the delisting fact is corroborated by the blank vendor cells, the cause is web-sourced.
- **Where they compete:** The same dominant segment, head-to-head, worldwide and on the same three platforms (console, PC, mobile). The overlap is specific, not categorical: EA's licensed-sports franchises — EA SPORTS FC, Madden NFL, College Football, NHL, UFC, F1, **PGA TOUR** — sit directly against Take-Two's NBA 2K, WWE 2K and **PGA TOUR 2K**, the same licensed-sports-simulation model with the same annual-release cadence and the same third-party licence cost [`CIQ Comps → Business Description` (EA and TTWO rows), As-Of 2026-11-19 — vendor export; `FY26 10-K, Item 1 — General / Our Businesses`]. EA also runs live services and mobile, the two things that generate 78.1% of Take-Two's FY26 revenue.
- **Scale:** LTM total revenue **$7,846** against Take-Two's **$6,409.7** — the only named rival in the comp set larger than Take-Two on revenue apart from Nintendo. EA's LTM window ends **June 30, 2026** (income-statement filing date 2026-08-03), Take-Two's ends September 30, 2026, so the two windows do not line up [`CIQ Comps → Financial Data`; `CIQ Comps → Credit Health Panel` — vendor export].
- **Profitability / return on capital:** LTM gross margin **79.9%**, EBITDA margin **22.0%**, EBIT margin **17.9%**, net income margin **13.8%**; LTM revenue growth +5.0%, EBITDA growth −3.3%, EBIT growth −5.2%; total debt/capital 20.9%, total debt/EBITDA 1.0x [`CIQ Comps → Operating Statistics` and `→ Financial Data`, LTM to Jun-30-2026, As-Of 2026-11-19 — vendor export]. **ROIC / ROE: not in this pool.** The comp export has no return-on-capital or return-on-equity column for any peer, and EA no longer files, so no post-buyout equity base is observable. Caution for the `moat` agent: these margins are measured **before** the ~$20bn of buyout debt landed on EA, so EA's forward interest burden is not in the 13.8% net margin.
- **Source named in:** `FY26 10-K, Item 1 — Competition` (first name in the list); `FY26 10-K, Item 1A — industry competition risk factor`; `CIQ Comps → Business Description`, As-Of 2026-11-19 — vendor export.
- **One-line read:** The closest structural twin — a licensed-sports-plus-owned-IP publisher across console, PC and mobile — and it currently converts revenue to profit far better than Take-Two does (EBIT margin +17.9% against Take-Two's −1.8%), but it has just swapped a public balance sheet for the largest leveraged buyout on record, so its future pricing and spending behaviour is less predictable than its history.

### Competitor B — Roblox Corporation

- **Ticker / listing:** **NYSE:RBLX**, listed. Day close $47.33, 715.7m shares, market capitalisation $33,874.7, total enterprise value $32,562.7 [`CIQ Comps → Financial Data`, As-Of 2026-11-19 — vendor export].
- **Where they compete:** For exactly the revenue line Take-Two depends on most. Roblox runs a user-generated immersive platform and earns from in-experience spending, not from selling games — which is the same economics as Take-Two's recurrent consumer spending, 78.1% of FY26 revenue and 82.5% of the six months to September 30, 2026 [`CIQ Comps → Business Description` (Roblox row) — vendor export; `FY26 10-K, Item 7`; `Q2 FY27 10-Q, MD&A, p.24`]. Geographic overlap is "the United States and internationally", and Take-Two's own filing identifies the threat precisely: competitors "with relatively limited start-up time or expertise" whose "proliferation of titles in these open developer channels makes it difficult for us to differentiate ourselves… and to compete for players without substantially increasing our marketing expenses and development costs" [`FY26 10-K, Item 1A — low barriers to entry / open developer channels`].
- **Scale:** LTM total revenue **$5,897.6** (window ending September 30, 2026 — the same window as Take-Two's), i.e. 92% of Take-Two's $6,409.7. The closest peer in the whole comp set by revenue size. Net **cash** of $1,286 against Take-Two's net **debt** of $1,237.8 [`CIQ Comps → Financial Data`; `CIQ Comps → Credit Health Panel`, LTM period ending 2026-09-30 — vendor export].
- **Profitability / return on capital:** LTM gross margin **27.3%**, EBITDA margin **−13.0%**, EBIT margin **−17.4%**, net income margin **−16.5%**; LTM revenue growth **+32.1%** — the fastest in the comp set; LTM diluted EPS −$1.36; total debt/capital 101.4%, debt/EBITDA "NM" (EBITDA is negative); S&P issuer credit rating BBB−, credit-health ranks Overall 3 / Operational 4 / Solvency 4 / Liquidity 2 (1 = best) [`CIQ Comps → Operating Statistics`, `→ Financial Data`, `→ Credit Health Panel`, As-Of 2026-11-19 — vendor export]. **ROIC / ROE: not in this pool** (no return column in the peer export); both would be negative on the above figures, but no figure is published here and none is invented.
- **Source named in:** `FY26 10-K, Item 1 — Competition`; `FY26 10-K, Item 1A — industry competition risk factor`; `CIQ Comps → Business Description`, As-Of 2026-11-19 — vendor export.
- **One-line read:** Take-Two's nearest rival by size, growing ten times faster, and losing money at every line — it is competing for the same player hours and the same in-game wallet while charging nothing for access, which is a pricing problem for Take-Two's premium model rather than a margin benchmark.

### Competitor C — Playtika Holding Corp.

- **Ticker / listing:** **NasdaqGS:PLTK**, listed. Day close $2.57, 382.7m shares, market capitalisation $983.5, total enterprise value $2,882. Note the vendor export records Playtika as operating "as a subsidiary of Playtika Holding UK II Limited" — it is a controlled listed company, not a free-floating one [`CIQ Comps → Financial Data` and `→ Business Description`, As-Of 2026-11-19 — vendor export].
- **Where they compete:** Directly against Zynga, in mobile free-to-play. Playtika "develops mobile games in the United States, Europe, the Middle East, Africa, the Asia Pacific, and internationally" with "a portfolio of casual and social casino-themed games" distributed "through various web and mobile platforms and direct-to-consumer platforms" [`CIQ Comps → Business Description` — vendor export]. That is the same genre set and the same channel as Take-Two's Zynga titles (Toon Blast, Toy Blast, Match Factory!, Color Block Jam, Zynga Poker, Wizard of Oz Slots Casino, Hit It Rich!) [`FY26 10-K, Item 1 — Our Businesses`; `CIQ Comps → Business Description` (TTWO row)]. This matters out of proportion to Playtika's size: mobile was **50.1% of Take-Two's FY26 revenue** and is the platform management says carries the lower gross margin [`FY26 10-K, Item 7 — net revenue by platform`; `Q2 FY27 10-Q, MD&A, p.23`].
- **Scale:** LTM total revenue **$2,828.4**, window ending September 30, 2026 — 44% of Take-Two's consolidated revenue, and 85% of Take-Two's $3,333.0 of FY26 mobile revenue. Smaller than Take-Two overall but well inside 5x, and roughly the same size as the part of Take-Two it actually competes with [`CIQ Comps → Financial Data`; `CIQ Comps → Credit Health Panel` — vendor export; `FY26 10-K, Item 7`].
- **Profitability / return on capital:** LTM gross margin **73.6%**, EBITDA margin **24.5%**, EBIT margin **18.9%**, net income margin **−9.8%**; LTM revenue growth +3.7%, EBITDA growth +13.8%, EBIT growth +30.4%; LTM diluted EPS −$0.74; total debt/EBITDA 3.5x and total debt/capital **116.7%** (shareholders' equity is negative, so the ratio exceeds 100%); S&P issuer credit rating BB−, credit-health ranks Overall 4 / Operational 2 / Solvency 4 / Liquidity 4 [`CIQ Comps → Operating Statistics`, `→ Financial Data`, `→ Credit Health Panel`, As-Of 2026-11-19 — vendor export]. **ROIC / ROE: not in this pool**, and in Playtika's case return on equity would be uninterpretable anyway because the equity base is negative — say so rather than print a number.
- **Source named in:** `FY26 10-K, Item 1 — Competition`; `FY26 10-K, Item 1A — industry competition risk factor`; `CIQ Comps → Business Description`, As-Of 2026-11-19 — vendor export.
- **One-line read:** A pure mobile free-to-play operator that turns 73.6% gross margin into 18.9% at the operating line on a smaller revenue base — evidence that the mobile model Take-Two bought with Zynga can earn an operating margin, which makes Take-Two's consolidated −1.8% EBIT margin a Take-Two problem rather than a mobile-industry problem.

Tencent is the awkward one — the same FY26 10-K names it as a competitor *and* as the partner through which Take-Two sells NBA 2K Online in China, its "most established market" in Asia [`FY26 10-K, Item 1 — Competition` and `Item 1 — Identify and Lead New Paradigms and Market Trends`; corroborated in `CIQ Strategic Alliances export (TTWO), retrieved 2026-11-19 — vendor export`, which lists Tencent Holdings as a strategic alliance sourced to "Take-Two… 2026 Form 10-K"]. Scope note for that export, quoted as required: "Recently disclosed strategic alliances only (within the last two years)… Include Strategic Alliances for: Current subsidiaries" — it proves the alliance exists, never its size.

**What the pool does not carry, for the `moat` agent specifically:** there is **no ROIC, ROCE or ROE column for any peer** anywhere in this pool. Take-Two's own return on capital is available from the vendor ratio series — **−0.8% in FY26 and −1.0% on the LTM to September 30, 2026**, against +13.4% in FY2021 [`ciq_facts.json → multi_year_trajectory (CIQ Financials → Ratios, "Return on Capital %")`; same series in `CIQ Financials_Annual → Ratios`, retrieved 2026-11-19 — vendor export]. So a "Take-Two earns more than its peers on capital" claim **cannot be evidenced from this pool at all**, in either direction, and must not be made. A margin comparison **can** be made, and on the numbers above it runs against Take-Two: its −1.8% LTM EBIT margin is the fourth-lowest of eleven and sits 17.7 percentage points below the comp-set median of +15.9%.

---

## 3. Competitive Position

**Losing share — on the only measurable proxy in the pool, and the proxy is cycle-distorted.** No market-share figure exists anywhere in this pool: the FY26 10-K never states a share of market, and the only rank claim management makes is unquantified — "Our NBA 2K Online business is the top online PC sports game in China" [`FY26 10-K, Item 1 — Identify and Lead New Paradigms and Market Trends`]. The usable proxy is revenue growth against the same comp set over a broadly matched window: Take-Two grew LTM revenue **+3.1%** against a ten-peer **median of +14.6%**, with Roblox +32.1%, Nintendo +25.2%, Konami +24.8% and NEXON +24.1% all growing far faster [`CIQ Comps → Operating Statistics`, As-Of 2026-11-19 — vendor export]. Take-Two's own latest reported period points the same way: Q2 FY27 net bookings fell **18%** year on year to $1,601.3, recurrent consumer spending fell **9%** against guidance of −5%, and mobile revenue fell **$90.5** year on year to $731.1 [`Q2 FY27 earnings release (November 9, 2026), p.1`; `Q2 FY27 investor deck (November 9, 2026), p.4`; `Q2 FY27 10-Q, MD&A, p.24`].

**Now the numbers that disagree, named.** Two figures in this same engine's data point the other way and are not overturned, only explained. First, FY26 revenue grew **+18.2%** ($1,022.8 higher, to $6,656.4) — faster than the peer median growth above [`FY26 10-K, Item 7`]. Second, FY27 guidance of **$8,000–8,200** net bookings against FY26 net bookings of $6,721 implies **+19% to +22%** growth [`Q2 FY27 investor deck, pp.5 and 8`]. Both are consistent with the proxy rather than contradicting it, because the cause is release timing, not competitive loss: the LTM window to September 30, 2026 is the pre-launch trough between Borderlands 4 (September 2025) and Grand Theft Auto VI, which released **today, November 19, 2026** [`Q2 FY27 10-Q`; `Q2 FY27 investor deck, p.13`]. Two honest limits on the read: the peer LTM windows end on different dates (Take-Two and Roblox September 30, 2026; EA June 30, 2026; Ubisoft March 31, 2026), and revenue growth is a share proxy only if the market itself grew — which this pool does not measure. **Verdict: Losing share on the latest twelve-month window; the read is a one-metric proxy inside a stated launch trough, is not confirmed, and is not durable evidence of competitive decline.**

---

## 4. Competitive Shape

**Fragmented in development and publishing, gated by a four-firm distribution oligopoly — with no HHI or top-N share data in this pool to size either half.** The fragmented half is Take-Two's own description: it competes with firms "that range in size and cost structure from very small with limited resources to very large with greater financial, marketing, technical, and other resources than ours", names twelve of them, and expects more — "As there are relatively low barriers to entry to develop a mobile or online game, we expect new game competitors to enter the market", including "a vast number of small companies and individuals who are able to create and launch games… with relatively limited start-up time or expertise" [`FY26 10-K, Item 1 — Competition`; `FY26 10-K, Item 1A — low barriers to entry`]. The comp set itself spans $38.6 to $13,684.5 of LTM revenue across six countries, with no peer above 14% of the set's combined revenue — consistent with fragmentation, not proof of it [`CIQ Comps → Financial Data` — vendor export]. The concentrated half is where the money actually passes: Take-Two's **five largest customers were 80.6% of FY26 net revenue, with Apple, Sony, Google, and Microsoft each above 10.0% individually** — and that concentration has been stable for three years (80.6% FY26, 81.0% FY25, 79.8% FY24) [`FY26 10-K, Item 1 — Sales`; `FY26 10-K, Note 2 (Concentration of credit risk)`]. Three of those four gatekeepers are simultaneously named competitors, which the filing states as a risk in its own heading: "We cannot publish our titles without the approval of hardware licensors that are also our competitors", with title-by-title approval required and Sony and Microsoft licences that must be obtained from rivals [`FY26 10-K, Item 1A — hardware licensor risk factor`]. Inside each publisher the revenue is concentrated again — Take-Two's top five franchises were 54.3% of FY26 net revenue and Grand Theft Auto products 12.4% — and the filing generalises it to the industry: "a relatively small number of hit titles account for a large portion of total revenue in our industry" [`FY26 10-K, Item 1A — dependence on key products / industry competition`]. **So the honest structural label is a two-layer market: many competing content suppliers, four tollbooths.** One structural change is only months old and worth flagging: the single closest rival, Electronic Arts, left the public market on August 4, 2026 in a ~$55bn buyout carrying ~$20bn of debt, so the largest direct competitor is now a private, levered entity whose results will not be publicly observable in future periods [`EA Form 8-K Ex.99.1, filed 2026-08-04 (SEC EDGAR, CIK 0000712515)`; `Web: thewrap.com / esportsadvocate.net, Aug 2026 — dated, unverified`; corroborated by the blank price/multiple cells and BB− rating in `CIQ Comps → Financial Data` / `→ Credit Health Panel`, As-Of 2026-11-19 — vendor export].

**One unresolved ownership overlap, flagged and routed, not concluded.** A holder recorded as "Saudi Electronic Games Holding Co, Asset management arm" is a top-five institutional holder of Take-Two with **11,414,680 shares** — about 6.1% of the 187.2m shares outstanding — unchanged across every quarter from December 31, 2025 to September 30, 2026 [`CIQ Public Ownership → History`, periods Dec-31-2025 to Sep-30-2026, retrieved 2026-11-19 — vendor export; `ciq_facts.json → top_institutional_holders`; share count from `CIQ Comps → Financial Data`]. Take-Two's FY26 10-K separately names **"Savvy Games"** as a competitor [`FY26 10-K, Item 1 — Competition`]. Whether these are the same corporate group is **not established anywhere in this pool** — the pool contains no filing, no ownership note and no vendor row that links them, so no link is asserted here. Recorded as a question for `capital-allocation-governance` and `management-governance` to resolve, because a named competitor's affiliate holding ~6% of the company would be a related-party and alignment matter (CLAUDE.md §24 filter 6), not a competitive-map conclusion.

---

## 5. Caveat

Credible competitors **were** identified — three profiled, eight more tabulated, every one of them either named in Take-Two's own FY26 10-K competition disclosure or carried in the Capital IQ comp set — so the "cannot identify competitors" limitation does not apply. Four specific gaps do apply, and downstream agents should treat them as hard limits rather than estimation problems:

1. **No market-share data of any kind.** Neither the filings nor any vendor tab in this pool gives Take-Two's or any peer's share of console, PC, mobile or total interactive-entertainment spending, and there is no industry-size figure to divide by. The Section 3 read is therefore a revenue-growth proxy, not a share measurement. *What would resolve it:* a Newzoo, Circana/NPD, Sensor Tower, data.ai or IDC market-sizing report, or a peer's own disclosed share.
2. **No peer return-on-capital.** The comp export carries margins, growth, leverage and multiples but **no ROIC, ROCE or ROE column**, for any of the ten peers. Take-Two's own return on capital is available (−0.8% FY26, −1.0% LTM to September 30, 2026), so the gap is one-sided: the company can be measured, the peers cannot. The `moat` agent must not state or imply that Take-Two earns more (or less) than peers on capital from this pool. *What would resolve it:* a Capital IQ or Bloomberg peer-ratio export including ROIC/ROE, or each peer's own annual filing.
3. **Mismatched measurement windows, and vendor-not-filing basis.** Peer LTM periods end anywhere from March 31, 2026 (Ubisoft) to November 2026, and every peer figure is the vendor's normalised read rather than that peer's audited accounts. Any cross-peer comparison carries both qualifiers. *What would resolve it:* each peer's own latest annual and interim filing.
4. **No numbers at all for six named competitors** — Embracer Group, Epic Games, Playrix, Savvy Games, Tencent and the platform owners Sony, Microsoft, Apple and Google. Epic Games and Playrix are private; Savvy Games is state-backed and private; Tencent, Sony, Microsoft, Apple and Google are listed but outside this comp set, and for them "interactive entertainment" is a fraction of a much larger company, so a group-level margin would be the wrong comparable even if it were here. The gatekeeper trio's economics in particular — the platform fee they charge Take-Two — are the single most important competitive number in this business and are nowhere in the pool as a rate. *What would resolve it:* Tencent's and Sony's own segment disclosures for their games divisions, and the published platform-fee schedules or any Take-Two platform agreement exhibit stating the royalty rate.

# Moat — TTWO

**Upstream inputs read:** `08_competitive-map.md` (REQUIRED — present), `07_business-quality.md`, `10_external-dependency.md`, plus `00_data-triage.md`, `02_business-identity.md`, `03_segment-map.md`, `04_unit-economics.md`, `05_customer-geography.md`, `06_value-chain.md`, `11_capital-allocation-governance.md`. Nothing missing.

**Regime:** US / SEC domestic filer (NasdaqGS:TTWO, Delaware), US GAAP, reporting currency USD, fiscal year ends **March 31** — "FY2026" = year ended March 31, 2026 [`00_data-triage.md §2A`; `FY26 10-K, cover page`]. All dollar figures USD millions as the filings state them. TTWO is an operating company, not a financial, so the economic test below is **ROIC against WACC**, per `frameworks/SECTOR_OVERLAYS.md` (Generic operating company row — `07_business-quality.md §0` found no sector overlay matches this business).

**Two pool items excluded by triage instruction and cited nowhere below:** the DEF 14A export (July 27, 2026) is an empty "no tabular data" shell, and `Alphabet-Inc-NasdaqGS-GOOGL-Key-Developments` is a misfiled export for a different issuer [`00_data-triage.md §1, §3`].

**Durability discount applied up front (MODULE_RULES §24 Filter 5).** `07_business-quality.md` scored the industry rate-of-change / disruption row **35 (≤40)** and flagged the thesis as a sector / technology-cycle bet. Per this module's rules, **moat durability is discounted throughout this report**: a moat in a fast-changing industry decays faster and is harder to underwrite than the same moat in a stable one (CLAUDE.md §24, Filter 5).

## 1. Named Competitors

Inherited verbatim from `08_competitive-map.md §2` — **no new competitor is named here.** Every name below sits in Take-Two's own FY26 10-K competition disclosure, in the Capital IQ comp set, or both [`FY26 10-K, Item 1 — Competition`; `FY26 10-K, Item 1A — "The interactive entertainment software industry is highly competitive"`].

**Profiled rivals (the three closest):**
- **Electronic Arts Inc.** — the closest structural twin (licensed-sports plus owned IP, across console, PC and mobile), LTM revenue $7,846.0, and **no longer listed**: taken private August 4, 2026 in a ~$55bn buyout carrying ~$20bn of debt, so its future results will not be publicly observable [`competitive-map §2, Competitor A`].
- **Roblox Corporation (NYSE:RBLX)** — nearest rival by size (LTM revenue $5,897.6, 92% of Take-Two's), growing ten times faster (+32.1% vs +3.1%), losing money at every line, and competing for the same in-game wallet while charging nothing for access [`competitive-map §2, Competitor B`].
- **Playtika Holding Corp. (NasdaqGS:PLTK)** — pure mobile free-to-play, LTM revenue $2,828.4 = 85% of Take-Two's $3,333.0 of FY26 mobile revenue, earning an 18.9% EBIT margin on the same genre set and channel as Zynga [`competitive-map §2, Competitor C`].

**Also named / tabulated (one line each):** **Nintendo (TSE:7974)** — largest in the set at $13,684.5 revenue and 19.8% EBIT margin; **Ubisoft (ENXTPA:UBI)** — shrinking (−21.8%) and deeply loss-making (−86.7% EBIT margin); **Konami (TSE:9766)** — the set's most profitable at 33.2% EBIT margin; **NEXON (TSE:3659)** — 26.7% EBIT margin, +24.1% growth; **37 Interactive (SZSE:002555)**, **PLAYSTUDIOS (NasdaqCM:MYPS)**, **tinyBuild (AIM:TBLD)** — smaller comp-set members. **Embracer Group, Epic Games, Playrix, Savvy Games, Tencent, Sony, Microsoft, Apple and Google** are named competitors with **no comparable figures anywhere in this pool** [`competitive-map §2A`]. Tencent is simultaneously a named competitor and the partner through which NBA 2K Online sells in China; Sony, Microsoft, Apple and Google are simultaneously named competitors and each a >10% customer [`FY26 10-K, Item 1 — Competition` and `Item 1 — Sales`].
