## 2. Moat Sources

A moat is something that makes it hard for the competitors above to take Take-Two's profits. Strength bands per `CLAUDE.md` §12: 0–20 very weak · 21–40 weak · 41–60 mixed · 61–80 strong · 81–100 very strong. Higher = stronger moat.

| Possible Moat | Present? (Y/N) | Evidence | Strength /100 |
|---|---|---|---:|
| **Brand** | **Y** | The Grand Theft Auto series has **sold-in over 465 million units worldwide**, described in the filing as "the interactive entertainment industry's most iconic and critically acclaimed brand"; GTA V alone **over 225 million units**; Red Dead Redemption 2 over 80 million; the Zynga portfolio over 10 billion downloads [`FY26 10-K, Item 1 — Our Businesses / Rockstar Games, pp.1–6`]. The pricing proof is specific: **GTA VI base edition at $80 against the industry's standard $70**, a 14% premium taken on the single biggest launch in the industry, with a higher-priced Deluxe Edition alongside [`Q1 FY27 earnings call transcript, Aug 7 2026, Q&A (Pitz / Zelnick)`]. **The limits, stated:** Grand Theft Auto products were only **12.4% of FY26 net revenue** and the top five franchises 54.3%, so the brand covers a minority of the revenue base [`FY26 10-K, Item 1A — dependence on key products`, via `competitive-map §4`]; the CEO declines to use the power — *"Our goal is not to maximize price"* — and NBA 2K27 base pricing was **held at $70** [`Q1 FY27 transcript, Aug 7 2026, Q&A (Zelnick)`]; the company cuts wholesale price by design, *"typically… three to nine months after a product's initial launch"* [`FY26 10-K, Item 1 — Marketing, p.5`]; and the filing itself warns competitors using AI may reduce *"our ability to charge the same prices we have historically charged"* [`FY26 10-K, Item 1A, p.11`] | **60** |
| **Cost advantage** | **N** | The numbers run the other way. LTM gross margin **57.7%** against the ten-peer median **66.9%**; LTM EBIT margin **−1.8%** (vendor) / **−2.8%** (derived from the filings, see §3) against the peer median **+15.9%** — fourth-lowest of eleven [`CIQ Comps → Operating Statistics`, As-Of 2026-11-19 — vendor export; `competitive-map §2A`]. The cost base is the problem, not the advantage: selling and marketing **$1,770.8 = 26.6% of FY26 revenue** and R&D **$1,074.6 = 16.1%** together absorb the whole gross profit [`FY26 10-K, MD&A, pp.43–44`]. `06_value-chain.md` found **no contractual pass-through** of input costs, with realised recovery of **56% as reported (~106% ex-stock-compensation) of a 250bps input-cost rise in H1 FY27, achieved on the cost side rather than through price** — a demonstrated ability to absorb cost, not a structural cost edge. Playtika turns a 73.6% gross margin into 18.9% at the operating line on a base 44% of Take-Two's size, which makes the consolidated operating loss a Take-Two outcome rather than an industry one [`competitive-map §2, Competitor C`] | **18** |
| **Distribution** | **N** | Distribution is the single largest claim *against* Take-Two's profits, not a defence of them. Five customers were **80.6% of FY26 net revenue** (81.0% FY25, 79.8% FY24), with **Apple, Sony, Google and Microsoft each above 10.0% individually**, sold *"primarily without long-term agreements or other commitments,"* and customers *"may terminate their relationship with us at any time"*; Sony *"may terminate the agreement for any or no reason upon 30 days' notice"*, and the Sony plus both Microsoft console licences **expire March 31, 2027** [`FY26 10-K, Item 1 — Sales`; `FY26 10-K, Note 1 (Concentration of Credit Risk), p.68`; `FY26 10-K, Item 1 — Arrangements with Platform Manufacturers, pp.3–4`]. The filing states the dependency in its own risk-factor heading: *"We cannot publish our titles without the approval of hardware licensors that are also our competitors."* **91.0% of FY26 mobile revenue** was earned on Apple and Google platforms, and those platforms *"ha[ve] broad discretion to change and interpret [their] terms of service"* and *"may also change [their] fee structure"* [`FY26 10-K, Item 1A, p.11`]. One partial, unsized offset: the CEO says a direct-to-consumer channel *"has had a material effect positive on our margins in the Mobile business"* — but **no filing quantifies the D2C share**, so the offset is not sized from available data [`Q1 FY27 transcript, Aug 7 2026, Q&A (Zelnick)`; `10_external-dependency.md §1`] | **22** |
| **Scale** | **Y (structural only)** | The scale is real and measurable: LTM revenue **$6,409.7**, second-largest of the named rivals behind Nintendo ($13,684.5) and EA ($7,846.0); **12,909 full-time staff of whom 9,998 work in development studios** across 14 countries [`CIQ Comps → Financial Data`, As-Of 2026-11-19 — vendor export; `FY26 10-K, Item 1, p.1`]. **But it buys nothing an owner is paid for.** Five consecutive years of negative return on capital (§3) on a bigger revenue base each year; the $1,770.8 marketing line is *rising* with scale, exactly as the filing predicts (*"increasing player acquisition and retention costs"*); and the company states in writing that *"there are relatively low barriers to entry to develop a mobile or online game"* and that it competes with *"a vast number of small companies and individuals who are able to create and launch games… with relatively limited start-up time or expertise"* — on the **50.1% of FY26 revenue** that is mobile [`FY26 10-K, Item 1A, p.11`; `FY26 10-K, MD&A, p.44`]. This is the textbook **moat in structure, not economics** | **35** |
| **Technology / IP** | **Y** | The ownership claim is the company's own and it is specific: *"Most of our intellectual property is internally owned and developed, which we believe best positions us financially and competitively"*, protected *"under copyright, patent, trademark, and trade secret laws as well as through contractual restrictions on disclosure, copying, and distribution"* [`FY26 10-K, Item 1 — Intellectual Property, p.3`; `FY26 10-K, Item 1 — General, p.1`]. The capital behind it is on the balance sheet: **$2,346.3 of capitalised software development costs and licences, of which $2,149.7 relates to titles not yet released** [`FY26 10-K, Note 7, p.83`]. **Three evidenced limits.** (1) **No patent portfolio is disclosed anywhere in this pool** — no count, no coverage, no expiry; the only patent discussion runs *against* Take-Two: *"many patents have been issued that may apply to potential new modes of delivering, playing or monetizing products… future opportunities… may be precluded by existing patents that we are unable to acquire or license on reasonable terms"* [`FY26 10-K, Item 1A, p.30`]. (2) The IP is *"susceptible to piracy and unauthorized copying"* [`FY26 10-K, Item 1A, p.21`]. (3) Most of the IP it **bought** lost its value: accumulated goodwill impairment of **$5,887.3 against gross goodwill of $6,949.2 (~85% of all goodwill ever recorded)**, plus Developed Game Technology intangible impairments of $577.4 (FY24) and $465.3 (FY23) and software-development-cost impairments of $77.5 (FY25) and $109.9 (FY24) [`FY26 10-K, Note 9, pp.83–84`; `FY26 10-K, Note 7, p.83`] | **48** |
| **Licenses / regulation** | **N** | **The licences are a cost and a liability, not a barrier.** Take-Two needs no operating licence from any state and is not rate-regulated, so there is no regulatory gate protecting it. The licences it *does* hold — major sports leagues, players' associations, car manufacturers, music labels — are third-party rights where *"Competition for these licenses is intense"*, where *"we are subject to guaranteed minimum payments, royalties or standards of performance and may not be able to terminate these agreements prior to their stated expiration"*, and where *"certain intellectual property rights may be licensed to us on a nonexclusive basis. The owners… are free to license such rights to third parties, **including our competitors**, on terms that may be superior to those offered to us"* [`FY26 10-K, Item 1A — Our ability to acquire and maintain licenses, pp.21–22`; `FY26 10-K, Note 1 — Licenses`]. EA holds the directly competing licensed-sports set (EA SPORTS FC, Madden, NHL, UFC, F1, **PGA TOUR** against Take-Two's NBA 2K, WWE 2K and **PGA TOUR 2K**) [`competitive-map §2, Competitor A`]. Regulation points the other way too: loot-box and in-game-currency rules live or pending in six jurisdictions, aimed at the **78.1% of FY26 revenue** that is in-game monetisation [`FY26 10-K, Item 1A — loot boxes / virtual currencies`; `10_external-dependency.md §1A`] | **15** |
| **Network effects** | **Y (real but unmeasurable)** | There is genuine evidence of a multiplayer community that persists: *"we're 13 years after GTA Online was launched and people are still highly engaged and we have record-setting engagement at times"*, plus the GTA+ membership programme, and *"Our NBA 2K Online business is the top online PC sports game in China"* [`Q1 FY27 transcript, Aug 7 2026, Q&A (Zelnick)`; `FY26 10-K, Item 1 — Rockstar Games, p.2`; `FY26 10-K, Item 1 — Identify and Lead New Paradigms`]. **It cannot be sized, and the comparison is unfavourable.** No MAU, DAU, paying-user count, ARPDAU or retention figure exists anywhere in this pool [`02_business-identity.md §3a`; `07_business-quality.md §1`], so the strength of the effect is **not proven from available data**. Meanwhile the one named rival built entirely on a user-generated network — Roblox — grew LTM revenue **+32.1%** against Take-Two's **+3.1%**, and Take-Two's own recurrent consumer spending fell **9%** in Q2 FY27 against guidance of −5% [`CIQ Comps → Operating Statistics` — vendor export; `Q2 FY27 investor deck, Nov 9 2026, slide 4`] | **38** |
| **Switching costs** | **N** | **Both sides of the ledger are weak, and the player side got weaker in the latest filing.** Players: the company's own disclosed service period for accounting purposes is *"generally between **five and fifteen** months depending on the software product"* [`FY26 10-K, Note 1 — Revenue Recognition, p.76`] — **down from "between six and fifteen months"** the year before [`FY25 10-K, Note 1 — Revenue Recognition, p.76`]; and amortisation periods for software products *"generally range from **9 to 36 months**"* [`FY26 10-K, Note 1 — Software Development Costs`] — **down from "12 to 36 months"** [`FY25 10-K, Note 1`]. Both disclosed unit lifetimes **shortened year over year**, which is the opposite direction from a switching-cost moat [`04_unit-economics.md`]. A player loses nothing by not buying the next virtual-currency pack. Customers: no long-term agreements, terminable at will, 30 days' notice from Sony (see Distribution row). The only genuine lock is **mutual** — a platform wants Grand Theft Auto as much as Take-Two wants the platform — and mutual is not a moat for either side | **22** |
| **Natural resource access** | **N** | Not applicable, and evidenced as such rather than assumed. The company's own market-risk section names **only two** exposures — interest rates and foreign exchange — and **no commodity**; **97.0% of FY26 net revenue came through digital online channels** (physical retail and other 3.0%) [`FY26 10-K, Item 7A, opening paragraph`; `FY26 10-K, MD&A, p.44`]. There is no resource input in a download to have privileged access to | **0** |
| **Location advantage** | **N** | No location advantage is claimed or evidenced. Studios sit in 14 countries and the only location-specific facts in the filings run against the company: a Turkish Lira devaluation that *"negatively affected our results"*, and an impaired San Francisco office lease valued on sublease assumptions [`FY26 10-K, Item 1A — international trade`; `FY26 10-K, Note — Leases (San Francisco Office fair value)`]. Revenue is 59% United States / 41% International with no market where position rests on physical location [`ciq_facts.json → geographic` (`CIQ Financials → Segments`, 12 months Mar-31-2026) — vendor export] | **5** |

**Summary of the rows.** Four sources are present (brand, scale, technology/IP, network effects); the strongest is **brand at 60**. Six are absent. **No source reaches the 61–80 "strong" band.** Section 3 then tests whether any of this translates into money.

---

## 3. Competitive Economics

All margin figures for the named competitors are **taken from `competitive-map`'s profiles and §2A table**, not independently re-pulled, so the two reports cannot diverge. Every peer figure is `CIQ Comps → Operating Statistics` / `→ Financial Data`, As-Of **2026-11-19** — a **tier-5 vendor export, not a filing** — and each peer's LTM window ends on a different date (listed). Take-Two's own figures are shown on **both** bases: the vendor read, and my own read computed from the audited filings, which wins under §4 where they differ.

| Company / Competitor | Gross Margin | EBIT Margin | Return on capital (ROIC) | Period | Source |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---|---|
| **Take-Two (vendor read)** | 57.7% | −1.8% | **−1.0%** | LTM to Sep-30-2026 | `CIQ Comps → Operating Statistics`, As-Of 2026-11-19; ROIC from `ciq_facts.json → multi_year_trajectory` (`CIQ Financials → Ratios`) — vendor export |
| **Take-Two (my read, from the filings)** | **57.0%** | **−2.8%** | **−2.2%** | LTM to Sep-30-2026 | Computed: see the build below. `FY26 10-K, Consolidated Statements of Operations`; `Q2 FY27 10-Q, Condensed Consolidated Statements of Operations, p.2` |
| **Take-Two (my read, from the filings)** | 57.7% | **−1.6%** | **−1.3%** | FY2026 (year to Mar-31-2026) | `FY26 10-K, MD&A, p.43 — loss from operations $(104.2), (1.5)% of revenue`; invested capital from `FY26 10-K, Consolidated Balance Sheets` |
| Competitor 1 — **Electronic Arts** | 79.9% | +17.9% | **Not disclosed** | LTM to Jun-30-2026 | `competitive-map §2 Competitor A` / `CIQ Comps` — vendor export |
| Competitor 2 — **Roblox** | 27.3% | −17.4% | **Not disclosed** | LTM to Sep-30-2026 | `competitive-map §2 Competitor B` / `CIQ Comps` — vendor export |
| Competitor 3 — **Playtika** | 73.6% | +18.9% | **Not disclosed** (equity base is negative; a return on equity would be uninterpretable) | LTM to Sep-30-2026 | `competitive-map §2 Competitor C` / `CIQ Comps` — vendor export |
| Nintendo (TSE:7974) | 45.7% | +19.8% | Not disclosed | LTM, vendor window | `competitive-map §2A` — vendor export |
| Ubisoft (ENXTPA:UBI) | 88.1% | −86.7% | Not disclosed | LTM to Mar-31-2026 | `competitive-map §2A` — vendor export |
| Konami Group (TSE:9766) | 52.2% | +33.2% | Not disclosed | LTM, vendor window | `competitive-map §2A` — vendor export |
| NEXON (TSE:3659) | 60.3% | +26.7% | Not disclosed | LTM, vendor window | `competitive-map §2A` — vendor export |
| 37 Interactive (SZSE:002555) | 77.1% | +13.9% | Not disclosed | LTM, vendor window | `competitive-map §2A` — vendor export |
| PLAYSTUDIOS (NasdaqCM:MYPS) | 79.1% | −10.9% | Not disclosed | LTM, vendor window | `competitive-map §2A` — vendor export |
| tinyBuild (AIM:TBLD) | 34.1% | −9.4% | Not disclosed | LTM, vendor window | `competitive-map §2A` — vendor export |
| **Comp-set median (10 peers)** | **66.9%** | **+15.9%** | **Not disclosed** | mixed windows | `competitive-map §2A` — vendor export |

**Why every peer return-on-capital cell says "Not disclosed."** `competitive-map §5, gap 2` establishes it: the comp export carries margins, growth, leverage and multiples but **no ROIC, ROCE or ROE column for any of the ten peers**, and EA no longer files. The gap is one-sided — the company can be measured, the peers cannot. Per that instruction, **no claim is made here that Take-Two earns more or less than its peers on capital.** *What would resolve it:* a Capital IQ or Bloomberg peer-ratio export including ROIC, or each peer's own annual filing.

### The economic moat test (required)

> Return on capital **below** cost of capital: **−0.7% through-cycle ROIC (5-year FY2022–FY2026 average)** vs **~8.2% WACC** (**−890 bps**) — ROIC computed from the audited filings on a normalized 21.0% tax rate and cross-checked against the vendor series; WACC a labelled CAPM group estimate, **"Inference, not from filings"**, inputs shown below. On the latest LTM figure the gap is **−1,040 bps** (−2.2% vs ~8.2%); on the most generous 10-year through-cycle reading it is still **−400 bps** (+4.2% vs ~8.2%).

**Build 1 — the return, computed, not swallowed.** Management discloses no ROIC/ROCE headline, so there is no management figure to cross-check; the figure that circulates is the **vendor's**, and I have computed my own against it as the rules require.

- **Numerator (NOPAT).** EBIT comes from the filings, not the vendor: FY2026 loss from operations **$(104.2)**, stated as **(1.5)% of revenue** [`FY26 10-K, MD&A, p.43`]. LTM to Sep-30-2026 = FY26 $(104.2) − H1 FY26 $(76.4) + H1 FY27 $(153.0) = **$(180.8)** [`Q2 FY27 10-Q, p.2 — six months ended September 30, 2026 and 2025`]. **Flagged divergence:** the vendor records LTM EBIT at **$(112.5)** — about **100 bps of margin more favourable** than the filings-derived $(180.8). The filing wins (§4, §5) and the more conservative figure is used; the vendor read is printed in the table beside it.
- **Normalized structural tax rate: 21.0%.** FY26's **reported** effective tax rate was **(50.8)%** against a 21.0% US federal statutory rate — a distorted single-year rate, not the structural one [`FY26 10-K, MD&A — Provision for income taxes`; cross-checked in `ciq_facts.json`-adjacent `CIQ Financials → Income Statement`, which marks the FY2023–FY2026 effective rate "NM"]. **What I stripped to get to 21.0%:** the full valuation allowance maintained against US net deferred tax assets, the **$45.7** software-development tax-credit benefit (+1.2% in the rate reconciliation), the foreign-earnings effect (−0.5%), and goodwill-impairment-related non-deductible items; state and local tax net of federal benefit adds +0.4%, so 21.4% is the fuller structural rate and **21.0% is the figure used** [`FY26 10-K, Note — Income Taxes, rate reconciliation`]. **This 21.0% is the anchor the DCF must reconcile to** — `valuation/04_intrinsic-dcf` §1 must compute NOPAT on the same rate, not on the (50.8)% reported rate. Note the sign is invariant to the choice: with EBIT negative, a *higher* assumed tax rate makes NOPAT *less* negative, so 21.0% is the conservative end and no plausible rate turns the return positive.
- **Denominator (invested capital), on a gross basis.** Total debt + total equity, averaged: FY2026 = $2,958.3 + $3,510.9 = $6,469.2; FY2025 = $4,105.9 + $2,137.7 = $6,243.6; average **$6,356.4**. Sep-30-2026 = $2,911.4 + $3,673.6 = $6,585.0; average with FY2026 = **$6,527.1** [`FY26 10-K, Consolidated Balance Sheets`; `Q2 FY27 10-Q, Condensed Consolidated Balance Sheets, p.1`; corroborated in `CIQ Financials_Annual → Balance Sheet` — vendor export].
- **Result.** FY2026 ROIC = $(104.2) × (1 − 0.21) ÷ $6,356.4 = **−1.3%**. LTM ROIC = $(180.8) × 0.79 ÷ $6,527.1 = **−2.2%**. Against the vendor's **−0.8% (FY26)** and **−1.0% (LTM)**: my computed figures are **40–120 bps more negative**. Same sign, same band, same verdict — but the divergence is flagged and the computed figure is the one used (§4).
- **Net-cash check — not applicable, and the cash-netted basis is worse anyway.** Take-Two is **net debt**, not net cash: net debt **$1,237.8** on the vendor basis (which may net short-term investments — a **broad** basis, per the sidecar's own caution), against total debt $2,911.4 and cash plus short-term investments $1,673.6 at Sep-30-2026 [`ciq_facts.json → net_debt_m, total_debt_m`; `CIQ Financials_Quarterly → Balance Sheet` — vendor export]. The invested capital above is therefore already **gross** (no cash netted). For completeness, netting cash out of the denominator makes the return *worse*, not better: FY2026 ROIC on an ex-cash capital base of $4,629.0 average = **−1.8%**.

**Build 2 — the through-cycle return, because the latest year is a cycle TROUGH, not a peak.** `07_business-quality.md §4` establishes the direction of the distortion and it runs the opposite way to the usual trap: the LTM window to Sep-30-2026 is the **pre-launch trough** between Borderlands 4 (September 2025) and **Grand Theft Auto VI, released today, November 19, 2026**, with Q2 FY27 net bookings −18% year on year [`Q2 FY27 earnings release, Nov 9 2026, p.1`; `Q2 FY27 10-Q`]. So the latest figure must not be used raw either — **it is labelled a trough and the test runs on a through-cycle average**:

| Through-cycle window | Basis | Average ROIC | vs ~8.2% WACC |
|---|---|---:|---:|
| FY2022–FY2026 (5 years, the post-Zynga company) | +9.0%, −4.3%, −3.1%, −4.2%, −0.8% | **−0.7%** | **−890 bps** |
| FY2017–FY2026 (10 years, the most generous reading) | 6.0, 6.9, 7.4, 11.2, 13.4, 9.0, −4.3, −3.1, −4.2, −0.8 (%) | **+4.2%** | **−400 bps** |

[`ciq_facts.json → multi_year_trajectory` (`CIQ Financials → Ratios`, "Return on Capital %") — tier-5 vendor export.] The **5-year window is the right structural one** — Zynga was acquired in May 2022, and the FY2017–FY2021 years describe a materially smaller, pre-acquisition company that had not yet written off 85% of its goodwill. The 10-year average is shown because it is the most favourable honest reading available, and it is **still 400 bps below the cost of capital.** `07_business-quality.md` also anchors the through-cycle **EBITDA margin at roughly 12%** (ten-year average EBITDA $471.8 on average revenue $3,919.8), almost exactly where FY26 (12%) and the LTM (11%) sit — so the trough is a trough in *absolute* profit, not a collapse away from the business's normal margin.

**Build 3 — the cost of capital, sourced in the required priority, never invented.**

1. **Company-disclosed figure — sought, and it does not exist as a number.** Take-Two states it uses *"risk-adjusted discount rates **based on our weighted average cost of capital**"* in its goodwill impairment testing, and lists "discount rate" among the significant assumptions audited as a critical audit matter — but **the numeric rate is nowhere in the filings** [`FY26 10-K, Critical Accounting Policies — Goodwill`; `FY26 10-K, Note 9 (Goodwill and Intangible Assets, Net), pp.83–84`; `FY25 10-K, Report of Independent Registered Public Accounting Firm — Critical Audit Matter`]. **Comparator only, not promoted to WACC:** the weighted-average **operating-lease discount rate of 4.74% (FY2026; 4.75% FY2025, 4.56% FY2024)** [`FY26 10-K, Note — Leases, p.91`]. That is an incremental borrowing rate on lease obligations, not the group's cost of capital, and it appears here only as a floor comparator.
2. **So a labelled group CAPM estimate is used — "Inference, not from filings" (CLAUDE.md §3).** Inputs shown:
   - **Risk-free rate 3.9%** — the company's **own** disclosed input: *"Risk-free interest rate 3.8% / 3.9%"* for FY2026 market-based awards, *"based on U.S. Treasury yields"* over a 2.8-year expected service period [`FY26 10-K, Note — Stock-Based Compensation, Market-Based Awards (Monte Carlo assumptions), p.99`]. Corroborated by the company's own market-rate quote: SOFR ~**3.91%** at September 30, 2026 [`Q2 FY27 10-Q, Note — Debt / Item 3`]. A longer-dated Treasury would be higher, which would only **widen** the gap — 3.9% is the conservative end.
   - **Beta 0.92** — Take-Two's own 5-year beta [`CIQ Comps → Operating Statistics, "5 Year Beta" (subject row)`, As-Of 2026-11-19 — vendor export; peer betas in the same column run −0.45 to 1.53, median 0.65].
   - **Equity risk premium 5.0%** — **judgment, not from filings.** No equity-risk-premium figure exists anywhere in this pool. This is the single weakest input and is sensitised below.
   - **Cost of equity = 3.9% + 0.92 × 5.0% = 8.5%.**
   - **Pre-tax cost of debt 5.4%** — the company's own most recent issue coupons (**5.400% Senior Notes due 2029**, **5.600% due 2034**) and its revolver pricing of **SOFR + 1.000%–1.625%** (= 4.9%–5.5% at SOFR 3.91%), with an alternate base rate of 7.00% [`FY26 10-K, Note 11 (Debt)`; `Q2 FY27 10-Q, Note — Debt`]. After tax at 21.0%: **4.3%**.
   - **Weights** — equity $40,549.3 (187.2m shares × $216.61 day close) = **93.3%**; total debt $2,911.4 = **6.7%** [`ciq_facts.json → shares_outstanding_m, current_price, total_debt_m` (`CIQ Comps → Financial Data`; `CIQ Financials → Balance Sheet`) — vendor export].
   - **WACC = 0.933 × 8.5% + 0.067 × 4.3% = 8.2%.** Sensitised on the judgment input: an ERP of 4.5%–6.0% gives a WACC of **7.8%–9.1%**.
3. **Reality-tested, not merely assembled (CLAUDE.md §16).** The 8.2% estimate sits **above** the company's own 4.74% lease borrowing rate (as a WACC should sit above a debt rate) and is not flattered by any observable yield: the trailing earnings yield is **negative** (LTM net loss $240.5), and FCF on the §15 basis (CFO − total capex) is **$367.1 − $304.5 = $62.6**, a **0.15%** yield on the $40,549.3 market capitalisation [`ciq_facts.json → ltm_ocf_m`; `CIQ Financials_Annual → Cash Flow, Capital Expenditure` — vendor export]. The market is plainly pricing a large forward recovery rather than current cash flow, which means 8.2% is not an implausibly low rate manufactured to create a spread.

**The test is robust to every input choice.** Even at the 4.74% lease-rate floor — a deliberately unreasonably low stand-in for a cost of capital — the 5-year through-cycle ROIC of −0.7% is **540 bps** below it. There is no positive cost of capital at which Take-Two's through-cycle return on capital clears the bar. **That, not the peer margin gap, is the central fact of this report.**

---

## 4. Where The Company Sits

1. **Relative to peers.** **Company sits at the bottom of named peers on margin; on capital efficiency the comparison cannot be made.** On margin the numbers are unambiguous: gross margin **57.7%** against the ten-peer median **66.9%** (−920 bps), and EBIT margin **−1.8% (vendor) / −2.8% (filings-derived)** against the median **+15.9%** — fourth-lowest of eleven, ahead only of Ubisoft (−86.7%), Roblox (−17.4%) and PLAYSTUDIOS (−10.9%), and below both direct structural rivals EA (+17.9%) and Playtika (+18.9%) [`competitive-map §2A`; `CIQ Comps → Operating Statistics`, As-Of 2026-11-19 — vendor export]. On capital efficiency: **no peer ROIC, ROCE or ROE figure exists anywhere in this pool**, so **"Insufficient data to compare against named peers"** applies to the return-on-capital comparison specifically, in either direction [`competitive-map §5, gap 2`]. Two qualifiers travel with the margin read: peer windows end on different dates (March 31, 2026 for Ubisoft through November 2026), and every peer figure is the vendor's normalised read rather than that peer's audited accounts.

2. **Absolute (the economic moat test).** **The company earns a return on capital below its cost of capital** — **−0.7% through-cycle ROIC (5-year) vs ~8.2% WACC**, a gap of **−890 bps**; **−2.2% LTM vs ~8.2%**, −1,040 bps; **+4.2% on the most generous 10-year reading vs ~8.2%**, still −400 bps. This is the decisive test, and it is **not** "Not assessable": the return is computed from the audited filings and cross-checked against the vendor series, and the cost of capital is a labelled CAPM estimate whose every input is shown and whose conclusion survives the full sensitivity range (7.8%–9.1%) and even a 4.74% floor. **Whatever advantage exists in Section 2 is therefore structural, not economic.**

---

## 5. Moat Verdict

# No moat proven

Specifically the **"moat in structure, not economics"** case the rules name: Take-Two has a real, cited brand asset and real scale, and **neither lifts the return on capital above the cost of capital**.

The strongest moat is **brand, at 60/100** — Grand Theft Auto has sold-in over 465 million units, is described in the filing as the industry's most iconic brand, still produces record engagement thirteen years after GTA Online launched, and just carried an **$80** price against the industry's **$70** [`FY26 10-K, Item 1, pp.1–6`; `Q1 FY27 transcript, Aug 7 2026, Q&A`]. The durability test it must pass over the next five years is precise and already measurable in the company's own reporting: **the new Grand Theft Auto base must build a second durable live-service annuity that holds the group return on capital above ~8.2% across a full release cycle, not for one peak year** — tested by recurrent consumer spending as a share of net bookings against the guided fall to ~65% in FY27 from 78% in FY26, and by RCS growth against the −9% it printed in Q2 FY27 versus guidance of −5% [`Q2 FY27 investor deck, Nov 9 2026, slides 4, 5 and 8`]. Two near-dated items sit inside that window and belong to other agents but bear on brand durability: all three console publisher licences (Sony and both Microsoft agreements, covering 39.0% of FY26 revenue) **expire March 31, 2027**, and the loot-box rulemaking in six jurisdictions points at the 78.1% of revenue that is in-game monetisation.

**The hard rule is applied, not argued around.** Because the return on capital is **below** the cost of capital on every basis tested (§3), the verdict **cannot be "Strong moat"** regardless of any peer-relative reading — and in this case the peer-relative reading runs the same way anyway (bottom of the set on margin). **Durability is further discounted** because `07_business-quality.md` scored industry rate-of-change **35 (Weak, ≤40)**: half the revenue base (mobile, 50.1%) sits in a market whose barriers to entry the company itself calls *"relatively low"*, and the proof that disruption already bit is in the accounts — **$5,887.3 of accumulated goodwill impairment against $6,949.2 of gross goodwill** taken because of *"industry conditions and changes in our strategies"* (CLAUDE.md §24, Filter 5) [`FY26 10-K, Note 9, pp.83–84`].

### Moat trajectory — **eroding (not confirmed)**

**Trajectory evidence panel.** Window FY2022 (year to Mar-31-2022, the last pre-Zynga year) → FY2026 / LTM to Sep-30-2026. All Take-Two figures from the filings where available, with the vendor series used for the ROIC line and for FY2022 EBITDA. "Supports" means the row points toward erosion.

| Metric (same 5-year window) | Start (FY2022) | Latest (FY2026 / LTM) | Direction | Supports / contradicts the stated trajectory |
|---|---:|---:|---|---|
| **Gross margin** | 58.2% ($2,040.0 ÷ $3,504.8) | 57.7% FY26 / **57.0% LTM** | roughly flat, −50 to −120 bps | **Neutral** — too small to carry a direction either way |
| **EBITDA margin** | 19.1% ($671.0 ÷ $3,504.8) | 11.8% FY26 / **11.3% LTM** | **down ~730–780 bps** | **Supports** |
| **EBIT / operating margin** | +15.6% ($545.0 ÷ $3,504.8) | −1.6% FY26 / **−2.8% LTM** (filings) | **down ~1,720–1,840 bps**; sign flipped | **Supports** |
| **Return on capital (ROIC) vs cost of capital** | +9.0% vs ~8.2% = **+80 bps spread** | −0.8% FY26 (vendor) / −1.3% computed; **−2.2% LTM computed** vs ~8.2% = **−1,040 bps** | **down ~1,120 bps of spread**; crossed from above to below | **Supports — and this is the decisive row** |
| **Market share (closest disclosed proxy)** | not measurable — no share data in this pool | LTM revenue **+3.1%** vs ten-peer median **+14.6%**; but FY26 revenue **+18.2%** and FY27 guidance implies **+19% to +22%** | **mixed** | **Mixed / partly contradicts** — see adjudication |
| **Cash conversion (CFO ÷ EBITDA)** | 38.5% ($258.0 ÷ $671.0) | **79.2% FY26** ($624.3 ÷ $788.5) / 50.6% LTM | **up** (though it ran 0.2%, −3.7%, −11.9% in FY23–FY25) | **Contradicts** |
| **Pricing / realised price or mix** | industry-standard $60–$70 AAA pricing; no premium evidenced | **GTA VI at $80 vs the $70 standard** (+14%); NBA 2K27 held at $70 | **up on the flagship** | **Contradicts** |

Sources for the panel: `FY26 10-K, MD&A, p.43` and `Consolidated Statements of Operations / Cash Flows`; `Q2 FY27 10-Q, pp.1–2`; `ciq_facts.json → margin_trend, multi_year_trajectory` (`CIQ Financials → Income Statement` / `→ Ratios`) and `CIQ Financials_Annual → Cash Flow` — tier-5 vendor export; `CIQ Comps → Operating Statistics`, As-Of 2026-11-19 — vendor export; `Q2 FY27 investor deck, Nov 9 2026, slides 5 and 8`; `Q1 FY27 transcript, Aug 7 2026, Q&A (Pitz / Zelnick)`.

**Naming and adjudicating every contradicting row (CLAUDE.md §3).** The panel is **split — three rows support erosion, one is neutral, two contradict it and one is mixed — so the word "confirmed" is not available here and is not used.**

- **Cash conversion contradicts, and it genuinely does.** CFO ÷ EBITDA went from 38.5% to 79.2% in FY26. It does not overturn the erosion read for a stated reason: the same series collapsed to **0.2% (FY23), −3.7% (FY24) and −11.9% (FY25)** in between, with operating cash flow **negative in two of the last four years** [`FY26 10-K, Consolidated Statements of Cash Flows`], so this is a volatile release-timing series rather than an improving structural one — and conversion of EBITDA says nothing about whether the capital behind it earns a return, which is the row that defines the moat.
- **Realised price contradicts, and it is the strongest point against the erosion read.** An $80 price on the flagship against a $70 industry standard is a real, exercised pricing advantage and is exactly why the brand row still scores 60. It does not overturn the read because **GTA products were 12.4% of FY26 revenue**, the CEO states the policy as *"Our goal is not to maximize price"*, NBA 2K27 pricing was held flat, and the company cuts wholesale price *"typically… three to nine months after a product's initial launch"* — so the premium is narrow in coverage and deliberately not generalised [`FY26 10-K, Item 1 — Marketing, p.5`; `Q1 FY27 transcript, Aug 7 2026, Q&A`].
- **The share proxy is mixed, and `competitive-map` already adjudicated it — its qualifier is carried intact.** LTM revenue growth of +3.1% against a peer median of +14.6% points to losing ground, but FY26 revenue grew **+18.2%** and FY27 guidance implies **+19% to +22%**. Competitive-map's own verdict is quoted rather than hardened: *"Losing share on the latest twelve-month window; the read is a one-metric proxy inside a stated launch trough, is not confirmed, and is not durable evidence of competitive decline"* [`competitive-map §3`]. No market-share figure of any kind exists in this pool.
- **Gross margin is neutral, not supporting.** It is roughly flat over the window. The erosion lives entirely **below** the gross line — in the $1,770.8 marketing line (26.6% of revenue), $1,074.6 of R&D (16.1%), and development amortisation — which is why the EBITDA, EBIT and ROIC rows all move while gross margin does not [`FY26 10-K, MD&A, pp.43–44`].

**What still makes the direction "eroding" despite a split panel.** One structural fact in the window is not a cycle artefact at all: Take-Two wrote off **$5,887.3 of goodwill against $6,949.2 of gross goodwill — ~85% of all goodwill ever recorded** — explicitly because of *"industry conditions and changes in our strategies for games within the reporting unit"*, plus $577.4 and $465.3 of Developed Game Technology impairments [`FY26 10-K, Note 9, pp.83–84`]. A permanent destruction of invested capital on that scale, five consecutive years of negative ROIC, and **two disclosed unit lifetimes that both shortened in the latest filing** (player service period "six and fifteen" → **"five and fifteen"** months; title amortisation "12 to 36" → **"9 to 36"** months) are evidence about the durability of the economics, not about when a title shipped. **So: eroding on the return-on-capital, operating-margin and unit-life lines; offset so far on pricing and cash conversion; and NOT confirmed, because the window ends in a stated pre-launch trough and two panel rows plus the share proxy point the other way.** Downstream language must match that calibration — this is an eroding-but-unconfirmed read, not confirmed erosion, and it must not be carried into a scenario driver or a kill criterion as though it were settled.

**Which way I would be wrong, and when it lands.** **The single next data point is Take-Two's Q3 FY27 result — the first full quarter with Grand Theft Auto VI on sale (it released November 19, 2026) — covering the quarter ending December 31, 2026 and expected on or about February 9, 2027** [`CIQ Estimates → Consensus, "next release flagged Feb-09-2027"`; `CIQ Events Calendar` — vendor export; `Q2 FY27 10-Q`]. The bar is explicit and already in the pool: FY27 guidance of **$8,000–8,200** of net bookings against FY26's $6,721, and the vendor's consensus column carries an FY2027 estimate of **$1,570.9 of EBIT on $8,554.7 of revenue** (an 18.4% margin) [`Q2 FY27 investor deck, slides 5 and 8`; `CIQ Financials_Annual → Income Statement, estimate column` — vendor export, estimate not fact]. If that is delivered, NOPAT at the 21.0% normalized rate would be roughly **$1,241 on ~$6,500 of invested capital ≈ +19% ROIC**, i.e. roughly **1,080 bps above** the ~8.2% cost of capital — and the trajectory read flips from eroding to widening, with the moat level reopening to Narrow or better once a full cycle (not one peak year) is on that footing. If instead the launch quarter lands materially short of the guided range while the FY27 implied second-half RCS share keeps falling toward ~65%, the erosion read moves from unconfirmed to confirmed and feeds the permanent-impairment / declining-perpetuity trigger in `valuation/04` §5 and `valuation/07`.

---

**Not proven from available data, recorded as hard limits rather than estimation problems:** any peer's return on capital (no ROIC/ROCE/ROE column exists for any of the ten peers in this pool); Take-Two's or any peer's share of console, PC, mobile or total interactive-entertainment spending (no share figure and no industry-size denominator anywhere); player retention, MAU/DAU, paying-user count and ARPDAU, so the strength of the network-effect row cannot be sized; any patent count, coverage or expiry; the platform fee rate Apple, Google, Sony and Microsoft charge, which is the single most important competitive number in this business; the direct-to-consumer share of mobile revenue, so the one disclosed offset to the gatekeeper dependency is unsized; and Take-Two's own numeric weighted-average cost of capital, which the filings reference but never state — hence the labelled CAPM estimate in §3.



---

## business-model / 10_external-dependency.md

_Source: `10_external-dependency.md`_

# External Dependency Check — TTWO

**Filing regime:** US / SEC domestic filer, US GAAP, reporting currency USD, fiscal year ends March 31 (so "FY2026" = year ended March 31, 2026). Per triage `00_data-triage.md`. All dollar figures below are USD millions unless stated, and are taken from the filings in their own units.

**Scope note:** this report covers only what management cannot control. Business quality, management decisions, and valuation are other agents' work. Two pool items are excluded by triage instruction: the DEF 14A export (empty shell, no proxy content) and `Alphabet Inc NasdaqGS GOOGL Key Developments` (a misfiled export for a different issuer).

---

## 1. Dependency Table

| External Variable | Dependency Level (Low / Mid / High) | Why It Matters | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Interest rates** | **Low** | Rates hit TTWO's interest income more than its interest cost. All $2,970 of principal by maturity is fixed-rate; the $1,000 revolver was undrawn at both period ends. The company's own market-risk section says there was "no material interest rate risk exposure" and that historically rate moves "have not had a significant effect on our operating results." The one real exposure is refinancing: $600 of 3.700% notes mature in 2027, and the market rate the company itself quotes is higher (alternate base rate 7.00%, SOFR ~3.91% plus a 1.000%–1.625% margin at Sep 30, 2026). | `FY26 10-K, Item 7A (Interest Rate Risk)`; `Q2 FY27 10-Q, Item 3 (Interest Rate Risk)`; `CIQ Financials_Annual → Capital Structure Details, as of Mar-31-2026 — 0 floating / 2,970 fixed; 2027 maturity 600 — vendor export`; `FY26 10-K, Note 11 (Debt) — 3.700% Senior Notes due 2027` |
| **FX** | **Mid** | 40.8% of FY26 net revenue was earned outside the U.S. (40.6% in the Sep-2026 quarter), and the company publishes a sensitivity: a 10.0% rise in the U.S. dollar against all currencies cuts revenue by 4.0%. Hedging is real but deliberately narrow — the forward and option-collar book covers balance-sheet items (cash, intercompany loans, receivables, payables), not revenue translation. Separately, the cost side sits in volatile-currency countries: the company names the Turkish Lira devaluation in the six months to Sep 30, 2023 as having "negatively affected our results," and it runs studios in Turkey among 14 countries. Cumulative FX transaction losses of $17.4 (FY26), $22.6 (FY25) and $28.6 (FY24) are small against $6,656 of FY26 revenue. | `FY26 10-K, Item 7A (Foreign Currency Exchange Rate Risk)`; `Q2 FY27 10-Q, Item 3 (Foreign Currency Exchange Rate Risk)`; `FY26 10-K, Item 1 (Business — Research and Development, studio locations)`; `FY26 10-K, Item 1A (We are subject to risks and uncertainties of international trade)` |
| **Government policy — trade and tariffs** | **Mid** | TTWO's own product is 97.0% digital, so a tariff does not land on its shipments. It lands two steps away: on the component parts of the consoles TTWO's games run on, and on the global economy its customers spend from. The 10-K ties console availability and pricing directly to "an increase in tariffs or trade restrictions on component parts," and the Q2 FY27 10-Q still lists "global tariff policies" as a factor it is monitoring for consumer demand, pricing pressure, receivable credit quality and FX. 41.4% of H1 FY27 net revenue came from console. No dollar impact is quantified anywhere in the pool. | `FY26 10-K, Item 1A (Our business is subject to our ability to develop commercially successful products for the current video game platforms)`; `FY26 10-K, Item 1A (We face risks from our international operations)`; `Q2 FY27 10-Q, MD&A (Economic Environment and Retailer Performance; Hardware Platforms)` |
| **Government policy — tax** | **Mid** | Tax policy is a cash-flow driver management does not set. Software-development tax credits gave a $45.7 benefit to the FY26 tax rate and $54.5 in FY25, and the 10-K explicitly flags "changes in legislation that could scale back or eliminate tax credits for software development (which have benefitted our results in the past)." Against that, several named regimes could push the other way (CAMT, BEAT, ARPA's pay-deduction limit effective April 1, 2027, digital-services taxes). FY26 reported an effective tax rate of (50.8)% against a 21% statutory rate, so the tax line is already dominated by valuation-allowance and geographic-mix effects rather than headline rates. See §1A for each programme's status. | `FY26 10-K, Item 1A (Adverse effects of changes in tax rates and additional tax liabilities)`; `FY26 10-K, MD&A (Provision for income taxes)`; `Q2 FY27 10-Q, Note (Income Taxes)` |
| **Government policy — China market access** | **Mid** | Every game needs regulatory approval to operate in China, and the 10-K says enforcement "remains uncertain." China is described as TTWO's "most established" Asian market, where NBA 2K Online runs through a Tencent partnership and is "the top online PC sports game in China." A revocation or a refusal to approve would hurt. The pool discloses no China revenue figure, so the size of what is at risk is **not disclosed** — do not treat this as a sized exposure. | `FY26 10-K, Item 1 (Business — international)`; `FY26 10-K, Item 1A (We face risks from our international operations)` |
| **Regulation — in-game monetisation (loot boxes, virtual currencies, gambling law)** | **Mid today, with a High-severity tail** | This is the regulation that points at the heart of the business model. Recurrent consumer spending — virtual currency, in-game purchases, in-game advertising — was 78.1% of FY26 net revenue and 81% of Q2 FY27 net bookings. The 10-K names thirteen of its own games whose mechanics "may be deemed as 'loot boxes'", including NBA 2K, WWE 2K and Zynga Poker. Live or landed items: Australia and Brazil have "recently introduced more stringent regulations" on simulated gambling and loot boxes; Australia's Phase 2 Industry Codes were approved by eSafety and add age-assurance requirements; the EU's Consumer Protection Cooperation Network has published "Key Principles on In-game Virtual Currencies." Pending items: a UK government review, a European Commission Digital Fairness Act draft, and the Spanish and Portuguese bills. The FTC's January 2025 action against another developer — settled at a $20 million fine plus a ten-year compliance monitoring programme — shows the live enforcement channel. Dependency is Mid rather than High because no filing in the pool quantifies any realised revenue or cost impact on TTWO from any of these; the tail is High because the exposed base is 78.1% of revenue. | `FY26 10-K, Item 1A (Risks related to legal or regulatory compliance — loot boxes / virtual currencies)`; `FY26 10-K, MD&A (Net revenue from recurrent consumer spending — 78.1%)`; `Q2 FY27 earnings call transcript, Nov 9, 2026, prepared remarks (CFO: RCS "accounted for 81% of net bookings")` |
| **Regulation — content ratings and platform content policy** | **Low** | Content-rating bodies (ESRB and equivalents) and platform content policies can restrict where a game may be sold, and the 10-K lists both as risks. No disclosed instance of a TTWO title being blocked, and no quantified impact in the pool. | `FY26 10-K, Item 1A (ESRB ratings for our products could negatively affect our ability to distribute and sell; Content policies could negatively affect sales)` |
| **Geopolitics** | **Low** | The Russia-Ukraine war and the war in the Middle East appear in at least six separate risk factors — as demand risk, trade-restriction risk, cyber-threat risk, development-disruption risk and an inflation driver. But no revenue or cost exposure to any conflict region is quantified in the pool, and no realised impact is disclosed. Scored Low on that basis, not on a judgment that the risk is absent. | `FY26 10-K, Item 1A (We face risks from our international operations; Reliance on complex information technology systems; We may be adversely affected by the effects of inflation)` |
| **Consumer cycle (discretionary spending)** | **Mid** | The strongest claim on this is TTWO's own: "Most of our products involve discretionary spending on the part of consumers… This makes our products particularly sensitive to general economic conditions and economic cycles," and — note the past tense — "Reduced consumer spending **has** and may in the future continue to result in reduced demand for our products and may also require increased selling and promotional expenses." The exposed base is the 78.1% of revenue that comes from optional in-game purchases a player can stop at will. **The contradicting evidence, named:** recurrent consumer spending fell 9% in Q2 FY27 against guidance of a 5% decline — but the CFO attributed that to NBA 2K, not to the consumer, and in August 2026 the CEO answered an analyst's direct question about peers blaming "macro uncertainty" with "it's really not what we're seeing… we don't feel like the consumer is pulling back at all," attributing the mobile softness to how Color Block Jam was comping against its launch year and to "a bit of pressure on user acquisition." That is management commentary against a filing's acknowledgement, so the filing wins on the structural point (§4) — but the observed swing factor in both FY27 quarters to date was title performance, not the economy. Hence Mid, not High. | `FY26 10-K, Item 1A (Declines in consumer spending and other adverse changes in the economy)`; `Q2 FY27 earnings call transcript, Nov 9, 2026, prepared remarks (CFO: RCS down 9% vs guidance of a 5% decline)`; `Q1 FY27 earnings call transcript, Aug 7, 2026, Q&A (Creutz / Zelnick exchange)` |
| **Industrial cycle — third-party console hardware cycle** | **Mid** | This is the "industrial cycle" that actually matters here: console revenue was 39.0% of FY26 net revenue and 41.4% of H1 FY27 net revenue, and it rides on an installed base built, priced and supported by Sony and Microsoft. The 10-K is explicit on the mechanism: when next-generation consoles are announced, consumers historically cut purchases for prior-generation consoles, and "our business and operating results may be more volatile and difficult to predict during console transitions." It also notes the offsetting fact that current Sony and Microsoft consoles offer backwards compatibility, "which could mitigate the risk of such a decline," while adding "we cannot be certain how backwards compatibility will affect demand." | `FY26 10-K, Item 1A (current video game platforms; console transitions)`; `FY26 10-K, MD&A (Hardware Platforms — 39.0%)`; `Q2 FY27 10-Q, MD&A (Hardware Platforms — 41.4% for the six months ended September 30, 2026)` |
| **Platform owners / distribution gatekeepers — Apple, Google, Sony, Microsoft** *(row added: the largest non-controllable dependency in this business, and none of the ten template variables captures it)* | **High** | Four companies each took more than 10.0% of TTWO's FY26 net revenue, and the top five customers together took 80.6% (81.0% FY25, 79.8% FY24). 91.0% of FY26 mobile revenue was earned on Apple and Google platforms, and mobile was 50.1% of total net revenue. The gatekeepers set the terms: each "has broad discretion to change and interpret its terms of service," and "may also change its fee structure, add fees… alter how we are able to advertise… limit the use of personal information for advertising purposes." TTWO also "require[s] approval of hardware licensors to publish titles." The company has a partial lever — a direct-to-consumer channel the CEO says "has had a material effect positive on our margins in the Mobile business" — and it will "support and continue to support all third-party distributors who treat us appropriately and with which we can make sound economic arrangements." But no filing quantifies the D2C share, so the offset is **not sized from available data**. Nothing about this exposure can be hedged. | `FY26 10-K, Item 1 (Business — Sales and Marketing: five largest customers 80.6%, Apple / Sony / Google / Microsoft each >10.0%)`; `FY26 10-K, Item 1A (platform providers' broad discretion; 91.0% of mobile revenue on Apple and Google)`; `FY26 10-K, MD&A (mobile 50.1% of net revenue)`; `Q1 FY27 earnings call transcript, Aug 7, 2026, Q&A (Zelnick on direct-to-consumer margins and third-party distributors)` |

**Rows deliberately dropped, with the reason (not scored "Low" by default):**

- **Commodity prices** — dropped. TTWO's market-risk disclosure names only two exposures, interest rates and FX, and no commodity. 97.0% of FY26 net revenue came through digital online channels; there is no commodity input in a download. `FY26 10-K, Item 7A (opening paragraph)`; `FY26 10-K, MD&A (digital online channels 97.0%)`.
- **Freight / logistics rates** — dropped. Physical retail and other channels were 3.0% of FY26 net revenue, down from 3.6%. Shipping delays appear in the international-trade risk factor but there is no freight-rate exposure worth scoring against a 3.0% base. `FY26 10-K, MD&A (physical retail and other 3.0%)`.
- **Weather** — dropped. "Adverse weather conditions" appears once, inside a list of generic events that could reduce discretionary spending, with no operational weather exposure disclosed anywhere. That channel is already captured in the consumer-cycle row. `FY26 10-K, Item 1A (Declines in consumer spending)`.

---

## 1A. Named Policy & Subsidy Register — current status as of 2026-11-19

Every named programme that any dependency row above leans on. The pool's most recent filing is the Q2 FY27 10-Q signed November 9, 2026, ten days before the run date; the FY26 10-K was signed May 11, 2026. Where the newest pool document does not carry a programme's current status, that is recorded as not established rather than resolved in either direction.

| Programme (name) | Status as of 2026-11-19 | Terms in the reference period | Terms NOW (or successor programme) | Change, quantified | Stated end date? | Source + date |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **U.S. global 10% tariff — Executive Order of February 24, 2026** (issued after the Supreme Court's February 20, 2026 ruling that certain tariffs under the International Economic Emergency Powers Act were unconstitutional) | **Status not established from available sources.** The last dated fact in the pool is that on May 7, 2026 a panel of federal judges on the Court of International Trade "voted that such 10% tariffs on most U.S. imports are illegal," and that "significant uncertainty remains regarding the status of existing and newly announced tariffs, potential changes or pauses to such tariffs, tariff levels, and whether further additional tariffs or other retaliatory actions may be imposed, modified, or suspended." The Q2 FY27 10-Q (November 9, 2026) still monitors "global tariff policies" but gives no status update, and no successor instrument appears anywhere in the pool. | Global 10% tariff on all countries, for a period of 150 days from February 24, 2026 | Not established. The 150-day term described in the 10-K would run out in roughly late July 2026, and the May 7, 2026 court vote held the tariffs illegal — but **the pool contains no dated primary source showing what actually happened at either point**, and no successor release from the issuing authority. Treated at its last-known terms: a 10% global tariff of contested legal validity. | Not quantified for TTWO in any pool document. The company's product is 97.0% digital, so the channel is indirect — console component parts and consumer demand. | Yes — the Executive Order's own stated 150-day term from February 24, 2026, per the 10-K's description | `FY26 10-K, Item 1A (We face risks from our international operations), signed May 11, 2026`; `Q2 FY27 10-Q, MD&A (Economic Environment and Retailer Performance), signed November 9, 2026` |
| **One Big Beautiful Bill Act (OBBB)** | **In force (unchanged since enactment).** Confirmed still in force and still being assessed in the most recent filing. | Signed into law July 4, 2025. Permanent extension of certain expiring Tax Cuts and Jobs Act provisions; changes to BEAT, GILTI and FDDEI; permanent reinstatement of the deduction for domestic research expenditures and 100% bonus depreciation for certain qualified property; modifications to tax credits. Multiple effective dates — some provisions effective in FY ended March 31, 2026, others in future periods. | Same terms; the Q2 FY27 10-Q repeats the description verbatim and says the company "estimated the accounting for income tax effects of OBBB in our projected annual effective tax rate" and is "continuing to evaluate the impact." | TTWO states OBBB "reduced our estimated U.S. cash tax liability" but gives no figure. It did **not** affect U.S. deferred tax assets or liabilities, because a full valuation allowance is maintained against U.S. net deferred tax assets. | No | `FY26 10-K, Item 1A (Adverse effects of changes in tax rates), May 11, 2026`; `Q2 FY27 10-Q, Note (Income Taxes), November 9, 2026` |
| **Pillar Two global minimum tax (OECD / G20 Inclusive Framework)** | **In force (amended — terms changed), and amended in TTWO's favour.** | A 15% global minimum tax regime; could create additional tax liability in a jurisdiction where tax expense is below 15%. Countries enact it differently and on different timelines. The U.S. "has not yet enacted Pillar Two legislation." | Amended by OECD administrative guidance released **January 5, 2026**, which set out a "side-by-side" arrangement: new safe harbors exempting U.S.-parented groups from **two of the three** Pillar Two top-up taxes; the Transitional Country-by-Country Reporting Safe Harbor extended by one year, through the end of TTWO's fiscal year ending March 31, 2028; and the Simplified Effective Tax Rate Safe Harbor made permanent. | Top-up taxes a U.S.-parented group is exposed to: **two of three removed, one remaining.** Realised impact on TTWO: "not material to the tax provision for the three and six months ended September 30, 2026." | The extended Country-by-Country Reporting Safe Harbor runs through TTWO's fiscal year ending **March 31, 2028**. The Simplified Effective Tax Rate Safe Harbor is permanent. | `FY26 10-K, Item 1A (Adverse effects of changes in tax rates), May 11, 2026`; `Q2 FY27 10-Q, Note (Income Taxes), November 9, 2026` |
| **Corporate alternative minimum tax (CAMT), Inflation Reduction Act of 2022** | **In force (unchanged).** | 15% minimum tax on adjusted financial statement income (AFSI) of corporations whose average AFSI exceeds $1.0 billion over three consecutive years. Became effective for TTWO's fiscal year ended March 31, 2024. | Same terms. | TTWO "estimate[s] no tax liability relating to the CAMT for the current fiscal year" — so the quantified impact in the reference period is **nil**, with the exposure live in future periods because of book-versus-taxable-income differences. | No | `FY26 10-K, Item 1A (Adverse effects of changes in tax rates), May 11, 2026` |
| **American Rescue Plan Act of 2021 (ARPA) — expansion of the limit on compensation deductions** | **In force (unchanged), with a dated future effective date for TTWO.** | Expands the deduction limit on compensation for certain covered employees of public companies to include the next five highest-paid employees. | Same terms; the effective date for TTWO is **April 1, 2027** — i.e. it begins in fiscal 2028, not yet in any reported period. | Not quantified. **What this register changes about the read.** Two things, both against the grain of a simple "policy risk" story. First, the single most-discussed policy item — the tariff — has a **contested and unestablished** current status in this evidence pool, and the pool's own last dated fact is a court holding the tariffs illegal. It is recorded here at its last-known terms and must not be carried downstream as either a live 10% levy or as something that has ended. Second, Pillar Two moved **in TTWO's favour** in January 2026 (two of three top-up taxes removed for U.S.-parented groups, one safe harbor extended to March 2028, another made permanent), with the realised impact described as not material. The loot-box file is the one with real teeth, and most of it is still proposals rather than law — the items actually in force are Australia's Phase 2 Industry Codes, Brazil's regulations, and the E.U. network's interpretive principles. No programme in this table has been shown by a dated primary source to have terminated, so no termination language is used anywhere in this report.

---

## 2. Sensitivity, If Disclosed

TTWO publishes one quantified sensitivity. Everything else in Item 7A / Item 3 is qualitative.

| Variable | Disclosed sensitivity | On the FY26 revenue base of $6,656 | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| U.S. dollar against all currencies | A hypothetical **10.0% increase** in the USD **decreases revenues by 4.0%**; a hypothetical **10.0% decrease** in the USD **increases revenues by 4.0%** | ±$266 of revenue per 10% USD move | `FY26 10-K, Item 7A (Foreign Currency Exchange Rate Risk)`; restated identically in `Q2 FY27 10-Q, Item 3`, measured on the quarter in which 40.6% of revenue was non-U.S. |
| Interest rates — investment portfolio | No sensitivity figure given. Management's determination: "there was no material interest rate risk exposure to our Consolidated Financial Statements or liquidity as of March 31, 2026," on $443.8 of short-term investments and $1,545.5 of cash and equivalents ($524.2 and $1,149.4 respectively at September 30, 2026) | Not assessable from disclosure | `FY26 10-K, Item 7A (Interest Rate Risk)`; `Q2 FY27 10-Q, Item 3 (Interest Rate Risk)` |
| Interest rates — borrowing cost | No sensitivity figure given. No floating-rate exposure to sensitise: no borrowings under the 2022 Credit Agreement at either period end, and all $2,970 of principal by maturity is fixed | Not applicable | `FY26 10-K, Item 7A`; `CIQ Financials_Annual → Capital Structure Details, as of Mar-31-2026 — 0 floating / 2,970 fixed — vendor export` |

**Two important qualifiers on the FX sensitivity, both from the company's own words.**

1. **It is a revenue-line sensitivity, not an earnings one, and it is stated gross of the natural cost offset.** TTWO's own sentence immediately after the figure: "In our opinion, a substantial portion of this fluctuation would be offset by cost of revenue and operating expenses incurred in local currency." The company does not size "a substantial portion," so the profit effect of a given FX move cannot be computed from the pool. Treat the ±4.0% revenue figure as a **bound on the revenue line**, not a forecast of the earnings hit (CLAUDE.md §9).
2. **The hedge book does not cover this exposure.** The forward and option-collar contracts — $748.7 to sell foreign currency and $327.8 to buy at September 30, 2026, up from $349.2 and $140.3 at March 31, 2026, all under one year — are explicitly for "non-functional currency denominated cash balances and intercompany funding loans… accounts receivable and… accounts payable," are "not designated as hedging instruments," and carried an immaterial fair value at both dates. They manage balance-sheet translation, not the translation of foreign revenue. The company says so plainly: "Our hedging programs are designed to reduce, but do not entirely eliminate, the effect of currency exchange rate movements." `Q2 FY27 10-Q, Item 3`; `FY26 10-K, Item 7A (Balance Sheet Hedging Activities)`.

---

## 3. Classification

**Partly externally driven.**

The reasoning, stated against the alternatives:

- It is **not** company-controlled. 40.8% of revenue is non-USD with a published ±4.0%-per-10% sensitivity; 80.6% of revenue passes through five customers, four of whom each take more than 10% and each of whom can rewrite its own fee structure at will; 39.0%–41.4% of revenue rides on a console installed base two other companies build and price; and 78.1% of revenue sits in exactly the in-game monetisation that regulators in Australia, Brazil, the E.U., the U.K., Spain and Portugal are actively working on. Those are not background noise.
- It is **not** mostly externally driven either. The variables that normally make a stock a wrapper — commodity prices, freight, floating-rate debt, policy subsidies that fund the revenue line — are absent or immaterial here: no commodity input, 97.0% digital delivery, zero floating-rate debt, and the only quantified policy benefit is a $45.7 tax-credit effect on the tax line, not on revenue. Management also holds real levers: a hedge book it more than doubled in six months on the balance-sheet exposure, a direct-to-consumer channel the CEO says has "had a material effect positive on our margins in the Mobile business," a multi-platform release strategy, and pricing and content decisions. Most of all, the observed driver of results in both reported FY27 quarters was its own product — the Q2 net-bookings miss was attributed to NBA 2K content, not the economy, and the entire Q2 FY27 call was about the Grand Theft Auto VI launch (November 19, 2026) rather than any macro variable. No analyst on that call asked a macro question.
- So: **material, partly un-hedgeable exposure, sitting under outcomes whose first-order driver is the company's own release slate.** That is the middle category.

---

## 4. External Dependency Risk Score

# 52 / 100

**Direction warning: this score is INVERTED — HIGHER = WORSE (more dangerous external dependence).** Lower is better.

Bands: 0–20 company-controlled · 21–40 partly externally driven, hedgeable / actively managed · **41–60 material external exposure, mixed mitigation** · 61–80 mostly externally driven · 81–100 pure pass-through.

52 sits in the 41–60 band, which is consistent with the "Partly externally driven" classification (the "mostly externally driven" territory begins at 61). It is placed above the 21–40 band specifically because the mitigation is **mixed rather than good**:

| What pushes the score DOWN (toward company-controlled) | What pushes it UP |
|---|---|
| No commodity exposure; Item 7A names only rates and FX | Four gatekeeper customers each >10% of revenue, 80.6% top-five, with contractual discretion over fees and terms — **no hedge exists for this** |
| Zero floating-rate debt; revolver undrawn; "no material interest rate risk exposure" | 78.1% of revenue in the in-game monetisation that six jurisdictions are regulating, with a $20 million FTC settlement against a peer as the live enforcement precedent |
| FX sensitivity published and modest (±4.0% of revenue per 10% USD move), with a company-stated natural cost offset | FX hedging covers balance-sheet items only, not revenue translation; cost base includes Turkey, where a past devaluation "negatively affected our results" |
| 97.0% digital delivery removes freight, tariff-on-own-product and physical-logistics exposure | 39.0%–41.4% of revenue depends on a third-party console installed base and its transition timing |
| Pillar Two amended in TTWO's favour (Jan 5, 2026); CAMT liability nil this year | Tariff status unestablished; a $45.7 tax-credit benefit that legislation "could scale back or eliminate," already $8.8 smaller than FY25 |
| Both FY27 quarters' results driven by own-title performance, not macro; CEO: "we don't feel like the consumer is pulling back at all" (Aug 7, 2026) | The filing's own words: reduced consumer spending "**has** and may in the future continue to result in reduced demand," on a wholly discretionary product |

No rejector-filter cap from MODULE_RULES applies to this agent's score (this module's three §24 filters are owned by `disqualifier-scan`, `capital-allocation-governance` and `business-quality`).

---

## 5. The Single Biggest Lever

A 20% adverse move in **discretionary consumer spending on games** would do the most damage, because 78.1% of FY26 net revenue is optional in-game purchases a player can simply stop making, and the 10-K says reduced consumer spending "has" already cut demand and forced higher promotional spend — whereas the only lever the company actually sizes, FX, caps the revenue hit of a 20% adverse USD move at 8.0% (roughly $533 on FY26 net revenue of $6,656, stated before the local-currency cost offset management says would absorb "a substantial portion" of it). `FY26 10-K, MD&A (recurrent consumer spending 78.1%)`; `FY26 10-K, Item 1A (Declines in consumer spending)`; `FY26 10-K, Item 7A (FX sensitivity)`.

_Source: `11_capital-allocation-governance.md`_

# Capital Allocation & Governance — TTWO

**Jurisdiction / regime:** United States, SEC domestic filer (Form 10-K / 10-Q / 8-K / DEF 14A), US GAAP, reporting currency USD, fiscal year ends March 31 — so "FY2026" means the year ended March 31, 2026 [FY26 10-K, cover page; FY26 10-K, Report of Independent Registered Public Accounting Firm]. All dollar figures below are USD millions unless stated.

**Data gap stated up front (per triage):** the DEF 14A export in this pool (`Take-Two_Interactive_Software_Inc_-_Form_DEF_14A(Jul-27-2026).xls`) is an empty shell containing one cell — "This filing has no tabular data to export." The proxy's content is therefore **absent**, and every proxy-dependent item is recorded as **"Not proven from available data"** rather than clean: executive compensation detail, say-on-pay and director election vote results, the audit committee's related-party-transaction policy, independent auditor fee information (which the 10-K incorporates by reference to the proxy — [FY26 10-K, Item 14]), stock-ownership guidelines, and whether insider sales ran under pre-set 10b5-1 plans. Separately, `Alphabet Inc NasdaqGS GOOGL Key Developments.xls` is a misfiled export for a different issuer (Alphabet Inc) and is **not** cited anywhere below; as a result TTWO's own material-events timeline is not in the pool, and the material-event reads here come from the 10-K, the two 10-Qs, and the two earnings releases instead.
