Days Payable Out. 24.5 (FY24) → 29.8 (FY25) → 26.3 (FY26) [CIQ Financials_Annual → Ratios — vendor export]; net cash provided by (used in) operating activities $(16.1)m (FY24), $(45.2)m (FY25), $624.3m (FY26) [FY26 10-K, MD&A cash-flow table and Consolidated Statements of Cash Flows]; accounts payable/accrued drain $(365.3)m (FY26), $(465.9)m (FY25); accounts receivable +$35.0m (FY26) [FY26 10-K, Consolidated Statements of Cash Flows]; LTM Sep-30-2026 cash from operations $367.1m on LTM revenue $6,410m, LTM capex $304.5m [`ciq_facts.json` `ltm_ocf_m`, `multi_year_trajectory`; CIQ Financials_Annual → Cash Flow, LTM column — vendor export]; deferred revenue $1,159.9m (Mar-31-2026) → $1,017.1m (Sep-30-2026) [Q2 FY27 10-Q, Condensed Consolidated Balance Sheets] | **55** |
| Senior management turnover (CEO, CFO, board chair in last 3 years) | There has been no turnover at all in the top three seats — Zelnick has been Chairman since February 2008 and CEO since January 2011, Slatoff President since May 2013, and Goldstein CFO since June 2007 — though the Chairman and CEO roles are combined in one person. | Zelnick "Executive Chairman of the Board of Take-Two Interactive Software, Inc. since February 2008 & CEO… from January 01, 2011"; Slatoff "President of Take-Two Interactive Software, Inc. from May 2013"; Goldstein "has been the Chief Financial Officer of Take-Two Interactive Software, Inc. since June 11, 2007"; Emerson Chief Legal Officer since May 2019 [CIQ Professionals export — vendor export]; board of 10 with 9 independent external directors and LaVerne Srinivasan as Lead Independent Director, Zelnick the only internal director [CIQ Board Members export — vendor export]; board tenure, committee composition and director election vote results — **Not proven from available data** (DEF 14A absent) | **10** |

**Acquisition pattern — serial-acquirer + opportunity-cost test (CLAUDE.md §24, Filter 4).**

The pattern here trips the filter, and it trips it on hard numbers rather than on a judgment about style.

*Frequency and size.* Two material deals inside four fiscal years, plus a tail of small ones. Zynga closed May 23, 2022; the consideration build is 46.3m shares worth $5,377.7m, a further 3.7m shares worth $479.7m issued for Zynga's assumed convertible notes [FY25 10-K, Consolidated Statements of Stockholders' Equity, FY2023 rows], and $3,310.9m of cash acquisitions in FY2023 [CIQ Financials_Annual → Cash Flow — vendor export; this cash line is the vendor's full-year total and may include other small deals] — summing to roughly **$9,168m**, with the components shown so the reader can rebuild it (§15). Gearbox closed June 2024 for 2.8m shares worth $448.6m [FY26 10-K, Consolidated Statements of Stockholders' Equity]. Smaller items continue: $3.1m of goodwill from "immaterial acquisitions" in FY2025 and $27.4m, $20.3m and $27.4m of asset acquisitions in FY2026, FY2025 and FY2024 [FY26 10-K, Note 9; FY26 10-K, Consolidated Statements of Cash Flows].

*Size relative to the company, and how it was funded.* The Zynga build of roughly $9,168m compares with the company's own total capitalization of **$4,059.9m** (total debt plus book equity) at March 31, 2022 — the deal was about 2.3x the entire pre-deal capital base on that basis [CIQ Financials_Annual → Historical Capitalization — vendor export]. The 50.0m shares issued were 43% of the 115.8m shares outstanding before the deal. And it was part debt-funded: $3,248.9m of long-term debt was issued in FY2023, taking total debt from $250.2m to $3,682.1m [CIQ Financials_Annual → Cash Flow and Historical Capitalization — vendor export]. A debt-funded deal at roughly half the company's own value is the exact condition the filter names.

*Outcome.* $6,665.1m of acquisition-related write-downs, built from $5,887.3m of accumulated goodwill impairment ($3,545.2m in FY2025 and $2,342.1m in FY2024) plus $777.8m of acquisition-related intangible impairments ($577.4m FY24, $137.0m + $39.3m FY25, $24.1m FY26) [FY26 10-K, Note 9]. That is 84.7% of the $6,949.2m of gross goodwill the company has ever recorded, leaving $1,061.9m net. The company's own risk-factor language ties it to the deal: "potential impairment charges incurred to write down the carrying amount of intangible assets generated as a result of an acquisition, such as the Goodwill impairment charge of approximately $3,500 we recognized during the fiscal year ended March 31, 2025" [FY26 10-K, Item 1A]. One qualifier must travel with this: the FY2026 10-K attributes the goodwill impairments to "one of our reporting units" without naming it; the FY2025 10-K separately attributes the intangible impairments to "our Zynga acquisition" [FY25 10-K, MD&A — Gross profit discussion]. So the Zynga attribution is explicit for the intangibles and strongly implied but **not named in the filing** for the goodwill.

*Opportunity cost, not just the deal's own loss.* The Private Division label — and "our rights to substantially all of the label's titles" — was sold in October 2024 [FY25 10-K, Item 1], producing $32.7m of proceeds, removing $15.8m of goodwill and booking an $11.4m loss on divestitures [FY26 10-K, Note 9; FY25 10-K, Consolidated Statements of Cash Flows and segment disclosures]. The company describes a wider retreat: divestitures and "shutting down studios, that no longer fit our long-term strategies," and "several rounds of layoffs in the recent" period [FY25 10-K, Item 1A]. Title cancellations and restructuring write-downs ran $3,805.9m (FY25) and $3,038m (FY24) on the vendor's "Asset Writedown & Restructuring Costs" line [CIQ Financials_Annual → Cash Flow — vendor export]. Businesses sold under deal pressure and attention pulled off the existing franchises are part of the charge, not a footnote to it.

*What keeps it off a higher score.* The deal-making has stopped. Business acquisitions were $(2.6)m in FY2026 [FY26 10-K, Consolidated Statements of Cash Flows] and nil in the six months to September 30, 2026 against $(2.0)m a year earlier [Q2 FY27 10-Q, Condensed Consolidated Statements of Cash Flows]. No new material transaction is disclosed in either FY2027 10-Q or either FY2027 earnings release. That is why this row sits at 75 and not higher. Bare "synergies / strategic fit" language is not treated as evidence of value creation anywhere above, and none was credited.

Severity **75** — the most material signal in this table, and the cap in §4 follows from it.

---

## 2. Classification

**Capital allocation concerns.**

Specific, cited issues with how capital has been spent, against a governance structure that is otherwise largely clean. On the capital side: $6,665.1m of acquisition-related write-downs, 84.7% of all goodwill ever recorded [FY26 10-K, Note 9]; share count up 61.7% since March 2022 with zero buybacks in three years [CIQ Historical Capitalization — vendor export; FY26 10-K, Item 5]; $688.9m a year going into capitalized game development while return on capital is −0.8% [FY26 10-K, Consolidated Statements of Cash Flows; `ciq_facts.json`]; and $1,150m of debt repaid out of equity proceeds rather than operating cash, with operating cash flow negative in FY2024 and FY2025 [Q2 FY27 10-Q, Debt note; FY26 10-K, MD&A cash-flow table].

On the governance side the evidence does not support the harsher "Governance red flags" label: Ernst & Young has audited the company without interruption for 20 years and gave unqualified opinions on both the FY2026 accounts and internal control [FY26 10-K, Report of Independent Registered Public Accounting Firm]; no restatement is disclosed; nine of ten directors are independent with a named Lead Independent Director [CIQ Board Members export — vendor export]; the filing states there are no material unconsolidated entities or variable interest entities [FY26 10-K, MD&A — Off-Balance Sheet Arrangements]; and the vendor's supply-chain graph shows 0 intra-group rows out of 28 disclosed relationship rows, a 0.0% intra-group share — no sign that disclosed trading runs through the company's own entities [CIQ Suppliers/Customers export (TTWO), retrieved 2026-11-19 — vendor export; scope is the vendor's own two-year recently-disclosed window for current subsidiaries, not the full counterparty base, and it proves relationships exist, never their size]. The three counterparties the graph flags `likely_group` are Epic Games, Inc., Sony Interactive Entertainment Inc. and Prima Interactive (Pty) Ltd — plainly outside platform and distribution partners, so that flag reads as a vendor name-and-industry heuristic artefact rather than a related-party finding; no filing in this pool places them inside the group.

Nor is this "Owner-operator discipline": the Executive Chairman and CEO holds 216,403 shares, 0.12% of the 187.2m outstanding, down 28% in nine months, the President holds none directly, and open-market insider activity over the trailing twelve months was 0 buys against 16 sells [CIQ Public Ownership → History and Insider Trading — vendor export; `ciq_facts.json`]. Insider economic ownership is small and one-directional.

Two caveats carried forward rather than resolved. First, the ZMC Advisors management agreement is a real related-party channel — $79.5m of combined FY2026 cost, 1.2% of revenue, with the consulting fee up 156% in two years against revenue up 24% [FY26 10-K, Note 3] — well below the 25%-of-revenue disqualifier bar, but the proxy that would carry the audit committee's related-party policy and the full compensation picture is absent from the pool, so that side is **Not proven from available data**, not clean. Second, the $215.0m receivables-purchase facility was unused in FY2026 [FY26 10-K, Note 2]; if drawn, its proceeds land in operating cash flow by the company's own stated accounting, which would flatter a cash-conversion line that is already the weak point.

---

## 3. Most Material Signal

The acquisition pattern. One further large deal — particularly a debt-funded one — would move this from "capital allocation concerns" to "governance red flags," because the company would be repeating the exact transaction type whose outcome its own notes already quantify at $6,665.1m of write-downs, and it would be doing so from a weaker footing than in 2022: borrowings of $2,519.8m against $1,149.4m of cash [Q2 FY27 10-Q, Condensed Consolidated Balance Sheets], operating cash flow of $367.1m on the last twelve months [`ciq_facts.json`], and $600m of notes due in 2027 and $800m in 2028 [`ciq_facts.json` `debt_maturity_wall`]. The currency last time was 50.0m newly issued shares, 43% of the pre-deal count; the share base has already grown 61.7% since then, so a repeat would compound the dilution signal rather than sit beside it. The second route to the same downgrade is quieter: the $2,494.9m capitalized software development asset at September 30, 2026 [Q2 FY27 10-Q, Condensed Consolidated Balance Sheets], which the company has impaired in each of the last three fiscal years and re-tests for recoverability every quarter [FY26 10-K, Note 1 and Note 9]. A material impairment there would show the same pattern — capital committed ahead of proven returns — with the ROIC already negative at −1.0% on the latest twelve months. Conversely, the single piece of evidence that would most improve this read is a few years of operating cash flow comfortably funding both the $688.9m-a-year development spend and debt reduction without new equity.

---

## 4. Capital Allocation Score /100

**50 / 100** (higher = better).

Build: severities across the twelve applicable rows (promoter pledging excluded as not applicable) average 39.6, which inverts to roughly 60. Weighting the three signals that matter most for a games publisher carrying a $2,494.9m capitalized development asset and a $6,665.1m write-down history — the acquisition pattern (75), dilution (65), and cash conversion (55) — above the low-severity governance-hygiene rows pulls the un-capped number down to roughly 52–55.

**Rejector-filter cap applied (CLAUDE.md §24, Filter 4; MODULE_RULES rejector table).** The acquisition-pattern row scored **75**, at or above the 70 threshold: two material deals in four years, the larger one debt-funded ($3,248.9m of debt issued in FY2023) at roughly 2.3x the company's own pre-deal total capitalization of $4,059.9m, followed by write-downs of $6,665.1m representing 84.7% of all goodwill ever recorded, with a label divested and studios closed under the resulting pressure. The Capital Allocation Score is therefore **capped at 50/100** regardless of the clean auditor record (Ernst & Young, 20 years, unqualified on both the accounts and internal control), the absence of restatements, the absence of off-balance-sheet vehicles, the 0.0% intra-group share of disclosed trading relationships, and zero turnover in the CEO, CFO and Chair seats. The cap binds: the un-capped weighted number (52–55) sat just above it, so the cap moves the score by only a few points, but it is applied and named as required.



---

## business-model / 12_red-flags-sweep.md

_Source: `12_red-flags-sweep.md`_

# Red Flags Sweep — TTWO

**Regime:** US / SEC domestic filer (NasdaqGS:TTWO, Delaware), US GAAP, reporting currency USD, fiscal year ends **March 31** — so "FY2026" means the year ended March 31, 2026 [FY26 10-K, cover page]. All dollar figures are USD millions as the filings state them. Grand Theft Auto VI **released today, November 19, 2026** [FY26 10-K, Item 1 — Rockstar Games, p.2; Q2 FY27 10-Q, MD&A — Hardware Platforms], so every dated item below is written relative to that fact rather than as a pending event.

**Upstream read:** all six required upstream outputs were on disk and were read in full, plus `00_data-triage.md`, `02_business-identity.md`, `04_unit-economics.md`, `06_value-chain.md` and `08_competitive-map.md`. One note for transparency: `09_moat.md` does **not** exist in the output folder, so if a flag below overlaps the moat agent's work I could not check it. Nothing in Section 2 depends on moat reasoning.

**Two pool defects, already recorded by triage and carried here unchanged:** the DEF 14A export (July 27, 2026) is an empty shell holding one cell — "This filing has no tabular data to export" — and `Alphabet-Inc-NasdaqGS-GOOGL-Key-Developments__Key-Developments.txt` is a misfiled export for a different issuer. Neither is cited as TTWO evidence anywhere below.

---

## 1. Already Covered Upstream

| Upstream Agent | Flag Already Surfaced |
|---|---|
| `01_disqualifier-scan` | No disqualifier triggered; **near-miss**: operating cash flow negative in 2 of the last 4 years (FY2024 −$16.1, FY2025 −$45.2); pledge and related-party dollar detail unreadable because the DEF 14A export is empty; vendor credit panel scores relative credit health "Bottom" |
| `03_segment-map` | Single reportable segment with profit disclosed only at the consolidated level; **two goodwill reporting units never named**, one of which absorbed the impairments; no profit split for mobile vs console vs PC, RCS vs full game, or Rockstar vs 2K vs Zynga; hit concentration (five franchises = 54.3% of FY26 revenue) |
| `05_customer-geography` | Top five customers = **80.6%** of FY26 net revenue; Apple, Sony, Google, Microsoft each >10%; sales made "primarily without long-term agreements"; US = 59.2% of revenue; **contract-expiry cluster — console licences covering 39.0% of revenue stated as expiring March 31, 2027**, Sony terminable "for any or no reason upon 30 days' notice" |
| `07_business-quality` | Aggregate quality 38/100; five straight years of negative return on capital (−4.3% to −1.0%); three straight operating losses; **$5,887.3 accumulated goodwill impairment on $6,949.2 of gross goodwill (~85%)**; **zero engagement metrics (MAU/DAU, retention, ARPDAU) anywhere in the pool**; Net Bookings and recurrent consumer spending are company-defined metrics that vest management's performance RSUs; §24 Filter 5 trip (fast-changing industry) |
| `10_external-dependency` | Dependency score 52/100 (inverted); platform gatekeepers rated **High** and un-hedgeable; loot-box / in-game-currency rulemaking across six jurisdictions against 78.1% of revenue; FX ±4.0% of revenue per 10% USD move with hedges covering balance-sheet items only; tariff status unestablished; $45.7 software-development tax-credit benefit that legislation "could scale back or eliminate" |
| `11_capital-allocation-governance` | Capital allocation 50/100 (capped by §24 Filter 4); **$6,665.1 of acquisition-related write-downs**; share count +61.7% since March 2022 with zero buybacks in three years; $1,150 of debt repaid out of equity proceeds; ZMC Advisors related-party management agreement ($79.5 combined FY26 cost); 0 buys / 16 sells of insider open-market activity; $215.0 receivables-purchase facility unused in FY26 whose proceeds, if drawn, land in operating cash flow; shortened title-amortisation and player-life windows |

**One correction between the siblings and the newest document in the pool, surfaced here because three upstream reports lean on the superseded fact.** `05_customer-geography.md` (§3, added flag), `06_value-chain.md` (§5) and `07_business-quality.md` (§4) all carry the FY26 10-K's statement that **both** Xbox agreements and the Sony agreement expire **March 31, 2027**, covering the 39.0% of FY26 revenue earned on console. That was correct when the FY26 10-K was signed May 11, 2026 [FY26 10-K, Item 1 — Arrangements with Platform Manufacturers, pp.3–4]. It is no longer the current state on the Microsoft limb: the Q2 FY27 10-Q filed **November 9, 2026** attaches, as Exhibit 10.1, a **new XBOX Publisher License Agreement effective September 17, 2026** between Take-Two and Microsoft Corporation, whose merger clause states the agreement "merges, replaces, and supersedes all related oral understandings, representations, prior discussions, letters of intent, or any preliminary or earlier agreements, **including the XBOX Console Publisher License Agreement between the parties** [***]" [Q2 FY27 10-Q, Exhibit Index, item 10.1; Q2 FY27 10-Q, Exhibit 10.1 — XBOX Publisher License Agreement, §18.10 (Miscellaneous)]. The synthesizer should therefore not restate "both console licences expire March 31, 2027" as a current fact: the Sony limb and its 30-day at-will termination right stand [FY26 10-K, Item 1, p.3], while the current-generation Xbox licence has already been replaced. What the replacement contains is the subject of Section 2, row 2.

---

## 2. New Red Flags

Severity is **INVERTED — higher = worse**. Bands per CLAUDE.md §12: 0–20 very low · 21–40 low · 41–60 mixed/moderate · 61–80 high · 81–100 very high.

| Red Flag | Why It Matters | Evidence | Severity /100 *(higher = worse)* |
|---|---|---|---:|
| **The tax line is both a stated vote of no confidence in future profits and, from this month, structurally re-cut with no quantified effect.** A deferred tax asset is a stored future tax deduction; a "valuation allowance" is management writing it down because it does not expect to earn enough taxable profit to use it. TTWO has written down **$1,262.3 of its $1,454.8 gross deferred tax assets — 86.8%** — and the stated reason is its own trading record: "Due to our **cumulative loss position, which provides significant negative evidence**, we recognized a tax expense of **$135.3** from an increase in our valuation allowance." Then, in the same month GTA VI shipped, the company moved intangible assets between jurisdictions and told readers it has not yet worked out what that does to its tax rate. Two consequences for the synthesizer: (i) FY27–FY28 earnings per share could carry a very large **non-cash** tax credit if the allowance is released on the back of the launch — that is an accounting release, not operating performance, and must not be read as one; (ii) the effective tax rate is currently unforecastable from the pool, having already printed **(50.8)%** against a 21% statutory rate in FY26 — a tax *charge* of $100.4 on a pre-tax *loss*. | Valuation allowance $(1,262.3) at Mar-31-2026 vs $(1,127.0) at Mar-31-2025 against total deferred tax assets $1,454.8; roll-forward $1,127.0 + $229.4 − $94.1 = $1,262.3 [FY26 10-K, Note 17 — Income Taxes, deferred tax table; FY26 10-K, Schedule II — Valuation and Qualifying Accounts]. "Cumulative loss position… significant negative evidence… tax expense of $135.3" [FY26 10-K, Note 17 — Income Taxes]. Rate-reconciliation build of the same effect, a different cut of it: "expense of $113.4 from an increase in the U.S. valuation allowance expense, $18.2 from an increase in the foreign valuation allowance expense, and $79.0 from our geographic mix and foreign earnings, partially offset by a $45.7 benefit from tax credits and $39.7 of excess benefits from employee stock compensation"; effective rate **(50.8)%**; provision $100.4 vs a $12.4 benefit in FY25 [FY26 10-K, MD&A — Provision for income taxes, p.47]. Loss carryforwards: domestic **$1,077.8**, foreign **$356.4**, of which **$34.9 expires 2027–2029** and **$272.8 expires 2027–2033** — $307.7 starts running out inside the window this launch covers [FY26 10-K, Note 17 — Income Taxes]. Subsequent event: "In November 2026, we completed a reorganization involving certain intangible assets to simplify our global operating structure. As a result, our jurisdictional mix of earnings will be affected beginning in November 2026. We are **still evaluating the impact** of this on our estimated annual effective tax rate, deferred tax assets, and valuation allowance for future periods" [Q2 FY27 10-Q, Note 16 — Subsequent Events, p.20] | **55** |
| **The replacement Microsoft console licence hides the terms the old one disclosed, and hands Microsoft a per-game kill switch.** The new agreement removes part of the March 2027 expiry cliff — that limb is genuinely better. What replaces it is less visible and, in one respect, worse. Every commercial term is redacted: the length of the **Initial Term** `[***]`, the length of each **Renewal Term** `[***]`, the **notice window** for non-renewal `[***]`, and the **"Royalty Percentage"** `[***]` — on the console channel that was 39.0% of FY26 net revenue and 41.4% of H1 FY27 net revenue. The exhibit carries **278** redactions. And Microsoft holds a right the FY26 10-K's description of the old agreements did not mention: if it "determines at any time before Commercial Release that the applicable Game does not materially comply with the Publisher Guide… or any Law," it "may, notwithstanding any prior approvals, terminate this Agreement **without cost or penalty** on a **Game-by-Game or country-by-country basis** on notice." For a publisher whose next release is a single title carrying the year, a counterparty-determined, per-title, per-country cancellation right is a sharper instrument than an expiry date. Redacting confidential commercial terms is normal and permitted; the consequence is still that an outsider now cannot read the fee or the term on two-fifths of revenue. | "This XBOX Publisher License Agreement is entered into and effective as of **September 17, 2026** ('Effective Date'), between…" [Q2 FY27 10-Q, Exhibit 10.1, preamble]. "17.1. Term. This Agreement will commence on the Effective Date [***] ('Initial Term'). This Agreement will automatically renew for [***]… unless one party gives the other notice of non-renewal within [***] of the end of the then-current Term" [same, §17.1]. "1.3 'Royalty Percentage' [***]" [same, Exhibit — Digital Content Royalty Terms, §1.3]. Termination right quoted above [same, §17.2]. Merger clause superseding the prior Xbox Console Publisher License Agreement [same, §18.10]. Exhibit index: "10.1 XBOX Publisher License Agreement, dated as of September 17, 2026, by and between Take-Two Interactive Software, Inc. and Microsoft Corporation" [Q2 FY27 10-Q, Exhibit Index]. Superseded position: "The terms of both Xbox Agreements expire on March 31, 2027" [FY26 10-K, Item 1 — Arrangements with Platform Manufacturers, p.4]. Console share 39.0% FY26 / 41.4% H1 FY27 [FY26 10-K, MD&A, p.44; Q2 FY27 10-Q, MD&A, p.23]. Redaction count 278 occurrences of `[***]` in the filing *(my count of the filed text)* | **48** |
| **Stock compensation is a $403.3 annual cost — 6.1% of revenue — and a quarter of it never reaches the income statement.** Share-based pay (shares given to staff instead of cash) cost **$305.3** through the profit and loss account in FY26, plus a further **$98.0 capitalized** into the balance-sheet game-development asset, so the gross charge was **$403.3 = 6.1% of $6,656.4 of revenue** — against a reported operating **loss** of $(104.2). The capitalized slice is the part to watch: it sits inside the $2,346.3 software-development asset, of which $2,149.7 relates to titles nobody has bought yet, and that asset has been impaired in each of the last three years. Separately, a **negative** stock-comp charge of $(27.9) ran through cost of revenue in FY26 against $+9.4 in FY25 — a $37.3 swing, worth **56 basis points** on revenue, i.e. roughly **19% of the +290bp FY26 gross-margin improvement** (*my arithmetic: 37.3 ÷ 6,656.4 = 0.56%; 56 ÷ 290 = 19%*). The cause is staff leaving: forfeitures reversed **$49.1** of expense and **$20.2** of capitalized cost in FY26, against $8.3 and $8.3 in FY25. It is disclosed in the footnote directly beneath the margin table, so this is a quality-of-earnings point rather than a hidden one — but it is not a repeatable source of margin, and no upstream report sized it. | Stock-based compensation expense before income taxes $305.3 (FY26), $324.0 (FY25), $335.6 (FY24); capitalized stock-based compensation $98.0 / $81.4 / $85.4; cost-of-revenue component $(27.9) / $9.4 / $24.4; forfeiture reversals $49.1 of expense and $20.2 capitalized in FY26 vs $8.3 / $8.3 in FY25 [FY26 10-K, Note 16 — Stock-Based Compensation, Stock-Based Compensation Expense table]. Same $(27.9) and $9.4 disclosed as footnote (1) to the cost-of-revenue table [FY26 10-K, MD&A — Cost of revenue, p.44]. Gross margin 57.2% FY26 vs 54.3% FY25; operating loss $(104.2); revenue $6,656.4 [FY26 10-K, MD&A, p.43]. Capitalized software development costs and licences $2,346.3, of which $2,149.7 unreleased; impairments $77.5 (FY25) and $109.9 (FY24) [FY26 10-K, Note 7, p.83] | **45** |
| **The pre-release assets of the flagship franchise have already been stolen once.** In September 2022 "an unauthorized third party illegally accessed and downloaded confidential information from Rockstar Games' systems, **including early development footage for the next Grand Theft Auto**," and in the same month a third party took credentials for a vendor platform 2K Games uses for customer help desk and sent players a malicious link. The company calls the direct costs "immaterial," and that is fair on cash. The flag is not the cost — it is that the loss event is **proven, not hypothetical**, in a business whose entire unbuilt inventory is source code and game assets: $2,149.7 of capitalized cost sits in unreleased titles, the filing confirms source code is stored centrally, and third parties "are given access to our sensitive and proprietary information." The company also writes that such disruptions "**have** negatively affected" its operations — past tense. No upstream report raised this. | "For example, in September 2022, we experienced a network intrusion in which an unauthorized third party illegally accessed and downloaded confidential information from Rockstar Games' systems, including early development footage for the next Grand Theft Auto. Subsequently, also in September 2022, an unauthorized third party illegally accessed credentials for a vendor platform that 2K Games uses to provide help desk support… We have incurred certain immaterial, incremental and one-time costs associated with these cybersecurity incidents"; "We securely store the source code for our interactive entertainment software products as it is created"; "Information technology system disruptions, network failures, or security breaches… **have** negatively affected, and in the future could negatively affect our business continuity, operations, financial results" [FY26 10-K, Item 1A — Reliance on complex information technology systems / cybersecurity, pp.26–27]. Governance side, stated for balance: a dedicated cybersecurity team under the CTO, NIST and ISO frameworks, penetration testing by independent assessors, and at least annual Audit Committee / Technology Risk Committee reporting [FY26 10-K, Item 1C — Cybersecurity, pp.35–36]. Unreleased capitalized cost $2,149.7 [FY26 10-K, Note 7, p.83] | **40** |
| **Money owed to the company is as concentrated as its revenue, uncollateralized, and insured only on the 3% of sales that ship in boxes.** Five customers were **69.6%** of gross accounts receivable at March 31, 2026 (72.1% a year earlier), three of them individually 22.7%, 21.0% and 14.0%. The company states "**We do not require collateral from our customers**," and the credit insurance it does buy is narrow — "trade credit insurance on a majority of our customers who **sell our physical products**" — while physical retail and other channels were only **3.0%** of FY26 net revenue. So essentially the digital 97.0% of the receivable book is unsecured and uninsured. This matters specifically now: the launch quarter that began today will push the receivable balance up sharply against the same three or four counterparties. Receivable concentration is a different exposure from the revenue concentration upstream already owns — it is about getting paid, not about access to the player. A matching point on the cash side: "We maintain cash balances at several major financial institutions. While we attempt to limit credit exposure with any single institution, **balances often exceed insurable amounts**," on $1,149.4 of cash at September 30, 2026. | "As of March 31, 2026 and 2025, five customers accounted for 69.6% and 72.1% of our gross accounts receivable… three customers who accounted for 22.7%, 21.0%, and 14.0% of our gross accounts receivable as of March 31, 2026"; "We do not require collateral from our customers"; "maintaining trade credit insurance on a majority of our customers who sell our physical products"; "balances often exceed insurable amounts" [FY26 10-K, Note 1 — Concentration of Credit Risk and Accounts Receivable, p.68]. Physical retail and other 3.0% of FY26 net revenue [FY26 10-K, MD&A, p.44]. Cash and cash equivalents $1,149.4 at Sep-30-2026 [Q2 FY27 10-Q, Condensed Consolidated Balance Sheets] | **38** |
| **$91.8 of capital went into a London freehold while return on capital was negative, and 90% of it landed in an asset that will never show up as a cost.** On August 10, 2026 the company bought the building it had been renting in London for a total capitalized cost of **$91.8** — equal to **56%** of a whole year's fixed-asset capex ($162.8 in FY26) in one transaction. Of that, **$82.9 (90.3%) was allocated to land**, which is not depreciated (no useful life — the filing states "N/A"), and only **$8.9** to the building over 20 years, i.e. about **$0.4 a year** of depreciation *(my arithmetic: 8.9 ÷ 20)*. The accounting effect is small and I will not inflate it — at most a couple of million dollars a year versus a more building-weighted split. Two real points remain: the purchase replaces a rent charge with an asset whose cost will essentially never run through the profit and loss account, so there is no earnings test of whether it was a good buy; and it is capital committed to property by a company whose return on capital has been negative five years running. The filing gives the "relative fair value basis" as the method but no supporting basis for the 90/10 split, and group operating lease costs were $78.0 in FY26 with the London share not disclosed, so the saving cannot be sized. | "On August 10, 2026, we purchased the building where we previously leased office space in London, England, for a total capitalized cost of $91.8, which includes cash consideration, direct transaction costs, net of the derecognition of the preexisting lease liability and right-of-use asset"; allocation table: Land $82.9, weighted average useful life "N/A"; Building $8.9, 20 years; Total $91.8; "treated as an asset acquisition, in which the total cost was allocated on a relative fair value basis" [Q2 FY27 10-Q, Note 15 — Asset Acquisition, p.19]. Purchases of fixed assets $(162.8) FY26 [FY26 10-K, Consolidated Statements of Cash Flows]. Operating lease costs $78.0 (FY26), $84.9 (FY25), $80.5 (FY24) [FY26 10-K, Note 10 — Leases]. ROIC −4.3% / −3.1% / −4.2% / −0.8% / −1.0% LTM [`ciq_facts.json` → `multi_year_trajectory` (CIQ Financials → Ratios) — tier-5 vendor export] | **35** |
| **The company flags unionisation of its own workforce as a risk that could change how it operates, on a cost base that is 77% developers.** 9,998 of 12,909 full-time employees work in product development, spread across 14 countries, and that payroll is the main fixed cost between launches. The filing's own words: "we are aware of an increase in the industry of workers exercising their right to form or join a union. If significant employee populations were to unionize, we could experience operational changes that **may materially impact our business**." Set against a company that has already run "several rounds of layoffs," closed studios, and booked $106.5 of business-reorganization expense in FY25 (a $(4.4) credit in FY26), labour organisation would reduce exactly the lever it has used to protect margin between releases. Nothing in the pool quantifies any current organising activity at TTWO, which is why this is scored low rather than moderate. | "Currently, none of our employees are represented by a union… but we are aware of an increase in the industry of workers exercising their right to form or join a union. If significant employee populations were to unionize, we could experience operational changes that may materially impact our business" [FY26 10-K, Item 1A — We depend on our key management and product development personnel, p.29]. Headcount 12,909 full-time employees at March 31, 2026, of which 9,998 in product development; 48.3% North America, 29.8% Europe, 17.7% Asia-Pacific, 4.2% Middle East [FY26 10-K, Item 1 — Human Capital Management, pp.1 and 8]. Business reorganization $(4.4) FY26 vs $106.5 FY25 [FY26 10-K, MD&A — Operating expenses, p.45]; "shutting down studios, that no longer fit our long-term strategies" and "several rounds of layoffs" [FY25 10-K, Item 1A] | **30** |

Two candidate flags were tested and are **not** raised, so the synthesizer does not have to wonder:

- **Pension / post-employment obligations: none.** The company runs defined-contribution plans only — cost $55.1 in FY26, rising steadily from $8.0 in FY2017 — with no defined-benefit net periodic cost in any of the ten annual columns [CIQ Financials_Annual → Pension/OPEB, FY2017–FY2026 — tier-5 vendor export].
- **Litigation overhang: not evidenced in this pool.** Item 3 points to the contingencies note, which describes "routine litigation in the ordinary course of business which we do not believe to be material," with losses in excess of accruals "not material"; the latest interim filing repeats it unchanged [FY26 10-K, Item 3, p.37 and Note 14, p.92; Q2 FY27 10-Q, Note 12]. No named case, class action, labour-board proceeding or enforcement matter appears anywhere in the pool. Recorded as "not proven from available data" rather than as a clean bill of health, because the DEF 14A export is empty and the pool holds no TTWO material-events timeline (the Key Developments export is the misfiled Alphabet file).

---

## 3. Most Severe New Flag

**The tax line — an $1,262.3 valuation allowance built on the company's own "cumulative loss position," now re-cut by a November 2026 intangible-asset reorganisation whose effect management says it is still evaluating.** The synthesizer should weight this above the other new flags for three reasons. First, it is the largest number in this report and it is management's own judgment, not an outside estimate: by writing down 86.8% of its stored tax deductions, the company has formally stated that it does not expect to earn enough taxable profit to use them [FY26 10-K, Note 17 — Income Taxes]. That sits directly alongside — and independently corroborates — the five straight years of negative return on capital that `07_business-quality.md` already scored. Second, it is the single most likely route to a misread of FY27–FY28 earnings: a release of that allowance after a successful Grand Theft Auto VI cycle would drop a very large **non-cash** credit into net income and earnings per share, and anyone reading the resulting figure as operating performance would be reading an accounting entry. The same risk runs the other way — FY26 already printed a **tax charge of $100.4 on a pre-tax loss**, a (50.8)% effective rate [FY26 10-K, MD&A, p.47]. Third, the newest fact in the entire pool makes the rate unforecastable rather than merely volatile: the company moved intangible assets between jurisdictions in **November 2026**, the same month the title launched, says the "jurisdictional mix of earnings will be affected beginning in November 2026," and states it is "still evaluating the impact of this on our estimated annual effective tax rate, deferred tax assets, and valuation allowance for future periods" [Q2 FY27 10-Q, Note 16 — Subsequent Events, p.20]. When the issuer itself has not sized the effect, no downstream model should present a FY27 tax rate as a known input, and $307.7 of loss carryforwards begin expiring inside the 2027–2033 window regardless [FY26 10-K, Note 17].

---

## 4. Cross-Cutting Patterns

**Pattern one — every discretionary reporting choice visible in the last two years lands in the same direction, into the peak year, on a metric that pays management.** Taken alone each is defensible, disclosed, and permitted; taken together they form a direction worth testing. FY26 cost of revenue carried a **negative** $(27.9) stock-comp charge from $49.1 of forfeitures, worth about 19% of the +290bp gross-margin improvement [FY26 10-K, Note 16 and MD&A, p.44]; $98.0 of FY26 stock comp was capitalized into a development asset that has been impaired three years running [FY26 10-K, Note 16; Note 7, p.83]; 90.3% of a $91.8 London property purchase was allocated to non-depreciating land [Q2 FY27 10-Q, Note 15, p.19]; the title-amortisation and player-life windows were shortened (`11_capital-allocation-governance.md`); and a $215.0 receivables-purchase facility stands ready whose proceeds, by the company's own stated accounting, "will be reflected as cash provided by operating activities" if drawn [FY26 10-K, Note 2]. The context that makes the direction worth checking rather than shrugging at: management's performance RSUs vest on **recurrent consumer spending net bookings**, a company-defined metric [FY26 10-K, Exhibit — form of performance-based RSU award agreement], FY27 guidance is given in that same non-GAAP currency, and the biggest launch in the company's history landed today. This is a verification task for the synthesizer and the accounting-forensics layer, not a finding of wrongdoing: nothing here is undisclosed, no restatement exists, Ernst & Young gave unqualified opinions on both the FY2026 accounts and internal control, and no single item above is large enough on its own to move the verdict.

**Pattern two — the gatekeeper relationships are becoming less legible precisely as console weight rises.** Console was 39.0% of FY26 revenue and 41.4% of H1 FY27 [FY26 10-K, MD&A, p.44; Q2 FY27 10-Q, MD&A, p.23], and the direction of travel on disclosure is backwards on all three limbs at once: the Microsoft licence's term and royalty percentage are now redacted where the old agreement's expiry date was stated in plain text [Q2 FY27 10-Q, Exhibit 10.1, §17.1 and Digital Content Royalty Terms §1.3; FY26 10-K, Item 1, p.4]; Sony retains a 30-day at-will termination right [FY26 10-K, Item 1, p.3]; and Apple and Google operate under terms of service they write and reinterpret at their own discretion [FY26 10-K, Item 1A, p.11]. Combined with the pool's complete absence of engagement metrics that `07_business-quality.md` already flagged, an outsider's ability to measure either side of this business — what players do, and what the gatekeepers charge — is narrowing rather than widening.
